1	# Notes
2	
3	See [design.md](../crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md)
4	
5	
6	## jj tips
7	
8	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
9	
10	```
11	## Chores format
12	
13	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
14	example: chores-01.md
15	
16	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
17	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
18	
19	Example chore file:
20	```
21	# Chores-01.md
22	 
23	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
24	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
25	how and why of a particular chore.
26	
27	## Do something (1.3.1)
28	
29	Describe something
30	```
31	
32	## Versioning during development
33	
34	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
35	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
36	we go.
37	
38	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
39	
40	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
41	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
42	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
43	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
44	
45	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
46	
47	For multi-step:
48	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
49	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
50	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
51	
52	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
53	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
54	commits were exploratory vs final.
55	
56	## Todo format
57	
58	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
59	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
60	references.
61	
62	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
63	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
64	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
65	
66	Examples:
67	
68	# Todo
69	
70	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
71	- Fix bug Y [1]
72	
73	# Done
74	
75	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
76	
77	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
78	
```

1	# Todo
2	
3	This file contains near term tasks with a short description
4	and reference links to more details.
5	
6	## In Progress
7	
8	## Todo
9	
10	See [Foramt details](README.md#todo-format)
11	
12	- Initial design [[1]]
13	- PoC implementation of a manager
14	- Explore `linkme` / `inventory` for a benchmark harness on top of
15	  `tprobe` — self-registering benchmarks (no `main()` edits per
16	  benchmark); possibly reusable for actor registration too [[9]]
17	- After 0.3.0: add a `criterion` benchmark mirroring the goal1 /
18	  goal2 workloads, so `tprobe`'s numbers can be cross-validated
19	  against an established statistical-sampling harness. Effort
20	  estimate, breakdown, and open design questions captured
21	  at [[19]]
22	- When a benchmark harness lands (criterion and/or the
23	  linkme/inventory idea at `[9]`), add a CPU-warmup helper to
24	  `tprobe` — probably `tprobe::warmup::cpu_boost(iterations)` or
25	  `cpu_boost(Duration)` — since `calibrate()` no longer warms
26	  the CPU itself. Harnesses that call `calibrate()` directly
27	  (without an app-level dispatch loop) need to prime the CPU to
28	  boost frequency first
29	- Add `--repeat N` flag to goal1/goal2 to run the full
30	  warmup+measurement sequence N times and report cross-run mean
31	  and stdev on `mean min-p99` / `adj mean min-p99`. Built-in
32	  replacement for the `for _ in 1..=30; do goal1 2 ...` shell
33	  loop; gives the empirical repeatability number A/B work
34	  depends on. See [[18]] for floors, technique, and
35	  expected-scale heuristics
36	- Revisit band-table `range` semantics — drop the `+1` so
37	  `first == last → range = 0` (true "no spread" instead of
38	  today's "1 tk / rounds to 0 ns"). `last - first` reads
39	  more naturally and matches what a reader expects "range"
40	  to mean. Pre-existing behavior, not introduced in 0.3.0-3
41	- **Bound `TProbe`'s records-buffer memory.** Each scope
42	  appends a 32-byte `Record` to an unbounded `Vec`; at
43	  `goal1 --inner 10` (~13M scopes/s), 20 s of measurement
44	  buffers 260M records ≈ 8 GB minimum, ~16 GB after
45	  `Vec` power-of-2 over-allocation, which swaps/OOMs on
46	  typical dev boxes. Pre-existing from 0.1.0-2 when the
47	  probe was vendored; becomes hard to miss at long
48	  measurement windows + small `inner`. Options (pick one
49	  once we instrument drain cost):
50	  - (a) **Hard cap + truncation counter.** Fixed max
51	    records (e.g. 64M ≈ 2 GB); past the cap, increment a
52	    dropped counter and skip the `push`. Report shows
53	    `truncated: N scopes dropped`. Preserves hot-path
54	    cost. Partial data on very long runs.
55	  - (b) **Periodic inline drain.** When records hit a
56	    threshold, drain into the histogram and continue.
57	    Memory bounded. Adds occasional drain stalls (ms-ish)
58	    that show up in the `p99-max` tail.
59	  - (c) **Inline drain, no buffer.** `end` / `end_batch`
60	    compute `per_event` and call `hist.record` directly.
61	    Fully bounded memory, no stalls, but adds ~15–20 ns
62	    to the hot path — non-trivial when the probe is
63	    measuring ~5 ns work per event.
64	  - (d) **Skinnier record (just `u64` per-event ticks).**
65	    4× memory reduction. Buys time but doesn't fix the
66	    unbounded-growth shape.
67	- Clean up `SingleThreadRuntime::run_for` inner loop:
68	  (a) remove the unnecessary `let Self { actors, queue, probe } = self;`
69	      destructure — disjoint `&mut` through named field paths
70	      (`self.actors[i]`, `self.queue`, `self.probe`) compiles
71	      without the split;
72	  (b) replace `while done < inner { …; done += 1; }` with
73	      `for _ in 0..inner { …; done += 1; }` (break on empty
74	      queue stays) — more idiomatic for a known upper bound,
75	      same generated code under `--release`;
76	  (c) update the struct doc comment that references
77	      "field-split-borrows actors and queue".
78

> AGENT

Acquainted. Here's the snapshot:

**Project** — `actor-x1`, Rust workspace at `v0.3.2` with two crates:
- `crates/actor-x1` — actor runtime (design in `crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md`)
- `crates/tprobe` — timing/perf probe

**Repo setup** — dual jj-git repos: app at `.`, bot session at `.claude`. Working copies: app is clean on `main` (d4e980b), `.claude` has the usual in-session session-file churn. `jj log` is shallow here (`~`), so the full history lives elsewhere if needed.

**Latest work** — 0.3.0 ladder closed with band-table raw/adj-mean columns, then 0.3.1 (band-table decimals) and 0.3.2 (comma-formatted counts). Most recent commit just captures the criterion-benchmark effort estimate as a note.

**Open `notes/todo.md` items** — initial actor design, manager PoC, a `linkme`/`inventory` benchmark-harness exploration, criterion cross-validation against `tprobe`, a `tprobe::warmup::cpu_boost` helper, `--repeat N` for goal1/goal2, a band-table `range` semantic tweak, a memory-bound for `TProbe`'s records buffer (four options a–d), and a `SingleThreadRuntime::run_for` cleanup.

Nothing in progress. Ready for direction — want me to pick one up, or is there something else in mind?

```
1	//! Goal1 binary: two actors on a single thread ping-pong an empty
2	//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration in seconds (f64),
3	//! after an optional warmup phase. Prints messages-handled /
4	//! throughput and a `tprobe` band-table report of per-dispatch
5	//! latency.
6	//!
7	//! Usage: `goal1 --help`
8	
9	use std::time::Duration;
10	
11	use actor_x1::runtime::SingleThreadRuntime;
12	use actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};
13	use clap::Parser;
14	use tprobe::{self as perf, fmt_commas, pin, ticks};
15	
16	/// Actor that, on every message received, sends exactly one message
17	/// back to its configured peer.
18	struct PingPongActor {
19	    peer_id: u32,
20	}
21	
22	impl Actor for PingPongActor {
23	    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.
24	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
25	        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);
26	    }
27	}
28	
29	/// CLI for goal1: single-thread two-actor ping-pong with warmup,
30	/// per-dispatch probe batching, and ns-or-ticks band-table report.
31	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
32	#[command(
33	    version,
34	    about,
35	    long_about = None,
36	    max_term_width = 80,
37	    before_help = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")),
38	)]
39	struct Cli {
40	    /// Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup).
41	    #[arg(value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
42	    duration_s: f64,
43	
44	    /// Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the
45	    /// same dispatch loop as the measurement phase (probe active)
46	    /// so cache/branch-predictor/frequency land in the same state
47	    /// by the time the probe is cleared and measurement begins.
48	    #[arg(short = 'w', long, default_value_t = 0.5, value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
49	    warmup: f64,
50	
51	    /// Number of dispatches per probe scope. Larger values
52	    /// amortize probe apparatus overhead and push stored tick
53	    /// values into a range where the histogram's 0.1 %-relative
54	    /// buckets resolve small variations.
55	    #[arg(short = 'i', long, default_value_t = 1, value_parser = clap::value_parser!(u64).range(1..))]
56	    inner: u64,
57	
58	    /// Display probe values as raw ticks instead of nanoseconds.
59	    #[arg(short = 't', long)]
60	    ticks: bool,
61	
62	    /// Fractional precision of numeric cells in the band table.
63	    /// Omit for a mode-aware default (0 for ticks, 1 for ns) —
64	    /// typically what you want. Pass an integer N to force N
65	    /// decimals in either mode.
66	    #[arg(short = 'd', long)]
67	    decimals: Option<usize>,
68	
69	    /// Pin the workload thread to a logical CPU. Accepts a single
70	    /// id or a comma-separated / range list; only the first core
71	    /// is used (goal1 is single-threaded). Tightens stdev by
72	    /// eliminating OS thread migration noise.
73	    #[arg(short = 'p', long)]
74	    pin: Option<String>,
75	}
76	
77	/// `value_parser` helper: reject negative, NaN, and infinite values.
78	fn parse_non_negative_secs(s: &str) -> Result<f64, String> {
79	    let v: f64 = s
80	        .parse()
81	        .map_err(|e: std::num::ParseFloatError| e.to_string())?;
82	    if !v.is_finite() || v < 0.0 {
83	        Err(format!("'{s}' is not a non-negative finite number"))
84	    } else {
85	        Ok(v)
86	    }
87	}
88	
89	/// Parse CLI, run warmup + measurement on two single-thread
90	/// ping-pong actors, print message count + throughput, then
91	/// render the per-dispatch band-table report.
92	fn main() {
93	    let cli = Cli::parse();
94	
95	    println!("{} {}", env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),);
96	
97	    // Pin the main thread before any work — calibration and the
98	    // dispatch loop both run here, so they share the pinned core.
99	    let pin_core: Option<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
100	        None => None,
101	        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
102	            Ok(cores) => cores.first().copied(),
103	            Err(e) => {
104	                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
105	                std::process::exit(2);
106	            }
107	        },
108	    };
109	    pin::pin_current(pin_core);
110	
111	    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("goal1.dispatch");
112	    let a_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));
113	    let b_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));
114	    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));
115	    rt.seed(a_id, Message);
116	
117	    // Warmup: same loop, probe active, records discarded at the boundary.
118	    rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup), cli.inner);
119	    rt.probe_mut().clear();
120	
121	    // Calibration runs on the warmed system, before measurement
122	    // begins, so freq/cache state matches what the probe will see
123	    // during measurement.
124	    let overhead = perf::calibrate();
125	
126	    // Measurement.
127	    let count = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s), cli.inner);
128	    let mmps = count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;
129	    println!(
130	        "goal1: {} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, inner={})",
131	        fmt_commas(count),
132	        cli.duration_s,
133	        fmt_commas(cli.inner),
134	    );
135	    match pin_core {
136	        Some(c) => println!("  pinning: main → core {c}"),
137	        None => println!("  pinning: none (unpinned)"),
138	    }
139	    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();
140	    let framing_ns = overhead.framing_ticks as f64 / tpn;
141	    let lpi_ns = overhead.loop_per_iter_ticks / tpn;
142	    let per_event_tk = overhead.per_event_ticks(cli.inner);
143	    let per_event_ns = per_event_tk as f64 / tpn;
144	    println!(
145	        "  apparatus: framing={} tk ({:.2} ns); loop_per_iter={:.2} tk ({:.2} ns); per-event at inner={} = {} tk ({:.2} ns)",
146	        overhead.framing_ticks,
147	        framing_ns,
148	        overhead.loop_per_iter_ticks,
149	        lpi_ns,
150	        fmt_commas(cli.inner),
151	        per_event_tk,
152	        per_event_ns,
153	    );
154	    println!();
155	    rt.probe_mut()
156	        .report(cli.ticks, Some(&overhead), cli.decimals);
157	}
158

1	//! Goal2 binary: two actors, each on its own thread, ping-ponging
2	//! an empty [`Message`] over `std::sync::mpsc` channels for a
3	//! caller-supplied duration in seconds, after an optional warmup.
4	//! Prints messages-handled / throughput, an apparatus-overhead
5	//! diagnostic, and a `tprobe` band-table report per actor.
6	//!
7	//! Unlike goal1, `inner` is fixed at 1: ping-pong keeps at most
8	//! one message in flight per channel, so batched probe scopes
9	//! could never fill. If we later add a workload with deeper
10	//! per-channel queues we'll revisit.
11	//!
12	//! Usage: `goal2 --help`
13	
14	use std::time::Duration;
15	
16	use actor_x1::runtime::MultiThreadRuntime;
17	use actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};
18	use clap::Parser;
19	use tprobe::{self as perf, fmt_commas, pin, ticks};
20	
21	/// Actor that, on every message received, sends exactly one
22	/// message back to its configured peer. Same as goal1's but must
23	/// also be `Send` to cross thread boundaries.
24	struct PingPongActor {
25	    peer_id: u32,
26	}
27	
28	impl Actor for PingPongActor {
29	    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.
30	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
31	        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);
32	    }
33	}
34	
35	/// CLI for goal2: two-thread two-actor ping-pong with warmup,
36	/// per-dispatch probe, apparatus-overhead subtraction, and
37	/// ns-or-ticks band-table report per actor.
38	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
39	#[command(
40	    version,
41	    about,
42	    long_about = None,
43	    max_term_width = 80,
44	    before_help = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")),
45	)]
46	struct Cli {
47	    /// Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup).
48	    #[arg(value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
49	    duration_s: f64,
50	
51	    /// Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the
52	    /// same dispatch loop as the measurement phase (probe active)
53	    /// so cache / branch-predictor / frequency land in the same
54	    /// state by the time the probe is cleared and measurement
55	    /// begins.
56	    #[arg(short = 'w', long, default_value_t = 0.5, value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
57	    warmup: f64,
58	
59	    /// Display probe values as raw ticks instead of nanoseconds.
60	    #[arg(short = 't', long)]
61	    ticks: bool,
62	
63	    /// Fractional precision of numeric cells in the band table.
64	    /// Omit for a mode-aware default (0 for ticks, 1 for ns) —
65	    /// typically what you want. Pass an integer N to force N
66	    /// decimals in either mode.
67	    #[arg(short = 'd', long)]
68	    decimals: Option<usize>,
69	
70	    /// Pin each actor thread to a logical CPU. Accepts a
71	    /// comma-separated / range list; actor `i` pins to
72	    /// `pin[i % pin.len()]`. Examples: `--pin 0,1` pairs two
73	    /// actors to two CPUs; `--pin 0` oversubscribes both onto
74	    /// one core. Tightens stdev by eliminating OS thread
75	    /// migration noise.
76	    #[arg(short = 'p', long)]
77	    pin: Option<String>,
78	}
79	
80	/// `value_parser` helper: reject negative, NaN, and infinite values.
81	fn parse_non_negative_secs(s: &str) -> Result<f64, String> {
82	    let v: f64 = s
83	        .parse()
84	        .map_err(|e: std::num::ParseFloatError| e.to_string())?;
85	    if !v.is_finite() || v < 0.0 {
86	        Err(format!("'{s}' is not a non-negative finite number"))
87	    } else {
88	        Ok(v)
89	    }
90	}
91	
92	/// Parse CLI, calibrate apparatus, run two ping-pong actors on
93	/// their own threads, print summary and per-actor `tprobe`
94	/// band-table reports.
95	fn main() {
96	    let cli = Cli::parse();
97	    println!("{} {}", env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),);
98	
99	    // Calibrate on main thread before spawning. Matches goal1's
100	    // calibration code path; actor threads share the same CPU
101	    // microarchitecture so a single Overhead is good enough.
102	    let overhead = perf::calibrate();
103	
104	    let mut rt = MultiThreadRuntime::new("goal2.dispatch");
105	    let a_id = rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 });
106	    let b_id = rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 });
107	    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));
108	    rt.seed(a_id, Message);
109	
110	    let warmup = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup);
111	    let measurement = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s);
112	    let pin_cores: Vec<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
113	        None => vec![],
114	        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
115	            Ok(cores) => cores,
116	            Err(e) => {
117	                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
118	                std::process::exit(2);
119	            }
120	        },
121	    };
122	    let results = rt.run(warmup, measurement, &pin_cores);
123	
124	    let total_count: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();
125	    let mmps = total_count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;
126	    println!(
127	        "goal2: {} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, {n} actors, inner=1)",
128	        fmt_commas(total_count),
129	        cli.duration_s,
130	        n = results.len(),
131	    );
132	    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();
133	    let framing_ns = overhead.framing_ticks as f64 / tpn;
134	    let lpi_ns = overhead.loop_per_iter_ticks / tpn;
135	    let per_event_tk = overhead.per_event_ticks(1);
136	    let per_event_ns = per_event_tk as f64 / tpn;
137	    println!(
138	        "  apparatus: framing={} tk ({:.2} ns); loop_per_iter={:.2} tk ({:.2} ns); per-event at inner=1 = {} tk ({:.2} ns)",
139	        overhead.framing_ticks,
140	        framing_ns,
141	        overhead.loop_per_iter_ticks,
142	        lpi_ns,
143	        per_event_tk,
144	        per_event_ns,
145	    );
146	    if pin_cores.is_empty() {
147	        println!("  pinning: none (unpinned)");
148	    } else {
149	        let n = results.len();
150	        let plan: Vec<String> = (0..n)
151	            .map(|i| format!("actor{i}→core{}", pin_cores[i % pin_cores.len()]))
152	            .collect();
153	        println!("  pinning: {}", plan.join(", "));
154	    }
155	    println!();
156	
157	    for (i, (count, mut probe)) in results.into_iter().enumerate() {
158	        println!("  actor {i}: handled {} messages", fmt_commas(count));
159	        probe.report(cli.ticks, Some(&overhead), cli.decimals);
160	    }
161	}
162

1	//! actor-x1 — an experiment in the actor model in Rust.
2	//!
3	//! See `crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md` for the staged design. Stage 1 provides
4	//! a minimal runtime with two actors that ping-pong an empty
5	//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration; Goal1 runs both
6	//! actors on one thread, Goal2 runs each actor on its own thread.
7	//!
8	//! Public surface today:
9	//! - [`Message`]: the empty unit message Stage 1 actors exchange.
10	//! - [`Actor`]: the single-method actor trait.
11	//! - [`Context`]: the send-side handle passed to each dispatch.
12	//! - [`runtime`]: concrete runtimes (single-thread for Goal1; the
13	//!   multi-thread runtime will land in 0.1.0-3).
14	
15	pub mod runtime;
16	
17	/// Empty unit-type message exchanged between Stage 1 actors.
18	/// Carries no payload by design — Stage 1's contract is only
19	/// "dispatch happens"; Stage 2 replaces this with a richer type.
20	pub struct Message;
21	
22	/// Actor behavior: handle one [`Message`] at a time, never blocking.
23	///
24	/// The only method is [`Actor::handle_message`]; all outbound
25	/// communication goes through the [`Context`] the runtime supplies.
26	pub trait Actor {
27	    /// Handle a single incoming message. Use `ctx` to emit any
28	    /// resulting sends. Must not block.
29	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, msg: Message);
30	}
31	
32	/// Runtime-supplied send handle passed to each
33	/// [`Actor::handle_message`] call. Implementations differ between
34	/// runtimes: the single-thread runtime writes to a shared queue;
35	/// the (future) multi-thread runtime writes to per-actor channels.
36	pub trait Context {
37	    /// Enqueue `msg` for delivery to the actor identified by
38	    /// `dst_id`. Returns immediately; delivery is the runtime's job.
39	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message);
40	}
41

1	//! Runtimes that drive [`Actor`]s. Two flavors live here:
2	//! [`SingleThreadRuntime`] (Goal1: one thread, FIFO queue) and
3	//! [`MultiThreadRuntime`] (Goal2: one thread per actor, `mpsc`
4	//! channels).
5	
6	use std::collections::VecDeque;
7	use std::sync::mpsc::{self, Receiver, Sender};
8	use std::thread::{self, JoinHandle};
9	use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
10	
11	use crate::{Actor, Context, Message};
12	use tprobe::TProbe;
13	
14	/// Single-threaded runtime: all actors run on the calling thread,
15	/// sharing one FIFO message queue. Dispatch loops until either the
16	/// caller-supplied deadline expires or the queue drains.
17	///
18	/// Dispatch is wrapped in a [`TProbe`] batched scope covering
19	/// `inner` consecutive `handle_message` calls; per-event latency
20	/// is available via [`Self::probe_mut`] after [`Self::run_for`]
21	/// returns. The scope's `site_id` is the first `dst_id` in the
22	/// batch (arbitrary-but-stable when `inner > 1`).
23	pub struct SingleThreadRuntime {
24	    actors: Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>,
25	    queue: VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,
26	    probe: TProbe,
27	}
28	
29	impl SingleThreadRuntime {
30	    /// Create an empty runtime with no actors or pending messages.
31	    /// `probe_name` names the embedded probe in its band-table
32	    /// report (e.g. `"goal1.dispatch"`).
33	    pub fn new(probe_name: &str) -> Self {
34	        Self {
35	            actors: Vec::new(),
36	            queue: VecDeque::new(),
37	            probe: TProbe::new(probe_name),
38	        }
39	    }
40	
41	    /// Register `actor`; the returned `u32` is its stable id for
42	    /// the lifetime of the runtime and is usable as `dst_id` in
43	    /// [`Context::send`]. Ids are allocated sequentially from 0.
44	    pub fn add_actor(&mut self, actor: Box<dyn Actor>) -> u32 {
45	        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;
46	        self.actors.push(actor);
47	        id
48	    }
49	
50	    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`. Used to prime the
51	    /// dispatch loop before [`Self::run_for`].
52	    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {
53	        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));
54	    }
55	
56	    /// Dispatch messages until `duration` elapses or the queue
57	    /// empties. Each probe scope wraps `inner` consecutive
58	    /// dispatches. A partial trailing batch (queue emptied
59	    /// mid-scope) is discarded — neither recorded nor counted —
60	    /// so throughput numbers reflect whole-batch work only.
61	    ///
62	    /// Returns the total number of messages counted toward
63	    /// whole batches.
64	    pub fn run_for(&mut self, duration: Duration, inner: u64) -> u64 {
65	        let deadline = Instant::now() + duration;
66	        let mut count = 0u64;
67	        while Instant::now() < deadline {
68	            let first_dst = match self.queue.front() {
69	                Some((d, _)) => *d,
70	                None => break,
71	            };
72	            let Self {
73	                actors,
74	                queue,
75	                probe,
76	            } = self;
77	            let id = probe.start(first_dst as u64);
78	            let mut done: u64 = 0;
79	            while done < inner {
80	                let Some((dst, msg)) = queue.pop_front() else {
81	                    break;
82	                };
83	                let actor = &mut actors[dst as usize];
84	                let mut ctx = SingleCtx { queue };
85	                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);
86	                done += 1;
87	            }
88	            if done == inner {
89	                probe.end_batch(id, inner);
90	                count += done;
91	            } else {
92	                // Partial trailing batch: drop the scope (don't
93	                // record or count) and stop.
94	                break;
95	            }
96	        }
97	        count
98	    }
99	
100	    /// Mutable access to the embedded probe — used by callers to
101	    /// invoke [`TProbe::clear`] at the warmup → measurement
102	    /// boundary and [`TProbe::report`] after [`Self::run_for`]
103	    /// returns.
104	    pub fn probe_mut(&mut self) -> &mut TProbe {
105	        &mut self.probe
106	    }
107	}
108	
109	/// [`Context`] impl used by [`SingleThreadRuntime`]: pushes onto
110	/// the runtime's shared queue.
111	struct SingleCtx<'a> {
112	    queue: &'a mut VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,
113	}
114	
115	impl Context for SingleCtx<'_> {
116	    /// Push `(dst_id, msg)` onto the shared dispatch queue.
117	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {
118	        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));
119	    }
120	}
121	
122	#[cfg(test)]
123	mod tests {
124	    use super::*;
125	
126	    /// Minimal actor that drops every message without replying.
127	    struct Sink;
128	
129	    impl Actor for Sink {
130	        fn handle_message(&mut self, _ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {}
131	    }
132	
133	    /// Bounded ping-pong actor: replies to its peer up to `max`
134	    /// times, then goes silent. Used to produce a deterministic
135	    /// message count in unit tests without wall-clock dependence.
136	    struct Bouncer {
137	        peer: u32,
138	        sent: u32,
139	        max: u32,
140	    }
141	
142	    impl Actor for Bouncer {
143	        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
144	            if self.sent < self.max {
145	                ctx.send(self.peer, Message);
146	                self.sent += 1;
147	            }
148	        }
149	    }
150	
151	    #[test]
152	    fn seed_is_handled_and_queue_drains() {
153	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.drain");
154	        let id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink));
155	        rt.seed(id, Message);
156	        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_millis(1), 1);
157	        assert_eq!(n, 1);
158	    }
159	
160	    #[test]
161	    fn bounded_ping_pong_count() {
162	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.bounded");
163	        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
164	            peer: 1,
165	            sent: 0,
166	            max: 5,
167	        }));
168	        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
169	            peer: 0,
170	            sent: 0,
171	            max: 5,
172	        }));
173	        rt.seed(a, Message);
174	        // seed (1) + 5 from a + 5 from b = 11, all whole batches at inner=1.
175	        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 1);
176	        assert_eq!(n, 11);
177	    }
178	
179	    #[test]
180	    fn add_actor_assigns_sequential_ids() {
181	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.ids");
182	        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 0);
183	        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 1);
184	        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 2);
185	    }
186	
187	    #[test]
188	    fn partial_trailing_batch_is_dropped() {
189	        // 3 total dispatches available, inner=2 ⇒ one whole batch
190	        // counted (2) and one partial (1) discarded.
191	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.partial");
192	        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
193	            peer: 1,
194	            sent: 0,
195	            max: 1,
196	        }));
197	        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
198	            peer: 0,
199	            sent: 0,
200	            max: 1,
201	        }));
202	        rt.seed(a, Message);
203	        // Events: seed→b, b→a (b.sent=1), a→b (a.sent=1), b stops.
204	        // 3 dispatches total. inner=2 ⇒ first batch of 2 recorded
205	        // (count=2), then partial batch of 1 discarded.
206	        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 2);
207	        assert_eq!(n, 2);
208	    }
209	}
210	
211	// --- MultiThreadRuntime -------------------------------------------------
212	
213	/// Multi-threaded runtime: one thread per actor, messages flow
214	/// over per-actor [`std::sync::mpsc`] channels. Each actor thread
215	/// owns a [`TProbe`] recording per-`handle_message` latency at
216	/// `inner=1`; probes are returned to the caller when threads join.
217	///
218	/// Unlike [`SingleThreadRuntime`], actors are registered as
219	/// factory closures — construction runs inside the actor's own
220	/// thread, so users don't need `Send` on the trait itself, only on
221	/// the actor implementor and its factory.
222	///
223	/// Shutdown is in-band: an internal `Signal::Shutdown` value
224	/// travels the same channel that carries user messages, so the
225	/// actor's `recv()` loop exits cleanly after the measurement
226	/// window elapses. A `Signal::ClearProbe` is used at the warmup →
227	/// measurement boundary so only steady-state data reaches the
228	/// report.
229	///
230	/// Ping-pong style workloads hold one message in flight between
231	/// two actors; `inner > 1` batching would leave every probe scope
232	/// partial (queue empties mid-batch) so this runtime hardcodes
233	/// `inner = 1`. If we later add a Goal with higher queue depth
234	/// we'll revisit.
235	pub struct MultiThreadRuntime {
236	    factories: Vec<ActorFactory>,
237	    probe_name_prefix: String,
238	    pending_seeds: Vec<(u32, Message)>,
239	}
240	
241	/// Boxed factory: produces a `Box<dyn Actor + Send>` once, called
242	/// inside the actor's dedicated thread. `Send` on the outer
243	/// closure is required so it can be moved into `thread::spawn`.
244	type ActorFactory = Box<dyn FnOnce() -> Box<dyn Actor + Send> + Send>;
245	
246	/// Internal channel payload. User sends become [`Signal::User`];
247	/// [`Signal::ClearProbe`] and [`Signal::Shutdown`] are injected by
248	/// the runtime to drive phase transitions and exit.
249	enum Signal {
250	    User(Message),
251	    ClearProbe,
252	    Shutdown,
253	}
254	
255	impl MultiThreadRuntime {
256	    /// Create an empty runtime. `probe_name_prefix` is combined
257	    /// with the actor id to form each per-thread probe's name
258	    /// (e.g. `"goal2.dispatch.actor0"`).
259	    pub fn new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self {
260	        Self {
261	            factories: Vec::new(),
262	            probe_name_prefix: probe_name_prefix.to_string(),
263	            pending_seeds: Vec::new(),
264	        }
265	    }
266	
267	    /// Register an actor factory; returns the assigned id
268	    /// (0-indexed). `factory` is called inside the spawned thread
269	    /// that will drive this actor, so the actor type itself does
270	    /// not need to be `Send` before the call — only the factory.
271	    pub fn add_actor<F, A>(&mut self, factory: F) -> u32
272	    where
273	        F: FnOnce() -> A + Send + 'static,
274	        A: Actor + Send + 'static,
275	    {
276	        let id = self.factories.len() as u32;
277	        self.factories.push(Box::new(move || {
278	            Box::new(factory()) as Box<dyn Actor + Send>
279	        }));
280	        id
281	    }
282	
283	    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`. Delivered after
284	    /// all threads spawn and before warmup begins.
285	    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {
286	        self.pending_seeds.push((dst_id, msg));
287	    }
288	
289	    /// Spawn one thread per registered actor, run the warmup
290	    /// phase, clear all probes, run the measurement phase, shut
291	    /// down and join every thread. Returns each actor's
292	    /// `(messages_handled, probe)` pair in id order. Consumes the
293	    /// runtime's registered factories and pending seeds.
294	    ///
295	    /// `pin_cores` is a list of logical CPUs to pin actor threads
296	    /// to; actor `i` pins to `pin_cores[i % pin_cores.len()]`.
297	    /// An empty slice leaves all actor threads unpinned.
298	    pub fn run(
299	        &mut self,
300	        warmup: Duration,
301	        measurement: Duration,
302	        pin_cores: &[usize],
303	    ) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)> {
304	        let factories = std::mem::take(&mut self.factories);
305	        let seeds = std::mem::take(&mut self.pending_seeds);
306	        let n = factories.len();
307	
308	        // Per-actor channels.
309	        let mut senders: Vec<Sender<Signal>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
310	        let mut receivers: Vec<Option<Receiver<Signal>>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
311	        for _ in 0..n {
312	            let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
313	            senders.push(tx);
314	            receivers.push(Some(rx));
315	        }
316	
317	        // Spawn one thread per actor.
318	        let mut handles: Vec<JoinHandle<(u64, TProbe)>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
319	        for (id, factory) in factories.into_iter().enumerate() {
320	            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
321	            // OK: receivers[id] was Some(...) since we pushed one per actor above.
322	            let rx = receivers[id].take().unwrap();
323	            let peers: Vec<Sender<Signal>> = senders.to_vec();
324	            let probe_name = format!("{}.actor{}", self.probe_name_prefix, id);
325	            let pin_target = if pin_cores.is_empty() {
326	                None
327	            } else {
328	                Some(pin_cores[id % pin_cores.len()])
329	            };
330	            let h = thread::Builder::new()
331	                .name(format!("actor-{id}"))
332	                .spawn(move || {
333	                    tprobe::pin::pin_current(pin_target);
334	                    let actor = factory();
335	                    actor_loop(actor, rx, peers, probe_name)
336	                })
337	                .expect("failed to spawn actor thread");
338	            handles.push(h);
339	        }
340	
341	        // Seed initial messages.
342	        for (dst, msg) in seeds {
343	            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
344	            // OK: receivers are alive (threads just spawned); a send failure would
345	            //   indicate an immediate thread panic on startup which the subsequent
346	            //   join will surface with a clearer message.
347	            senders[dst as usize].send(Signal::User(msg)).unwrap();
348	        }
349	
350	        // Phase 1: warmup.
351	        thread::sleep(warmup);
352	
353	        // Clear every probe at the phase boundary.
354	        for tx in &senders {
355	            let _ = tx.send(Signal::ClearProbe);
356	        }
357	
358	        // Phase 2: measurement.
359	        thread::sleep(measurement);
360	
361	        // Phase 3: shutdown.
362	        for tx in &senders {
363	            let _ = tx.send(Signal::Shutdown);
364	        }
365	        // Drop the main-thread copies so no dangling senders keep
366	        // channels open past thread exit.
367	        drop(senders);
368	
369	        // Join and collect (count, probe) in id order.
370	        let mut results = Vec::with_capacity(n);
371	        for h in handles {
372	            results.push(h.join().expect("actor thread panicked"));
373	        }
374	        results
375	    }
376	}
377	
378	/// [`Context`] impl used inside each actor thread: dispatches
379	/// user sends over the shared peer senders.
380	struct MultiCtx<'a> {
381	    senders: &'a [Sender<Signal>],
382	}
383	
384	impl Context for MultiCtx<'_> {
385	    /// Send `msg` to `dst_id`'s mpsc channel. A closed channel
386	    /// (peer has already exited) is silently ignored — matches
387	    /// the shutdown semantics.
388	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {
389	        let _ = self.senders[dst_id as usize].send(Signal::User(msg));
390	    }
391	}
392	
393	/// Actor thread's main loop. Creates a [`TProbe`] named
394	/// `probe_name`, blocks on `rx.recv()`, and for each
395	/// `Signal::User` message probes a scope, dispatches
396	/// `handle_message`, and increments the count.
397	/// `Signal::ClearProbe` resets the probe and count;
398	/// `Signal::Shutdown` (or a closed channel) exits the loop and
399	/// returns `(count, probe)`.
400	fn actor_loop(
401	    mut actor: Box<dyn Actor + Send>,
402	    rx: Receiver<Signal>,
403	    peers: Vec<Sender<Signal>>,
404	    probe_name: String,
405	) -> (u64, TProbe) {
406	    let mut probe = TProbe::new(&probe_name);
407	    let mut count: u64 = 0;
408	    loop {
409	        match rx.recv() {
410	            Ok(Signal::User(msg)) => {
411	                let id = probe.start(0);
412	                let mut ctx = MultiCtx { senders: &peers };
413	                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);
414	                probe.end(id);
415	                count += 1;
416	            }
417	            Ok(Signal::ClearProbe) => {
418	                probe.clear();
419	                count = 0;
420	            }
421	            Ok(Signal::Shutdown) | Err(_) => break,
422	        }
423	    }
424	    (count, probe)
425	}
426	
427	#[cfg(test)]
428	mod mt_tests {
429	    use super::*;
430	
431	    /// Unbounded ping-pong actor used for multi-thread tests.
432	    struct ThreadPingPong {
433	        peer: u32,
434	    }
435	
436	    impl Actor for ThreadPingPong {
437	        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _: Message) {
438	            ctx.send(self.peer, Message);
439	        }
440	    }
441	
442	    #[test]
443	    fn multi_thread_runs_measures_and_shuts_down_cleanly() {
444	        let mut rt = MultiThreadRuntime::new("test");
445	        rt.add_actor(|| ThreadPingPong { peer: 1 });
446	        rt.add_actor(|| ThreadPingPong { peer: 0 });
447	        rt.seed(0, Message);
448	        // 50ms each phase — leaves plenty of slack even on
449	        // heavily loaded CI, while still under 0.2s total.
450	        let results = rt.run(Duration::from_millis(50), Duration::from_millis(50), &[]);
451	        assert_eq!(results.len(), 2);
452	        // Ping-pong is symmetric: both actors should have handled
453	        // messages during the measurement window.
454	        let total: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();
455	        assert!(
456	            total > 0,
457	            "expected some messages processed during measurement, got {total}"
458	        );
459	    }
460	}
461	
```

1670	## Future work: criterion benchmarks for goal1 / goal2
1671	
1672	Effort estimate and design sketch captured for picking up
1673	later. The bot thinks ~3–5 focused hours of work; single-step
1674	`0.4.0` is probably the right shape, though a multi-step
1675	ladder also fits if you want each piece reviewable on its
1676	own.
1677	
1678	### Breakdown
1679	
1680	| Step                          | Effort    | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
1681	|---                            |---        |---                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
1682	| Wire criterion as dev-dep     | 5 min     | `[dev-dependencies]` entry + `[[bench]]` in `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`, `harness = false`.                                                                                                              |
1683	| Expose a benchmarkable API    | 30–60 min | Add `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch(inner) -> u64` (one batch, no deadline check). `run_for` with a duration doesn't fit criterion's "call this closure repeatedly" shape.                           |
1684	| goal1 bench                   | 30 min    | `benches/goal1.rs`: set up runtime once, seed, loop `b.iter(\|\| rt.dispatch_batch(1000))`.                                                                                                               |
1685	| goal2 bench                   | 45–60 min | Trickier — criterion's default `b.iter` would spin threads per-iteration (setup cost dominates). Use `iter_custom` to keep threads alive and measure one ping-pong round-trip as the iteration.           |
1686	| Cross-validation narrative    | 30–60 min | Short `crates/tprobe/notes/cross-validation.md` (or `benches/README.md`) comparing criterion's estimate to tprobe's `mean min-p99` for the same workload.                                                 |
1687	| Polish — docs, README         | 30 min    | README Usage section gains a "Benches" pointer; note `cargo bench` as the entry point.                                                                                                                    |
1688	
1689	### Open design questions
1690	
1691	- **Shared benchmarkable API.** Adding `dispatch_batch` is
1692	  a tprobe-agnostic public API addition to `actor-x1`. The
1693	  bot thinks this is fine — it's a natural primitive the
1694	  goal1 CLI already uses internally — but it's an API
1695	  decision, not a pure refactor. Similar for goal2.
1696	  Alternative: hack the bench against `run_for(tiny_duration, inner)`
1697	  without adding new API; hackier but ~15 min faster.
1698	- **Measurement target.** Criterion defaults to "time per
1699	  call"; our interesting number is "time per message".
1700	  That's just `/inner` for goal1 and `/1` for goal2.
1701	  Criterion also supports `throughput(Throughput::Elements(N))`
1702	  which reports messages/sec directly — the bot thinks
1703	  using that makes output immediately comparable to
1704	  goal1's existing `M msg/s` line.
1705	- **goal2's warmup + calibration story.** Criterion runs
1706	  its own warmup automatically; tprobe's app-level warmup
1707	  is irrelevant in benches. And since criterion measures
1708	  wall-clock rather than subtracting framing, `calibrate()`
1709	  isn't needed either. Benches skip both, which simplifies
1710	  things.
1711	
1712	### Cross-validation expectation
1713	
1714	The bot thinks criterion's per-iteration estimate and
1715	tprobe's `mean min-p99` should agree within:
1716	
1717	- **~10 %** on goal1 at `inner=1000` — both see a clean
1718	  single-thread loop; probe apparatus is amortized.
1719	- **~15 %** on goal2 `--pin 1,12` — cross-thread noise is
1720	  larger, but with same-CCX pinning the signal stabilizes.
1721	
1722	A larger discrepancy would be interesting — could point to
1723	probe bias (e.g. the OoO `rdtsc` coalescing artifact at
1724	`inner=1` showing up differently in criterion's sampling
1725	vs tprobe's direct histogram).
1726	
1727	### If multi-step is desired

1729	- `0.4.0-0` — plan marker.
1730	- `0.4.0-1` — expose `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch`
1731	  + `MultiThreadRuntime::dispatch_one` (or equivalent).
1732	- `0.4.0-2` — goal1 bench.

1734	- `0.4.0-4` — cross-validation narrative in

1736	- `0.4.0` — close, README refresh.
1737	
1738	The bot's lean: single-step `0.4.0` if the `dispatch_batch`

1742	## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness
1743	
1744	Idea captured during `0.3.0-0`; not scheduled for
1745	implementation yet. Explores using compile-time static
1746	registration (`linkme` distributed slices or `inventory` via
1747	`ctor`) to build a `tprobe`-driven benchmark runner where
1748	individual benchmarks declare themselves in separate files

1751	
1752	### Fit for benchmarks
1753	
1754	The bot thinks this is a strong fit. Each benchmark file
1755	drops in a `bench!(...)` invocation; the runner iterates
1756	the registered entries, sets up warmup/calibration/pin
1757	config once, and prints a `tprobe` band table per
1758	benchmark. The measurement primitive already exists
1759	(`tprobe`); the missing piece is the registration and
1760	iteration layer. Result: adding a benchmark is a
1761	one-file change with no central table to edit.
1762	
1763	### Fit for actor applications
1764	
1765	The bot thinks this is a partial fit. Registration gives
1766	discovery ("enumerate every `Actor` type the binary
1767	knows"), but actor construction still needs runtime config
1768	— id, name, channel endpoints, thread placement — which
1769	lives in `main` / runtime setup rather than at declaration
1770	sites. Useful if we find ourselves hand-editing a registry
1771	table per new actor; not a silver bullet for the full
1772	wiring problem.
1773	
1774	### Tradeoffs to validate before committing
1775	
1776	- `linkme` uses linker-section magic. Works on
1777	  `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` (our target) out of the box;
1778	  the bot thinks aggressive `strip` / LTO / unusual link
1779	  modes could in principle drop registrations, so a
1780	  release-mode smoke test with our actual flags is worth
1781	  doing before relying on it.
1782	- `inventory` uses static constructors (via `ctor`).
1783	  Broader platform support, pays a small init-time cost.
1784	  The bot thinks the main historical failure mode is
1785	  reachability elision — a registration in a
1786	  conditionally-compiled or test-only module silently not
1787	  registering — which is usually fine in a binary crate
1788	  but worth a sanity check.
1789	- Both approaches lose the "grep `main.rs` to see every

Before I start, two decisions I need your call on, since the notes at `notes/chores-01.md:1670` already sketch this as future work but leave choices open:

1. **Form factor.** `goal1-crit` and `goal2-crit` can be either:
   - **Criterion benches** at `crates/actor-x1/benches/goal1-crit.rs` and `.../goal2-crit.rs`, run via `cargo bench`. This is criterion's native shape and gives you its regression / baseline tooling for free.
   - **Binaries** at `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1-crit.rs` and `.../goal2-crit.rs` alongside existing `goal1`/`goal2`, still using criterion's `Criterion` machinery but invoked directly like the current goals. More symmetric with `goal1`/`goal2` but fights criterion's grain a bit.

   My lean: **benches**. The name "goal1-crit" works as the file stem / bench target (`cargo bench --bench goal1-crit`). Less glue, more idiomatic.

2. **Public API for benchmarkable work.** Criterion wants a closure it can call repeatedly; `run_for(duration, inner)` doesn't fit. Cleanest is to add one small public method:
   - `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch(inner: u64) -> u64` — one probe-free batch of `inner` dispatches, no deadline check. goal1's existing `run_for` can be refactored to call it, or left alone.
   - For goal2, keep the current `run` shape and use criterion's `iter_custom` to time a wall-clock window (threads stay alive across iters). No new public API needed on `MultiThreadRuntime`.

   Alternative: no new API, benches call `run_for(very_small_duration, inner)` — hackier, ~15 min saved, worse measurement shape.

Plan would be **single-step `0.4.0`** (version bump → implement → one push), following the breakdown in `notes/chores-01.md:1680`.

Want me to proceed with benches + `dispatch_batch`, or redirect?

  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// Mutable access to the embedded probe — used by callers to\n    /// invoke [`TProbe::clear`] at the warmup → measurement\n    /// boundary and [`TProbe::report`] after [`Self::run_for`]\n    /// returns.\n    pub fn probe_mut(&mut self) -> &mut TProbe {\n        &mut self.probe\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    /// Dispatch up to `inner` consecutive messages with no\n    /// deadline check and no probe involvement. Intended for\n    /// benchmark harnesses (e.g. `criterion`) that invoke a\n    /// fixed-size unit of work repeatedly and time it externally,\n    /// so the per-scope [`TProbe`] bookkeeping would only add\n    /// noise.\n    ///\n    /// Returns the number of messages actually dispatched; a\n    /// value less than `inner` means the queue emptied\n    /// mid-batch. Workloads like ping-pong (one reply per\n    /// message) keep at least one message in flight, so in\n    /// steady state this returns exactly `inner`.\n    pub fn dispatch_batch(&mut self, inner: u64) -> u64 {\n        let Self { actors, queue, .. } = self;\n        let mut done: u64 = 0;\n        while done < inner {\n            let Some((dst, msg)) = queue.pop_front() else {\n                break;\n            };\n            let actor = &mut actors[dst as usize];\n            let mut ctx = SingleCtx { queue };\n            actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);\n            done += 1;\n        }\n        done\n    }\n\n    /// Mutable access to the embedded probe — used by callers to\n    /// invoke [`TProbe::clear`] at the warmup → measurement\n    /// boundary and [`TProbe::report`] after [`Self::run_for`]\n    /// returns.\n    pub fn probe_mut(&mut self) -> &mut TProbe {\n        &mut self.probe\n    }\n}"
}

}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/actor-x1/benches/goal1-crit.rs",
  "content": "//! goal1-crit: criterion cross-validation benchmark for goal1.\n//!\n//! Drives the same single-thread two-actor ping-pong workload the\n//! goal1 binary runs, but under criterion's statistical sampling\n//! instead of tprobe's tick histogram. Invoked via\n//! `cargo bench --bench goal1-crit` from `crates/actor-x1`.\n//!\n//! Each iteration is one call to\n//! [`SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch`] of size `inner`; the\n//! benchmark sweeps `inner` ∈ {1, 100, 1000} to mirror goal1's\n//! `--inner` knob. `Throughput::Elements(inner)` has criterion\n//! report messages / second directly so the number can be\n//! compared against goal1's `M msg/s` line and the per-message\n//! time against goal1's `mean min-p99` / `adj mean min-p99` at\n//! the same `inner`.\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse actor_x1::runtime::SingleThreadRuntime;\nuse actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};\nuse criterion::{BenchmarkId, Criterion, Throughput, criterion_group, criterion_main};\n\n/// Same actor goal1's binary uses: reply to every message.\nstruct PingPongActor {\n    peer_id: u32,\n}\n\nimpl Actor for PingPongActor {\n    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Build a freshly-seeded two-actor ping-pong runtime.\nfn fresh_runtime() -> SingleThreadRuntime {\n    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"goal1-crit\");\n    rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));\n    rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));\n    rt.seed(0, Message);\n    rt\n}\n\n/// Parameterize over `inner` to mirror goal1's `--inner` knob.\nfn bench_goal1(c: &mut Criterion) {\n    let mut group = c.benchmark_group(\"goal1-crit\");\n    // Longer sampling reduces run-to-run variation; total bench\n    // time for the sweep stays under ~20 s because each batch\n    // is cheap.\n    group.measurement_time(Duration::from_secs(5));\n    for &inner in &[1u64, 100, 1000] {\n        group.throughput(Throughput::Elements(inner));\n        group.bench_function(BenchmarkId::from_parameter(inner), |b| {\n            let mut rt = fresh_runtime();\n            b.iter(|| rt.dispatch_batch(inner));\n        });\n    }\n    group.finish();\n}\n\ncriterion_group!(benches, bench_goal1);\ncriterion_main!(benches);\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/actor-x1/benches/goal2-crit.rs",
  "content": "//! goal2-crit: criterion cross-validation benchmark for goal2.\n//!\n//! Drives the same two-thread two-actor mpsc ping-pong workload\n//! the goal2 binary runs, via [`MultiThreadRuntime`]. Invoked as\n//! `cargo bench --bench goal2-crit` from `crates/actor-x1`.\n//!\n//! Shape note. `goal2`'s runtime is wall-clock driven — actor\n//! threads stay alive and ping-pong freely for a caller-supplied\n//! duration; there is no \"do N messages\" primitive. To fit\n//! criterion's iteration-count model, each `iter_custom` call\n//! runs a short fixed measurement window, counts the messages\n//! handled, then scales the reported duration to\n//! `measurement * iters / total_count` so criterion's per-iter\n//! estimate reflects per-message time. Each iteration is \"one\n//! `handle_message` call at either actor\". `Throughput::Elements(1)`\n//! makes criterion's throughput summary read as msgs / s.\n//!\n//! Compare the reported per-message time and throughput to\n//! goal2's `mean min-p99` / `adj mean min-p99` and `M msg/s` at\n//! the same pinning configuration (default: unpinned).\n\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n\nuse actor_x1::runtime::MultiThreadRuntime;\nuse actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};\nuse criterion::{Criterion, Throughput, criterion_group, criterion_main};\n\n/// Same actor goal2's binary uses: reply to every message. Must\n/// be `Send` to cross thread boundaries.\nstruct PingPongActor {\n    peer_id: u32,\n}\n\nimpl Actor for PingPongActor {\n    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Per `iter_custom` call: spin up a fresh runtime, run a short\n/// steady-state window, scale to iters. See module docs for why.\nfn bench_goal2(c: &mut Criterion) {\n    let mut group = c.benchmark_group(\"goal2-crit\");\n    group.throughput(Throughput::Elements(1));\n    // Keep total bench time bounded. Per sample: 50 ms warmup +\n    // 100 ms measurement + thread spawn/join (~a few ms) ≈ 160 ms.\n    // 50 samples * 160 ms ≈ 8 s of the sampling phase, plus\n    // criterion's own internal warmup (a handful of calls).\n    group.measurement_time(Duration::from_secs(10));\n    group.sample_size(50);\n    group.bench_function(\"pingpong\", |b| {\n        b.iter_custom(|iters| {\n            let mut rt = MultiThreadRuntime::new(\"goal2-crit\");\n            rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 });\n            rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 });\n            rt.seed(0, Message);\n\n            let warmup = Duration::from_millis(50);\n            let measurement = Duration::from_millis(100);\n            let results = rt.run(warmup, measurement, &[]);\n\n            let total_count: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();\n            // Scale: `measurement` of wall time produced\n            // `total_count` messages. Criterion wants total time\n            // for `iters` iterations, so project linearly.\n            let per_msg_ns = measurement.as_nanos() as u64 / total_count.max(1);\n            Duration::from_nanos(per_msg_ns.saturating_mul(iters))\n        });\n    });\n    group.finish();\n}\n\n/// Silence unused-`Instant`-import warning in case we later\n/// wire raw wall-clock measurement alongside the scaled value.\n#[allow(dead_code)]\nfn _instant_silencer() -> Instant {\n    Instant::now()\n}\n\ncriterion_group!(benches, bench_goal2);\ncriterion_main!(benches);\n"
}

3	An experiment in the actor model in Rust (Communicating

1601	## Comma-format summary counts (0.3.2)
1602	
1603	Pulls `fmt_commas` up into `tprobe`'s public API so the
1604	`goal1` / `goal2` binaries can reuse it on their summary
1605	lines. The raw message counts on those lines used to render
1606	undifferentiated — e.g. `goal1: 61687000 messages` — which
1607	at 8-9 digits was hard to read at a glance. They now read
1608	`goal1: 61,687,000 messages`, matching the band-table
1609	report's existing comma formatting for `count=N` and band
1610	`count` columns.
1611	
1612	- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.1` → `0.3.2`.
1613	- `crates/tprobe/Cargo.toml`: `0.1.4` → `0.1.5` — API
1614	  surface gains two re-exports.
1615	- `crates/tprobe/src/lib.rs`: `pub(crate) mod fmt;` →
1616	  `pub mod fmt;`; adds `pub use fmt::{fmt_commas,
1617	  fmt_commas_f64};` at the crate root.
1618	- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs`,
1619	  `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`: `use tprobe::...`
1620	  gains `fmt_commas`; the `goalN: <count> messages ...`
1621	  summary line uses `fmt_commas(count)`. goal2 also
1622	  comma-formats the per-actor `"  actor i: handled N
1623	  messages"` line. goal1 additionally comma-formats
1624	  `inner=N` in both its summary line and the
1625	  `apparatus:` line (relevant once users pass
1626	  `-i 1000000` or similar).
1627	
1628	### Design decisions recorded here
1629	
1630	- **Promote `fmt_commas` to public rather than duplicate
1631	  it.** The utility already lives in `tprobe` (needed by
1632	  the band-table renderer). Binaries reusing it via
1633	  `pub use` is cheaper than copy-pasting two functions
1634	  into actor-x1 — and if `tprobe` ever publishes, the
1635	  helpers travel with it as part of a coherent surface.
1636	- **Re-export at the crate root, keep `fmt` module also
1637	  public.** `use tprobe::fmt_commas` is the ergonomic
1638	  short path; `use tprobe::fmt::fmt_commas` still works
1639	  for callers who prefer module-qualified imports.
1640	  Exporting only `fmt_commas` would be stricter but the
1641	  paired `fmt_commas_f64` has the same utility shape,
1642	  so both go. `fmt::fmt` module name is workspace-local
1643	  and unlikely to collide.
1644	- **Only message counts comma-formatted, not the
1645	  throughput / wall-clock floats.** `204.486 M msg/s` is
1646	  fine as a 4-5 char decimal; `0.300s` is likewise
1647	  small. The comma-format helper targets integer counts
1648	  where the digit grouping actually helps.
1649	- **Single-step `0.3.2` patch release.** Two-file
1650	  application change plus a one-line `lib.rs` re-export.
1651	  No `-N` ladder.
1652	
1653	## Measurement precision and repeatability
1654	
1655	Methodology note added at
1656	[`crates/tprobe/notes/precision.md`](../crates/tprobe/notes/precision.md).
1657	Captures the precision floors a `tprobe` measurement is
1658	bound by (hardware tick, apparatus framing noise, OoO
1659	`rdtsc` coalescing, hdrhistogram bucket width, unmeasurable
1660	systemic noise), the shell-loop technique for measuring
1661	run-to-run repeatability, expected-scale heuristics on this
1662	3900X box, and a `--repeat N` future-work proposal for
1663	building the repeatability measurement into the binaries.
1664	
1665	Placed in `crates/tprobe/notes/` rather than inline here
1666	because the content is tprobe-specific reference material —
1667	it travels with the crate if tprobe splits into its own
1668	repo later. Tracked as future work via todo `[18]`.
1669	
1670	## Future work: criterion benchmarks for goal1 / goal2
```

1670	## Future work: criterion benchmarks for goal1 / goal2
1671	
1672	Effort estimate and design sketch captured for picking up
1673	later. The bot thinks ~3–5 focused hours of work; single-step
1674	`0.4.0` is probably the right shape, though a multi-step
1675	ladder also fits if you want each piece reviewable on its
1676	own.
1677	
1678	### Breakdown
1679	
1680	| Step                          | Effort    | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
1681	|---                            |---        |---                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
1682	| Wire criterion as dev-dep     | 5 min     | `[dev-dependencies]` entry + `[[bench]]` in `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`, `harness = false`.                                                                                                              |
1683	| Expose a benchmarkable API    | 30–60 min | Add `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch(inner) -> u64` (one batch, no deadline check). `run_for` with a duration doesn't fit criterion's "call this closure repeatedly" shape.                           |
1684	| goal1 bench                   | 30 min    | `benches/goal1.rs`: set up runtime once, seed, loop `b.iter(\|\| rt.dispatch_batch(1000))`.                                                                                                               |
1685	| goal2 bench                   | 45–60 min | Trickier — criterion's default `b.iter` would spin threads per-iteration (setup cost dominates). Use `iter_custom` to keep threads alive and measure one ping-pong round-trip as the iteration.           |
1686	| Cross-validation narrative    | 30–60 min | Short `crates/tprobe/notes/cross-validation.md` (or `benches/README.md`) comparing criterion's estimate to tprobe's `mean min-p99` for the same workload.                                                 |
1687	| Polish — docs, README         | 30 min    | README Usage section gains a "Benches" pointer; note `cargo bench` as the entry point.                                                                                                                    |
1688	
1689	### Open design questions
1690	
1691	- **Shared benchmarkable API.** Adding `dispatch_batch` is
1692	  a tprobe-agnostic public API addition to `actor-x1`. The
1693	  bot thinks this is fine — it's a natural primitive the
1694	  goal1 CLI already uses internally — but it's an API
1695	  decision, not a pure refactor. Similar for goal2.
1696	  Alternative: hack the bench against `run_for(tiny_duration, inner)`
1697	  without adding new API; hackier but ~15 min faster.
1698	- **Measurement target.** Criterion defaults to "time per
1699	  call"; our interesting number is "time per message".
1700	  That's just `/inner` for goal1 and `/1` for goal2.
1701	  Criterion also supports `throughput(Throughput::Elements(N))`
1702	  which reports messages/sec directly — the bot thinks
1703	  using that makes output immediately comparable to
1704	  goal1's existing `M msg/s` line.
1705	- **goal2's warmup + calibration story.** Criterion runs
1706	  its own warmup automatically; tprobe's app-level warmup
1707	  is irrelevant in benches. And since criterion measures
1708	  wall-clock rather than subtracting framing, `calibrate()`
1709	  isn't needed either. Benches skip both, which simplifies
1710	  things.
1711	
1712	### Cross-validation expectation
1713	
1714	The bot thinks criterion's per-iteration estimate and
1715	tprobe's `mean min-p99` should agree within:
1716	
1717	- **~10 %** on goal1 at `inner=1000` — both see a clean
1718	  single-thread loop; probe apparatus is amortized.
1719	- **~15 %** on goal2 `--pin 1,12` — cross-thread noise is
1720	  larger, but with same-CCX pinning the signal stabilizes.
1721	
1722	A larger discrepancy would be interesting — could point to
1723	probe bias (e.g. the OoO `rdtsc` coalescing artifact at
1724	`inner=1` showing up differently in criterion's sampling
1725	vs tprobe's direct histogram).
1726	
1727	### If multi-step is desired
1728	
1729	- `0.4.0-0` — plan marker.
1730	- `0.4.0-1` — expose `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch`
1731	  + `MultiThreadRuntime::dispatch_one` (or equivalent).
1732	- `0.4.0-2` — goal1 bench.
1733	- `0.4.0-3` — goal2 bench.
1734	- `0.4.0-4` — cross-validation narrative in
1735	  `crates/tprobe/notes/`.
1736	- `0.4.0` — close, README refresh.
1737	
1738	The bot's lean: single-step `0.4.0` if the `dispatch_batch`
1739	shape is clear up-front; multi-step if the API pieces
1740	deserve separate review.
1741	
1742	## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness
1743	
1744	Idea captured during `0.3.0-0`; not scheduled for
```

  "old_string": "## Future work: criterion benchmarks for goal1 / goal2\n\nEffort estimate and design sketch captured for picking up\nlater. The bot thinks ~3–5 focused hours of work; single-step\n`0.4.0` is probably the right shape, though a multi-step\nladder also fits if you want each piece reviewable on its\nown.\n\n### Breakdown\n\n| Step                          | Effort    | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                       |\n|---                            |---        |---                                                                                                                                                                                                        |\n| Wire criterion as dev-dep     | 5 min     | `[dev-dependencies]` entry + `[[bench]]` in `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`, `harness = false`.                                                                                                              |\n| Expose a benchmarkable API    | 30–60 min | Add `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch(inner) -> u64` (one batch, no deadline check). `run_for` with a duration doesn't fit criterion's \"call this closure repeatedly\" shape.                           |\n| goal1 bench                   | 30 min    | `benches/goal1.rs`: set up runtime once, seed, loop `b.iter(\\|\\| rt.dispatch_batch(1000))`.                                                                                                               |\n| goal2 bench                   | 45–60 min | Trickier — criterion's default `b.iter` would spin threads per-iteration (setup cost dominates). Use `iter_custom` to keep threads alive and measure one ping-pong round-trip as the iteration.           |\n| Cross-validation narrative    | 30–60 min | Short `crates/tprobe/notes/cross-validation.md` (or `benches/README.md`) comparing criterion's estimate to tprobe's `mean min-p99` for the same workload.                                                 |\n| Polish — docs, README         | 30 min    | README Usage section gains a \"Benches\" pointer; note `cargo bench` as the entry point.                                                                                                                    |\n\n### Open design questions\n\n- **Shared benchmarkable API.** Adding `dispatch_batch` is\n  a tprobe-agnostic public API addition to `actor-x1`. The\n  bot thinks this is fine — it's a natural primitive the\n  goal1 CLI already uses internally — but it's an API\n  decision, not a pure refactor. Similar for goal2.\n  Alternative: hack the bench against `run_for(tiny_duration, inner)`\n  without adding new API; hackier but ~15 min faster.\n- **Measurement target.** Criterion defaults to \"time per\n  call\"; our interesting number is \"time per message\".\n  That's just `/inner` for goal1 and `/1` for goal2.\n  Criterion also supports `throughput(Throughput::Elements(N))`\n  which reports messages/sec directly — the bot thinks\n  using that makes output immediately comparable to\n  goal1's existing `M msg/s` line.\n- **goal2's warmup + calibration story.** Criterion runs\n  its own warmup automatically; tprobe's app-level warmup\n  is irrelevant in benches. And since criterion measures\n  wall-clock rather than subtracting framing, `calibrate()`\n  isn't needed either. Benches skip both, which simplifies\n  things.\n\n### Cross-validation expectation\n\nThe bot thinks criterion's per-iteration estimate and\ntprobe's `mean min-p99` should agree within:\n\n- **~10 %** on goal1 at `inner=1000` — both see a clean\n  single-thread loop; probe apparatus is amortized.\n- **~15 %** on goal2 `--pin 1,12` — cross-thread noise is\n  larger, but with same-CCX pinning the signal stabilizes.\n\nA larger discrepancy would be interesting — could point to\nprobe bias (e.g. the OoO `rdtsc` coalescing artifact at\n`inner=1` showing up differently in criterion's sampling\nvs tprobe's direct histogram).\n\n### If multi-step is desired\n\n- `0.4.0-0` — plan marker.\n- `0.4.0-1` — expose `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch`\n  + `MultiThreadRuntime::dispatch_one` (or equivalent).\n- `0.4.0-2` — goal1 bench.\n- `0.4.0-3` — goal2 bench.\n- `0.4.0-4` — cross-validation narrative in\n  `crates/tprobe/notes/`.\n- `0.4.0` — close, README refresh.\n\nThe bot's lean: single-step `0.4.0` if the `dispatch_batch`\nshape is clear up-front; multi-step if the API pieces\ndeserve separate review.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness",
  "new_string": "## Criterion benchmarks for goal1/goal2 (0.4.0)\n\nAdds `goal1-crit` and `goal2-crit` criterion benches under\n`crates/actor-x1/benches/` that drive the same ping-pong\nworkloads as the `goal1` and `goal2` binaries, so criterion's\nstatistical sampling can cross-validate `tprobe`'s\ntick-histogram numbers. Invoked from `crates/actor-x1/` as\n`cargo bench --bench goal1-crit` and\n`cargo bench --bench goal2-crit`.\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.3.2` → `0.4.0`; adds\n  `criterion = \"0.5\"` to `[dev-dependencies]` and a\n  `[[bench]]` entry per bench (`harness = false`).\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs`: new\n  `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch(inner: u64) -> u64`\n  — one batched dispatch with no deadline check and no\n  probe involvement, suitable for `b.iter` closures.\n  Returns the number of messages actually dispatched\n  (equals `inner` in steady-state ping-pong; less if the\n  queue drains mid-batch). Two new unit tests:\n  steady-state full count, and partial-on-drain.\n- `crates/actor-x1/benches/goal1-crit.rs`: new file.\n  Parameterized sweep over `inner ∈ {1, 100, 1000}` via\n  `BenchmarkId::from_parameter`; each iteration calls\n  `rt.dispatch_batch(inner)` on a long-lived runtime.\n  `Throughput::Elements(inner)` so criterion's summary\n  reads as msgs / s.\n- `crates/actor-x1/benches/goal2-crit.rs`: new file. Uses\n  `iter_custom` — each call spins up a fresh\n  `MultiThreadRuntime`, runs a 50 ms warmup + 100 ms\n  measurement window, counts handled messages, and\n  returns a duration scaled to\n  `measurement * iters / total_count` so criterion's\n  per-iter estimate reads as per-message time.\n  `Throughput::Elements(1)`; `sample_size(50)` and\n  `measurement_time(10 s)` cap the bench at ~8 s of\n  sampling.\n- `crates/actor-x1/README.md`: new `## Benches` section\n  pointing at `cargo bench --bench goalN-crit`.\n- `notes/todo.md`: criterion-benchmark todo moves from\n  `## Todo` to `## Done`; ref [19] repointed from the\n  removed Future-work section to this one.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **`SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch` as a new\n  public API rather than a `run_for(tiny_duration, inner)`\n  hack.** `run_for` takes a wall-clock deadline and wraps\n  every scope in a `tprobe` `start`/`end_batch` pair —\n  neither fits criterion's \"call this closure repeatedly\"\n  shape. A minimal `dispatch_batch(inner) -> u64` primitive\n  with no timing bookkeeping is the natural `b.iter` shape,\n  and stays useful independent of the benches. Refactoring\n  `run_for` itself to delegate to `dispatch_batch` was\n  considered and deferred — keeps the diff narrow and\n  `run_for`'s probe integration is the thing actually\n  tested in production.\n- **`goal2-crit` scales the returned duration.**\n  `MultiThreadRuntime::run(warmup, measurement, pins)` is\n  wall-clock driven — there's no \"run until N messages\"\n  primitive, and the actor threads exchange messages\n  autonomously once running. Inside `iter_custom` the\n  bench runs a fixed measurement window, counts messages\n  handled, and returns `measurement * iters / total_count`\n  so criterion's per-iter estimate reads as per-message\n  time regardless of what `iters` value criterion picks.\n  Alternative — adding a message-counting stop signal to\n  `MultiThreadRuntime` — would bloat the runtime API for\n  a benchmark-only use case.\n- **Default configs are unpinned.** `goal1` and `goal2`\n  default to unpinned; the benches match that so the\n  no-flag comparison is apples-to-apples. Pinned\n  cross-validation (e.g. goal2 `--pin 1,12` vs a\n  pinned bench variant) can be added later via env var\n  or a second bench target if the comparison becomes\n  load-bearing.\n- **`criterion` default features enabled.** Brings in\n  `rayon` / `plotters` / `html_reports` for free. The\n  HTML reports under `target/criterion/` are useful for\n  eyeballing the distribution shape during\n  cross-validation; the compile-time cost (~30 s first\n  build) is absorbed once and cached afterwards.\n- **Single-step `0.4.0`.** Four artifacts — `dispatch_batch`,\n  criterion wiring, two bench files — review naturally as\n  one change. The earlier Future-work entry sketched a\n  `-N` ladder as optional; not needed.\n\n### Cross-validation expectation\n\n(Moved from the earlier Future-work entry so it stays\ndiscoverable next to the implementation.) The bot thinks\n`criterion`'s per-iteration estimate and `tprobe`'s\n`mean min-p99` should agree within:\n\n- **~10 %** on goal1 at `inner=1000` — both see a clean\n  single-thread loop; probe apparatus is amortized.\n- **~15 %** on goal2 unpinned — cross-thread noise is\n  larger; with same-CCX pinning (`--pin 1,12`) the\n  signal stabilizes.\n\nA larger discrepancy would be interesting — could point to\nprobe bias (e.g. the OoO `rdtsc` coalescing artifact at\n`inner=1` showing up differently in criterion's sampling\nvs tprobe's direct histogram).\n\nSpot check during development, unpinned on the dev box:\n`goal2` reported 228 K msg/s; `goal2-crit` reported\n~240 K msg/s.
