> DEVELOPER

Looking at goal2-crit, is measuring my measuring by calling rt.run so they are not measuring the same thing

> AGENT

You're right — that's a real bug. `goal2-crit` calls `rt.run(...)` which internally has every actor thread wrap `handle_message` in a `TProbe::start`/`end` pair (see `actor_loop` at `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs:406`). So criterion is measuring `work + tprobe overhead`, not just work. goal1-crit doesn't have this problem because `dispatch_batch` is probe-free.

That defeats the whole point of the cross-validation — if `tprobe` is biased, a measurement that includes `tprobe` inside it can't detect the bias.

Fix options:

1. **Add `MultiThreadRuntime::run_no_probe(warmup, measurement, pins) -> Vec<u64>`** — parallel to `SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch`. Duplicates `actor_loop` without the probe calls. Small amount of code, keeps both paths simple and fast (no per-message branch). My lean.
2. **Thread a `probe_enabled: bool` through `run` / `actor_loop`.** No duplication but adds a branch per message (tiny, but spoils the clean comparison).
3. **Skip the runtime in goal2-crit — raw threads + mpsc.** Honest and probe-free but it's no longer "same work" as goal2; it's a reimplementation of ping-pong.

Recommendation: option 1, bumped as `0.4.1` (bug fix in the freshly-shipped 0.4.0 benches).

OK to proceed with option 1?

> DEVELOPER

I have a new goal, goalzc, which has an ActorZC trait with a handle that takes messages of that are defined using googles zerocopy crate so something like:
pub trait ActorZC { fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, msg: zerocopy::AsBytes); }

> AGENT

A few things to sort out before I start sketching — `pub trait ActorZC { fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, msg: zerocopy::AsBytes); }` as written won't compile, and the direction has a couple of forks worth picking deliberately:

1. **zerocopy version / trait name.** `AsBytes` was renamed to `IntoBytes` in zerocopy 0.8 (current). Are you targeting 0.8 (`IntoBytes` + `FromBytes` + `Immutable` + `KnownLayout`), or pinning 0.7 for the older `AsBytes`/`FromBytes` naming?

2. **Trait shape.** `AsBytes`/`IntoBytes` is a trait, not a type, so the signature can't be literally `msg: zerocopy::AsBytes`. Three concrete shapes to pick from:
   - **Generic over M** — `trait ActorZC<M: IntoBytes + FromBytes + …> { fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, msg: M); }`. Monomorphized, zero-cost dispatch over a typed message, but each actor binds to one `M` and the runtime's message queue has to be typed too.
   - **Byte slice** — `fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx, bytes: &[u8])`, actor does its own `M::ref_from_bytes(bytes)`. Dyn-compatible, runtime stays message-agnostic, but moves the parse into the actor.
   - **Dyn trait object** — `fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx, msg: &dyn IntoBytes)`. Dyn-compatible but loses most of zerocopy's point; the actor can only serialize, not cast back to a typed view.

   My lean: **generic on M** if goalzc is "one message type per actor" (natural fit for zerocopy's type-safety story); **byte slice** if goalzc is "mixed message types over one channel" (matches goal1/goal2's `Message` shape more closely).

3. **What goalzc is measuring.** Is the point to (a) replace the current unit `Message` with sized zerocopy payloads to see per-message cost at realistic sizes, (b) specifically benchmark zerocopy's cast/deserialize overhead inside the dispatch loop, or (c) explore a fully generic runtime where the message type is a parameter?

4. **goal2-crit fix still open.** We left the probe-contamination bug from the previous turn unpatched (no `run_no_probe` yet). Do you want that rolled into this goalzc work, pushed separately as `0.4.1` first, or parked?

Which shape + which goal do you want, and what to do about goal2-crit?

> DEVELOPER

1. Definitely whatever is latest, but it should probably just be `msg: &[u8]`
2. byte slice

> AGENT

Good — so the trait is:

```rust
pub trait ActorZC {
    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, msg: &[u8]);
}
```

And `zerocopy` becomes an *actor-implementation* dependency (actors call `SomeStruct::ref_from_bytes(msg)` or `Ref::from_bytes(msg)`), not a trait signature dependency. That's cleaner.

Still need your call on four things before I can start:

1. **Message ownership across the channel.** `&[u8]` works at the actor's handler boundary, but the runtime has to store / ship the bytes somehow. Options — (a) `Vec<u8>` — owned, heap-allocates per send; (b) `Box<[u8]>` — same story, slightly tighter; (c) `bytes::Bytes` — refcounted, zero-copy clones; (d) a pooled buffer scheme — cheapest steady-state, most plumbing. For a first cut my lean is **`Vec<u8>`** — matches stdlib, no new dep, and the allocation cost is exactly the kind of thing goalzc is meant to measure against goal1/goal2's unit `Message`.

2. **Runtime flavor.** Does `goalzc` mirror goal1 (single-thread, `VecDeque`) or goal2 (multi-thread, mpsc)? Or both (`goalzc1` / `goalzc2`)? `bin/goalzc.rs` as the single binary name suggests one; my lean is start with **single-thread** (fastest feedback loop, zerocopy cast cost is clearest without thread noise) and add multi-thread if it proves interesting.

3. **What you're measuring.** (a) Cost of a realistic sized payload (e.g. 32 or 64 bytes) vs goal1's unit `Message` — how does per-message time move? (b) Specifically the zerocopy cast overhead — does `Foo::ref_from_bytes(msg)` add measurable cycles vs treating the bytes as raw? (c) Both? Knowing the target shapes the actor's `handle_message` body.

4. **goal2-crit probe-contamination fix** — roll it into goalzc's `0.5.0`, push separately as `0.4.1` first, or park? My lean: **park for now** — it's a known limitation, documented, and goalzc is more interesting to build while the design is fresh. We can loop back.

Your calls?

> DEVELOPER

1. Ideally actors get_msg(size) from a factory and use channels to communicate and messages are returned to the factory when they are dropped
2. multi-threaded initially 1:1 later M:N
3. measure cost of sending messages between actors for a period of time, so not individual sends initially
4. implement goalzc and goalzc-crit the goal is they measure exactly the same "work"

> AGENT

Good — clear target. Let me sketch the shape so we can nail a few decisions before code:

**Core pieces**

- `PooledMsg` — owned buffer (`Vec<u8>` or `Box<[u8]>`) with a custom `Drop` that returns itself to a shared `Arc<Pool>`. `Deref<Target=[u8]>` so the handler's `&[u8]` is just `&*pooled`.
- `Pool` — `Arc<Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>>>` for MVP (simple, some lock contention; measureable and upgradeable later to lock-free).
- `ContextZC` — `get_msg(size) -> PooledMsg` + `send(dst_id, msg: PooledMsg)`.
- `ActorZC` — `fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC, msg: &[u8])`. Runtime owns the incoming `PooledMsg`, hands the handler a borrow, drops it after the handler returns (so it goes back to the pool).
- `MultiThreadRuntimeZC` — 1:1 per-actor threads + mpsc, same shape as `MultiThreadRuntime` but the channel carries `Signal::User(PooledMsg)`. Two entry points: `run(...)` (with tprobe) for `goalzc`, and `run_no_probe(...)` (probe-free) for `goalzc-crit`. Applies the goal2-crit lesson from the start.
- `goalzc` binary — `--size N` flag (message byte size), `--warmup` / `--pin` matching goal2. Prints throughput + tprobe band table.
- `goalzc-crit` bench — criterion + `run_no_probe`, so it measures exactly the same work, probe-free.

**Handler pattern in ping-pong** (confirm this matches your intent):
```rust
fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC, msg: &[u8]) {
    let reply = ctx.get_msg(msg.len());
    ctx.send(self.peer_id, reply);
}
```
Every dispatch: pool `get` + channel send + recipient drops → pool `put`. Steady state is two buffers cycling through the pool (plus any slack). If that pool traffic is the *point* of the measurement, great — goalzc shows the cost vs goal2's unit-Message baseline.

**Decisions needed**

1. **Message size** — CLI flag `--size N` with a default (say 64 bytes)? Or fixed at compile time for the MVP?
2. **Pool buffer policy** — fixed-size (all buffers are `--size` bytes; simpler) or variable/size-classed (more general, more plumbing). My lean: **fixed-size** — matches the benchmark's "one ping-pong size" reality and keeps the pool as a `Vec<Vec<u8>>`.
3. **Version ladder** — this is meaningful scope (pool + trait + runtime + binary + bench). My lean: **multi-step `0.5.0`** with roughly these `-N` checkpoints: `-0` plan marker, `-1` Pool + PooledMsg, `-2` ActorZC + ContextZC + MultiThreadRuntimeZC (both `run` and `run_no_probe`), `-3` goalzc binary, `-4` goalzc-crit bench, `0.5.0` close (README + notes).
4. **Existing `MultiThreadRuntime`** — leave it untouched (goalzc is a parallel runtime, not a rewrite), and accept that the goal2-crit probe-contamination bug stays as-is until we loop back. OK?

Your calls on 1–4?

> DEVELOPER

1. Initially all messages are some MAX_SIZE
2. All are MAX_SIZE for now but later there will be sub-pools in the factory that handles which support various sizes
3. ms 0.5.0
4. Yes create new RuntimeZC

> AGENT

Ladder confirmed:

- `0.5.0-0` — plan marker (version bump + new `## goalzc + RuntimeZC: pooled zerocopy ping-pong (0.5.0)` section in `notes/chores-01.md` capturing this exchange).
- `0.5.0-1` — `pool.rs`: `Pool` + `PooledMsg` (with `Drop` return path) + unit tests.
- `0.5.0-2` — `runtime_zc.rs`: `ActorZC` / `ContextZC` traits + `RuntimeZC` with both `run` (tprobe-instrumented) and `run_no_probe` (probe-free) entry points + unit tests.
- `0.5.0-3` — `bin/goalzc.rs` (mirrors `goal2.rs`'s CLI shape; prints throughput + tprobe band table).
- `0.5.0-4` — `benches/goalzc-crit.rs` (criterion over `run_no_probe`; same scaling trick as goal2-crit but probe-clean this time).
- `0.5.0` — close: README Benches/Binaries updates, `notes/todo.md` move to Done, any polish.

Each `-N` gets its own `vc-x1 push main` cycle per CLAUDE.md's "run this flow after every step".

Two small decisions still open for `-2` / `-3` (so I can flag them now rather than mid-implementation):

1. **MAX_SIZE exposure** — (a) `const MAX_SIZE: usize = 64;` baked into the crate (simplest, forces re-build to vary); (b) a `--size N` CLI flag on `goalzc` with a default 64 (explores size sensitivity in one binary; `N` gets validated against `MAX_SIZE` or *becomes* `MAX_SIZE` for the run). My lean: **(b)** — goalzc is a benchmark, size sensitivity is exactly the kind of thing you'd want to sweep.
2. **Pool initial capacity** — start empty and lazily allocate on `get_msg`, or pre-populate (e.g., 4 buffers at runtime construction time). My lean: **start empty** — steady-state is 2 buffers for ping-pong, lazy allocation settles there after the first couple of dispatches. Pre-populating is a micro-optimization that distorts first-dispatch latency (already dropped by warmup).

OK to proceed to `0.5.0-0`? I'll bump the version, write the plan section in `notes/chores-01.md` capturing the ladder + both decisions above, and stop for review before the commit.

939	### Design decisions recorded here
940	
941	- **Extend subtraction to `loop_per_iter_ticks`.** Earlier
942	  (`0.1.0-6`) the subtraction was deliberately scoped to
943	  framing only, on the grounds that "the real dispatch loop's
944	  per-iter cost is part of what the user wants to measure".
945	  Revisited: the real loop's scaffolding (loop branch,
946	  counter increment) is structural overhead relative to the
947	  dispatch work, and `black_box(1)`'s `loop_per_iter_ticks`
948	  is a close-enough proxy for it. Biased slightly low
949	  because the real loop doesn't execute the `black_box`
950	  itself; the bias is small and worth the simpler story in
951	  the output. `overhead-model.md` records this tradeoff
952	  explicitly.
953	- **Global average correction, not per-band.** Correction
954	  depends on `batch`; in the current PoC every record in a
955	  given probe has the same `batch`, so a single constant
956	  correction applies uniformly. If later probes ever mix
957	  `batch` sizes within one drain, a per-band correction sum
958	  would be more faithful. Deferred until that situation
959	  actually arises; `overhead-model.md` flags the extension
960	  point.

Passing `PooledMsg` by value into the handler\n  would let a clever actor reuse the incoming buffer as\n  the outgoing one (avoiding pool churn), but the `&[u8]`\n  shape matches what most real zerocopy receivers look\n  like and is the cleaner cross-validation target.\n- **Fixed-size buffers (`MAX_SIZE`) for the MVP.**\n  Simpler pool (`Vec<Vec<u8>>`); matches a ping-pong\n  workload where every message is the same size. A\n  size-classed sub-pool factory is a future extension —\n  mentioned explicitly so it isn't lost.\n- **Lazy pool allocation.** Pool starts empty; `get_msg`\n  allocates a fresh `Vec<u8>` if the free list is empty.\n  Steady state settles to 2 buffers for ping-pong after\n  the first couple of dispatches. Pre-populating a fixed\n  capacity is a micro-optimization that distorts\n  first-dispatch latency (already dropped by warmup) and\n  is deferred.\n- **`--size N` CLI flag, not a compile-time const.**\n  goalzc is a benchmark; size sensitivity is exactly the\n  kind of thing a reader would want to sweep. `N` is\n  validated against `MAX_SIZE` at startup; if `N >\n  MAX_SIZE` the binary errors cleanly rather than\n  silently truncating.\n- **`RuntimeZC` is a parallel runtime, not a replacement.**\n  `MultiThreadRuntime` stays untouched — existing `goal2`\n  / `goal2-crit` keep working. `RuntimeZC` lives in its\n  own module and has no shared code with\n  `MultiThreadRuntime`; duplication is the price for a\n  clean split that's easy to revisit once the shape\n  stabilizes. The goal2-crit probe-contamination bug\n  stays un-patched in `MultiThreadRuntime` for now —\n  the `run_no_probe` entry point exists here from the\n  start instead.\n- **Multi-step `0.5.0` ladder.** Five natural checkpoints\n  (pool, runtime, bin, bench, close); each reviewable on\n  its own; a single-step `0.5.0` would be a large diff\n  to review in one pass.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"
}
```

  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs",
  "content": "//! Fixed-size buffer pool for `RuntimeZC` zerocopy messages.\n//!\n//! [`Pool`] hands out owned [`PooledMsg`] buffers up to\n//! [`MAX_SIZE`] bytes long. When a `PooledMsg` is dropped, its\n//! underlying `Vec<u8>` returns to the pool's free list rather\n//! than being freed; the next `get_msg` call reuses it. The\n//! pool starts empty and grows lazily as `get_msg` is called;\n//! steady state for ping-pong settles at two buffers.\n//!\n//! Storage is `Arc<Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>>>` — simple, some lock\n//! contention under multi-thread load. Switching to a lock-free\n//! structure (e.g. `crossbeam_queue::SegQueue`) is a future\n//! optimization once goalzc shows the contention cost actually\n//! matters.\n//!\n//! No zeroing happens on drop. A reused buffer's bytes\n//! `0..new_size` may contain residual data from the previous\n//! occupant; ping-pong handlers either overwrite or ignore\n//! those bytes, so the leak is benign for the workloads this\n//! crate measures. Real applications that ship pooled messages\n//! between trust domains would need an explicit zeroize step.\n\nuse std::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};\nuse std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};\n\n/// Maximum logical length, in bytes, of any [`PooledMsg`] this\n/// pool hands out. `get_msg(size)` panics if `size > MAX_SIZE`.\n/// Sized to fit common small message shapes (header + a handful\n/// of `u64`s) in one cache line; bump if the workloads grow.\npub const MAX_SIZE: usize = 64;\n\n/// Internal pool storage; held by `Arc` so actor threads share\n/// one free list. Keep this type private — callers see only\n/// [`Pool`] and [`PooledMsg`].\nstruct PoolInner {\n    free: Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>>,\n}\n\n/// Cheaply-cloneable handle to a shared buffer pool. Clones\n/// share the same free list (internally `Arc`).\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct Pool {\n    inner: Arc<PoolInner>,\n}\n\nimpl Pool {\n    /// Build a new empty pool. Buffers are allocated lazily by\n    /// [`Pool::get_msg`] when the free list is empty.\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            inner: Arc::new(PoolInner {\n                free: Mutex::new(Vec::new()),\n            }),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Get a [`PooledMsg`] of logical length `size` bytes.\n    /// Panics if `size > MAX_SIZE` — callers at system\n    /// boundaries (e.g. CLI parsers) are expected to validate\n    /// the requested size up-front.\n    ///\n    /// Pops from the free list if non-empty, otherwise\n    /// allocates a fresh `Vec<u8>` with `MAX_SIZE` capacity. In\n    /// either case the returned buffer's `len()` equals `size`;\n    /// for popped buffers, slots `0..size` retain whatever\n    /// bytes the previous occupant wrote (no zeroing — see\n    /// module docs).\n    pub fn get_msg(&self, size: usize) -> PooledMsg {\n        assert!(\n            size <= MAX_SIZE,\n            \"size {size} exceeds MAX_SIZE {MAX_SIZE}\"\n        );\n        let popped = {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: Mutex only poisoned by a panic while locked; the\n            //   pool's lock holders only push/pop, no panic path.\n            let mut free = self.inner.free.lock().unwrap();\n            free.pop()\n        };\n        let mut buf = popped.unwrap_or_else(|| Vec::with_capacity(MAX_SIZE)); // OK: empty pool → fresh MAX_SIZE-capacity allocation\n        buf.resize(size, 0);\n        PooledMsg {\n            buf: Some(buf),\n            pool: self.inner.clone(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Number of buffers currently sitting in the free list.\n    /// Useful for tests; not for production logic.\n    #[cfg(test)]\n    pub(crate) fn free_len(&self) -> usize {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: see Pool::get_msg for poisoning rationale.\n        self.inner.free.lock().unwrap().len()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for Pool {\n    /// Same as [`Pool::new`].\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self::new()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Owned byte buffer handed out by a [`Pool`]. Implements\n/// [`Deref`] / [`DerefMut`] over `[u8]` so handlers can take\n/// `&*msg` for an `&[u8]` view (or `&mut *msg` for `&mut [u8]`).\n/// On `Drop`, the buffer returns to its originating pool's\n/// free list rather than being freed.\npub struct PooledMsg {\n    buf: Option<Vec<u8>>,\n    pool: Arc<PoolInner>,\n}\n\nimpl Deref for PooledMsg {\n    type Target = [u8];\n    /// Borrow the buffer's contents as an `&[u8]` of length\n    /// `size` (the value passed to [`Pool::get_msg`]).\n    fn deref(&self) -> &[u8] {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: `buf` is only `None` between `Drop::drop`'s `take`\n        //   and the end of `drop`; `Deref` cannot run during that\n        //   window.\n        self.buf.as_ref().unwrap()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl DerefMut for PooledMsg {\n    /// Mutably borrow the buffer as `&mut [u8]`.\n    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut [u8] {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: see `Deref::deref`.\n        self.buf.as_mut().unwrap()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for PooledMsg {\n    /// Return the underlying `Vec<u8>` to the pool's free\n    /// list. The buffer's `len()` is whatever the occupant\n    /// last left it at; the next `get_msg(size)` consumer\n    /// resizes to its requested length.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        if let Some(buf) = self.buf.take() {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: see Pool::get_msg for poisoning rationale.\n            self.pool.free.lock().unwrap().push(buf);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// `get_msg(size)` returns a buffer of length `size`.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_returns_requested_size() {\n        let p = Pool::new();\n        let m = p.get_msg(16);\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), 16);\n    }\n\n    /// Boundary: `size == MAX_SIZE` is allowed.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_at_max_size_ok() {\n        let p = Pool::new();\n        let m = p.get_msg(MAX_SIZE);\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), MAX_SIZE);\n    }\n\n    /// Boundary: `size == 0` is allowed and yields an empty\n    /// slice. Cheap to allow; no reason to forbid.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_zero_size_ok() {\n        let p = Pool::new();\n        let m = p.get_msg(0);\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), 0);\n    }\n\n    /// `size > MAX_SIZE` panics (caller-side bug).\n    #[test]\n    #[should_panic(expected = \"exceeds MAX_SIZE\")]\n    fn get_msg_over_max_panics() {\n        let p = Pool::new();\n        let _ = p.get_msg(MAX_SIZE + 1);\n    }\n\n    /// Dropping a `PooledMsg` returns its buffer to the pool.\n    #[test]\n    fn drop_returns_buffer_to_pool() {\n        let p = Pool::new();\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 0);\n        let m = p.get_msg(8);\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 0);\n        drop(m);\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 1);\n    }\n\n    /// After drop+reacquire, the same backing allocation is\n    /// reused (pointer equality holds because `Vec::with_capacity`\n    /// + `resize` within capacity does not reallocate).\n    #[test]\n    fn buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get() {\n        let p = Pool::new();\n        let m1 = p.get_msg(8);\n        let ptr1 = m1.as_ptr();\n        drop(m1);\n        let m2 = p.get_msg(16);\n        assert_eq!(m2.as_ptr(), ptr1, \"expected reuse of the same allocation\");\n        assert_eq!(m2.len(), 16);\n    }\n\n    /// Cloned `Pool` handles share the same free list.\n    #[test]\n    fn clone_pool_shares_free_list() {\n        let p1 = Pool::new();\n        let p2 = p1.clone();\n        drop(p1.get_msg(8));\n        assert_eq!(p2.free_len(), 1);\n    }\n\n    /// Writes through `DerefMut` round-trip via `Deref`.\n    #[test]\n    fn writes_round_trip() {\n        let p = Pool::new();\n        let mut m = p.get_msg(4);\n        m.copy_from_slice(&[1, 2, 3, 4]);\n        assert_eq!(&*m, &[1, 2, 3, 4]);\n    }\n\n    /// Sharing a pool across threads is sound; buffers dropped\n    /// on a worker thread return to the same free list visible\n    /// from the main thread.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_works_across_threads() {\n        use std::thread;\n        let p = Pool::new();\n        let p2 = p.clone();\n        let h = thread::spawn(move || {\n            let m = p2.get_msg(8);\n            drop(m);\n        });\n        h.join().expect(\"worker thread panicked\");\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 1);\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "new_string": "- Multi-step `0.5.0` ladder.\n  - Five natural checkpoints: pool, runtime, bin, bench, close.\n  - Each reviewable on its own.\n  - A single-step `0.5.0` would be a large diff to review in one pass.\n\n## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)\n\nImplements the buffer pool that `RuntimeZC` will use to ship\nzerocopy messages between actors. New module is freestanding\n— no runtime / actor wiring yet; that lands in `0.5.0-2`.\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-0` → `0.5.0-1`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;` added before `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub const MAX_SIZE: usize = 64;` — fits a small header + a handful of `u64`s in one cache line; bumpable later if the workloads grow.\n  - `pub struct Pool` — `#[derive(Clone)]` `Arc<PoolInner>` handle.\n  - Internal `PoolInner { free: Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>> }` (private) — simple lock-protected free list; lock-free upgrade deferred until `goalzc` shows contention matters.\n  - `Pool::new()` / `Default` — empty pool; lazy allocation on first `get_msg`.\n  - `Pool::get_msg(size) -> PooledMsg` — pops the free list or allocates `Vec::with_capacity(MAX_SIZE)`; `resize(size, 0)` to set the logical length; panics on `size > MAX_SIZE` (caller's contract).\n  - `pub struct PooledMsg { buf: Option<Vec<u8>>, pool: Arc<PoolInner> }` — `Option` so `Drop` can `take()` the inner `Vec`.\n  - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` / `DerefMut` — handler view is `&*msg` / `&mut *msg`.\n  - `Drop` — pushes the inner `Vec` back onto the pool's free list.\n  - `Pool::free_len()` — `#[cfg(test)]` only; for free-list assertions in tests.\n\n### Tests added (all `#[cfg(test)]` in `pool.rs`)\n\n- `get_msg_returns_requested_size` — `len()` matches the requested size.\n- `get_msg_at_max_size_ok` — `size == MAX_SIZE` boundary works.\n- `get_msg_zero_size_ok` — `size == 0` yields an empty slice.\n- `get_msg_over_max_panics` — `#[should_panic]` on `size > MAX_SIZE`.\n- `drop_returns_buffer_to_pool` — free-list count goes 0 → 0 → 1 across get / drop.\n- `buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get` — pointer equality survives a get / drop / get cycle (capacity preserved, no realloc).\n- `clone_pool_shares_free_list` — drop on one `Pool` clone is visible from another.\n- `writes_round_trip` — `DerefMut` writes are visible through `Deref`.\n- `pool_works_across_threads` — `Pool` cloned to a worker thread; buffer dropped on worker returns to the shared free list.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- `Vec<u8>` rather than `Box<[u8]>` for the buffer.\n  - `Vec`'s `len`/`capacity` split is exactly the \"logical size ≤ MAX_SIZE\" shape we need.\n  - `Vec::resize` within capacity does not reallocate, so the get / drop / get cycle preserves the allocation.\n  - `Box<[u8]>` would force a fixed length and either an extra wrapper `(Box<[u8]>, usize)` or pad-and-truncate semantics — more plumbing for no win.\n- `Option<Vec<u8>>` inside `PooledMsg` so `Drop` can move the inner `Vec` out.\n  - The `Option` is only `None` during the brief window inside `Drop::drop`; `Deref`/`DerefMut` cannot observe it.\n  - `unsafe` alternatives (`ManuallyDrop` + a destructor) would shave the `unwrap`s but add unsafety; not worth it for a small struct.\n- No zeroing on drop.\n  - Slots `0..new_size` of a reused buffer carry residual bytes from the previous occupant.\n  - For ping-pong workloads the next consumer either overwrites or ignores those bytes — benign.\n  - Real applications crossing trust domains would need an explicit `zeroize`-on-drop step; documented in the module-level comment.\n- `assert!` on `size > MAX_SIZE`, not `Result`.\n  - Internal API; callers at system boundaries (CLI parsers) are expected to validate up-front.\n  - The `goalzc` binary will validate `--size` against `MAX_SIZE` before reaching `get_msg`.\n  - Panic-on-misuse is the right shape for a fast hot-path API where `Result` would force every call site to handle the impossible case.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"
}

> DEVELOPER

How do I run the pool tests

> AGENT

From the workspace root (or anywhere inside it):

```
cargo test -p actor-x1 --lib pool::
```

The `pool::` substring filters to anything under the `pool::tests` module — shows the 9 new tests and skips the 7 in `runtime::`. Drop the `pool::` to run all 16. Append a specific name (e.g. `pool::tests::drop_returns_buffer_to_pool`) to run just one.

Add `-- --nocapture` after the filter to see `println!` output if you add any while debugging.

> DEVELOPER

I want get_msg to return a Result with SIZE_TO_LARGE or NO_MSGS errors and Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32) -> Self

> AGENT

Got it — fixed-capacity preallocated pool with explicit error types. Before I rewrite, two small naming/shape calls I'll make unless you say otherwise:

- **Variant naming.** Rust idiom is CamelCase, so `PoolError::SizeTooLarge { requested, max }` and `PoolError::NoMsgs`. (Your `SIZE_TO_LARGE`/`NO_MSGS` reads more like C-style constants.)
- **`get_msg(size: usize)`.** Keep the `size` arg as `usize` for clean interop with `&[u8]` / `Vec` lengths, even though `Pool::new` takes `u32`. Internally compared to `msg_size as usize`.
- **Drop `MAX_SIZE` const.** It's superseded by the per-pool `msg_size`. Adds a `pool.msg_size() -> u32` getter so `RuntimeZC` / `goalzc` can validate up front.
- **Drop `Default for Pool`.** No sensible defaults for size/count.

Going to rewrite `pool.rs`, update tests, refresh the `0.5.0-1` chores section to describe the actual shipped API (and note the deviation from the `0.5.0-0` plan). Start now?

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs",
  "content": "//! Fixed-capacity buffer pool for `RuntimeZC` zerocopy messages.\n//!\n//! A [`Pool`] is configured at construction with `msg_size` (the\n//! maximum byte length of any buffer it hands out) and\n//! `msg_count` (the number of buffers it pre-allocates). Its\n//! free list never grows beyond `msg_count`: once all buffers\n//! are in flight, [`Pool::get_msg`] returns\n//! [`PoolError::NoMsgs`]. Requests for a size greater than the\n//! pool's `msg_size` return [`PoolError::SizeTooLarge`]. Buffers\n//! return to the free list when their owning [`PooledMsg`] is\n//! dropped.\n//!\n//! Storage is `Arc<Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>>>` — simple, some lock\n//! contention under multi-thread load. A lock-free upgrade\n//! (e.g. `crossbeam_queue::ArrayQueue`) is deferred until\n//! `goalzc` shows the contention cost matters.\n//!\n//! No zeroing happens on drop. A reused buffer's bytes\n//! `0..new_size` may contain residual data from the previous\n//! occupant; ping-pong handlers either overwrite or ignore\n//! those bytes, so the leak is benign for the workloads this\n//! crate measures. Real applications that ship pooled messages\n//! between trust domains would need an explicit zeroize step.\n\nuse std::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};\nuse std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};\n\n/// Failure modes for [`Pool::get_msg`].\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum PoolError {\n    /// The requested size exceeds the pool's configured\n    /// `msg_size`. `requested` is what the caller asked for;\n    /// `max` is the pool's `msg_size`.\n    SizeTooLarge { requested: usize, max: usize },\n    /// Every buffer the pool owns is currently in flight as a\n    /// live [`PooledMsg`]. Caller must wait for a drop or\n    /// increase `msg_count` when constructing the pool.\n    NoMsgs,\n}\n\nimpl std::fmt::Display for PoolError {\n    /// Render a human-readable one-liner for logs / panics.\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            PoolError::SizeTooLarge { requested, max } => {\n                write!(f, \"requested size {requested} exceeds pool msg_size {max}\")\n            }\n            PoolError::NoMsgs => f.write_str(\"pool exhausted: all buffers are in flight\"),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl std::error::Error for PoolError {}\n\n/// Internal pool storage; held by `Arc` so actor threads share\n/// one free list. Keep this private — callers see only [`Pool`]\n/// and [`PooledMsg`].\nstruct PoolInner {\n    msg_size: usize,\n    free: Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>>,\n}\n\n/// Cheaply-cloneable handle to a shared, fixed-capacity buffer\n/// pool. Clones share the same free list (internally `Arc`).\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct Pool {\n    inner: Arc<PoolInner>,\n}\n\nimpl Pool {\n    /// Build a pool that pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers,\n    /// each with capacity `msg_size` bytes. Both are `u32` so\n    /// the pool's total footprint is bounded at the type\n    /// level at `u32::MAX * u32::MAX` bytes, well beyond any\n    /// realistic usage.\n    ///\n    /// `msg_count == 0` is allowed — the pool immediately\n    /// returns [`PoolError::NoMsgs`] on every `get_msg` call,\n    /// useful for exhaustion tests.\n    pub fn new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32) -> Self {\n        let msg_size = msg_size as usize;\n        let msg_count = msg_count as usize;\n        let mut free = Vec::with_capacity(msg_count);\n        for _ in 0..msg_count {\n            free.push(Vec::with_capacity(msg_size));\n        }\n        Self {\n            inner: Arc::new(PoolInner {\n                msg_size,\n                free: Mutex::new(free),\n            }),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// The pool's configured maximum message size in bytes.\n    /// Callers use this to validate requests up-front or size\n    /// their outbound messages.\n    pub fn msg_size(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.inner.msg_size as u32\n    }\n\n    /// Take a buffer of logical length `size` from the pool.\n    /// Returns [`PoolError::SizeTooLarge`] if\n    /// `size > msg_size`, or [`PoolError::NoMsgs`] if every\n    /// buffer is currently in flight.\n    ///\n    /// Popped buffers retain whatever bytes the previous\n    /// occupant wrote (no zeroing — see module docs); the\n    /// slice's logical length is exactly `size`.\n    pub fn get_msg(&self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg, PoolError> {\n        if size > self.inner.msg_size {\n            return Err(PoolError::SizeTooLarge {\n                requested: size,\n                max: self.inner.msg_size,\n            });\n        }\n        let popped = {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: Mutex only poisoned by a panic while locked; the\n            //   pool's lock holders only push/pop, no panic path.\n            let mut free = self.inner.free.lock().unwrap();\n            free.pop()\n        };\n        let mut buf = popped.ok_or(PoolError::NoMsgs)?;\n        buf.resize(size, 0);\n        Ok(PooledMsg {\n            buf: Some(buf),\n            pool: self.inner.clone(),\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Number of buffers currently sitting in the free list.\n    /// Useful for tests; not for production logic.\n    #[cfg(test)]\n    pub(crate) fn free_len(&self) -> usize {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: see Pool::get_msg for poisoning rationale.\n        self.inner.free.lock().unwrap().len()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Owned byte buffer handed out by a [`Pool`]. Implements\n/// [`Deref`] / [`DerefMut`] over `[u8]` so handlers can take\n/// `&*msg` for an `&[u8]` view (or `&mut *msg` for `&mut [u8]`).\n/// On `Drop`, the buffer returns to its originating pool's\n/// free list rather than being freed.\npub struct PooledMsg {\n    buf: Option<Vec<u8>>,\n    pool: Arc<PoolInner>,\n}\n\nimpl Deref for PooledMsg {\n    type Target = [u8];\n    /// Borrow the buffer's contents as an `&[u8]` of length\n    /// `size` (the value passed to [`Pool::get_msg`]).\n    fn deref(&self) -> &[u8] {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: `buf` is only `None` between `Drop::drop`'s `take`\n        //   and the end of `drop`; `Deref` cannot run during that\n        //   window.\n        self.buf.as_ref().unwrap()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl DerefMut for PooledMsg {\n    /// Mutably borrow the buffer as `&mut [u8]`.\n    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut [u8] {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: see `Deref::deref`.\n        self.buf.as_mut().unwrap()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for PooledMsg {\n    /// Return the underlying `Vec<u8>` to the pool's free\n    /// list. The buffer's `len()` is whatever the occupant\n    /// last left it at; the next `get_msg(size)` consumer\n    /// resizes to its requested length.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        if let Some(buf) = self.buf.take() {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: see Pool::get_msg for poisoning rationale.\n            self.pool.free.lock().unwrap().push(buf);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// A freshly-built pool reports its `msg_size` and has\n    /// exactly `msg_count` buffers on the free list.\n    #[test]\n    fn new_preallocates_and_exposes_msg_size() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        assert_eq!(p.msg_size(), 64);\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 4);\n    }\n\n    /// `get_msg(size)` returns a buffer of logical length `size`.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_returns_requested_size() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 2);\n        let m = p.get_msg(16).expect(\"get_msg should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), 16);\n    }\n\n    /// Boundary: `size == msg_size` is allowed.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_at_msg_size_ok() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let m = p.get_msg(64).expect(\"get_msg should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), 64);\n    }\n\n    /// Boundary: `size == 0` is allowed and yields an empty\n    /// slice. Cheap to allow; no reason to forbid.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_zero_size_ok() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let m = p.get_msg(0).expect(\"get_msg should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), 0);\n    }\n\n    /// `size > msg_size` yields `SizeTooLarge` rather than\n    /// panicking. Caller sees both the requested and max values.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let err = p.get_msg(65).expect_err(\"expected SizeTooLarge\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            err,\n            PoolError::SizeTooLarge {\n                requested: 65,\n                max: 64\n            }\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Once all `msg_count` buffers are in flight, `get_msg`\n    /// returns `NoMsgs`.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 2);\n        let _a = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"first get_msg\");\n        let _b = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"second get_msg\");\n        assert_eq!(p.get_msg(8), Err(PoolError::NoMsgs));\n    }\n\n    /// A zero-capacity pool always returns `NoMsgs`.\n    #[test]\n    fn zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.get_msg(8), Err(PoolError::NoMsgs));\n    }\n\n    /// After dropping a live `PooledMsg`, the pool recovers a\n    /// slot and `get_msg` succeeds again.\n    #[test]\n    fn drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let m = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"first get_msg\");\n        assert_eq!(p.get_msg(8), Err(PoolError::NoMsgs));\n        drop(m);\n        let _m2 = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"post-drop get_msg\");\n    }\n\n    /// The same backing allocation is reused across a\n    /// get / drop / get cycle. Pointer equality holds because\n    /// `Vec::with_capacity(msg_size)` + `resize` within capacity\n    /// does not reallocate.\n    #[test]\n    fn buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let m1 = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"first get_msg\");\n        let ptr1 = m1.as_ptr();\n        drop(m1);\n        let m2 = p.get_msg(16).expect(\"second get_msg\");\n        assert_eq!(m2.as_ptr(), ptr1, \"expected reuse of the same allocation\");\n        assert_eq!(m2.len(), 16);\n    }\n\n    /// Cloned `Pool` handles share the same free list.\n    #[test]\n    fn clone_pool_shares_free_list() {\n        let p1 = Pool::new(64, 2);\n        let p2 = p1.clone();\n        let m = p1.get_msg(8).expect(\"get_msg\");\n        assert_eq!(p2.free_len(), 1);\n        drop(m);\n        assert_eq!(p2.free_len(), 2);\n    }\n\n    /// Writes through `DerefMut` round-trip via `Deref`.\n    #[test]\n    fn writes_round_trip() {\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let mut m = p.get_msg(4).expect(\"get_msg\");\n        m.copy_from_slice(&[1, 2, 3, 4]);\n        assert_eq!(&*m, &[1, 2, 3, 4]);\n    }\n\n    /// Sharing a pool across threads is sound; buffers dropped\n    /// on a worker thread return to the same free list visible\n    /// from the main thread.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_works_across_threads() {\n        use std::thread;\n        let p = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let p2 = p.clone();\n        let h = thread::spawn(move || {\n            let m = p2.get_msg(8).expect(\"worker get_msg\");\n            drop(m);\n        });\n        h.join().expect(\"worker thread panicked\");\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 1);\n    }\n\n    /// `PoolError` renders a readable `Display` string for\n    /// both variants.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_error_display() {\n        let e1 = PoolError::SizeTooLarge {\n            requested: 128,\n            max: 64,\n        };\n        assert_eq!(\n            e1.to_string(),\n            \"requested size 128 exceeds pool msg_size 64\"\n        );\n        let e2 = PoolError::NoMsgs;\n        assert_eq!(e2.to_string(), \"pool exhausted: all buffers are in flight\");\n    }\n}\n"
}

{

  "old_string": "## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)\n\nImplements the buffer pool that `RuntimeZC` will use to ship\nzerocopy messages between actors. New module is freestanding\n— no runtime / actor wiring yet; that lands in `0.5.0-2`.\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-0` → `0.5.0-1`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;` added before `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub const MAX_SIZE: usize = 64;` — fits a small header + a handful of `u64`s in one cache line; bumpable later if the workloads grow.\n  - `pub struct Pool` — `#[derive(Clone)]` `Arc<PoolInner>` handle.\n  - Internal `PoolInner { free: Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>> }` (private) — simple lock-protected free list; lock-free upgrade deferred until `goalzc` shows contention matters.\n  - `Pool::new()` / `Default` — empty pool; lazy allocation on first `get_msg`.\n  - `Pool::get_msg(size) -> PooledMsg` — pops the free list or allocates `Vec::with_capacity(MAX_SIZE)`; `resize(size, 0)` to set the logical length; panics on `size > MAX_SIZE` (caller's contract).\n  - `pub struct PooledMsg { buf: Option<Vec<u8>>, pool: Arc<PoolInner> }` — `Option` so `Drop` can `take()` the inner `Vec`.\n  - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` / `DerefMut` — handler view is `&*msg` / `&mut *msg`.\n  - `Drop` — pushes the inner `Vec` back onto the pool's free list.\n  - `Pool::free_len()` — `#[cfg(test)]` only; for free-list assertions in tests.\n\n### Tests added (all `#[cfg(test)]` in `pool.rs`)\n\n- `get_msg_returns_requested_size` — `len()` matches the requested size.\n- `get_msg_at_max_size_ok` — `size == MAX_SIZE` boundary works.\n- `get_msg_zero_size_ok` — `size == 0` yields an empty slice.\n- `get_msg_over_max_panics` — `#[should_panic]` on `size > MAX_SIZE`.\n- `drop_returns_buffer_to_pool` — free-list count goes 0 → 0 → 1 across get / drop.\n- `buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get` — pointer equality survives a get / drop / get cycle (capacity preserved, no realloc).\n- `clone_pool_shares_free_list` — drop on one `Pool` clone is visible from another.\n- `writes_round_trip` — `DerefMut` writes are visible through `Deref`.\n- `pool_works_across_threads` — `Pool` cloned to a worker thread; buffer dropped on worker returns to the shared free list.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- `Vec<u8>` rather than `Box<[u8]>` for the buffer.\n  - `Vec`'s `len`/`capacity` split is exactly the \"logical size ≤ MAX_SIZE\" shape we need.\n  - `Vec::resize` within capacity does not reallocate, so the get / drop / get cycle preserves the allocation.\n  - `Box<[u8]>` would force a fixed length and either an extra wrapper `(Box<[u8]>, usize)` or pad-and-truncate semantics — more plumbing for no win.\n- `Option<Vec<u8>>` inside `PooledMsg` so `Drop` can move the inner `Vec` out.\n  - The `Option` is only `None` during the brief window inside `Drop::drop`; `Deref`/`DerefMut` cannot observe it.\n  - `unsafe` alternatives (`ManuallyDrop` + a destructor) would shave the `unwrap`s but add unsafety; not worth it for a small struct.\n- No zeroing on drop.\n  - Slots `0..new_size` of a reused buffer carry residual bytes from the previous occupant.\n  - For ping-pong workloads the next consumer either overwrites or ignores those bytes — benign.\n  - Real applications crossing trust domains would need an explicit `zeroize`-on-drop step; documented in the module-level comment.\n- `assert!` on `size > MAX_SIZE`, not `Result`.\n  - Internal API; callers at system boundaries (CLI parsers) are expected to validate up-front.\n  - The `goalzc` binary will validate `--size` against `MAX_SIZE` before reaching `get_msg`.\n  - Panic-on-misuse is the right shape for a fast hot-path API where `Result` would force every call site to handle the impossible case.",
  "new_string": "## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)\n\nImplements the fixed-capacity buffer pool that `RuntimeZC`\nwill use to ship zerocopy messages between actors. The API\ndeviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in two ways, both decided\nduring review of the first cut:\n\n- Constructor takes explicit size / count: `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)` — pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of capacity `msg_size`, rather than starting empty and growing lazily.\n- `get_msg` returns `Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` rather than panicking — the two failure modes (oversized request, exhaustion) are normal runtime conditions for a fixed-capacity pool and callers are expected to handle them.\n\nThe plan-marker entry (`0.5.0-0`) described a lazy,\nMAX_SIZE-constant pool that the `get_msg` panicked on misuse;\nthat shape is superseded here.\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-0` → `0.5.0-1`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;` added before `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub enum PoolError` — two variants:\n    - `SizeTooLarge { requested: usize, max: usize }` — `size > msg_size`.\n    - `NoMsgs` — free list empty; every buffer is in flight.\n    - `impl Display` / `impl std::error::Error` so callers can bubble it through `?` on a `Box<dyn Error>` or print it in logs.\n  - `pub struct Pool` — `#[derive(Clone)]` `Arc<PoolInner>` handle.\n    - Internal `PoolInner { msg_size: usize, free: Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>> }` (private).\n    - `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)` — pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of `Vec::with_capacity(msg_size)`.\n    - `Pool::msg_size() -> u32` — exposes the configured max for callers who need to validate up front.\n    - `Pool::get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` — pops one buffer from the free list; `resize(size, 0)` for logical length; errors as above.\n    - `Pool::free_len() -> usize` — `#[cfg(test)]` only.\n  - `pub struct PooledMsg { buf: Option<Vec<u8>>, pool: Arc<PoolInner> }` — `Option` so `Drop` can `take()` the inner `Vec`.\n    - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` / `DerefMut` — handler view is `&*msg` / `&mut *msg`.\n    - `Drop` — pushes the inner `Vec` back onto the pool's free list.\n\n### Tests added (all `#[cfg(test)]` in `pool.rs`, 13 total)\n\n- `new_preallocates_and_exposes_msg_size` — fresh pool reports `msg_size` and has `msg_count` buffers ready.\n- `get_msg_returns_requested_size` — `len()` matches the requested size.\n- `get_msg_at_msg_size_ok` — `size == msg_size` boundary works.\n- `get_msg_zero_size_ok` — `size == 0` yields an empty slice.\n- `get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large` — errors with both `requested` and `max` set correctly.\n- `get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs` — after all slots are in flight, `get_msg` yields `NoMsgs`.\n- `zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs` — `Pool::new(_, 0)` always errors; sanity check for the zero edge.\n- `drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds` — dropping an in-flight msg re-opens a slot.\n- `buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get` — pointer equality survives a get / drop / get cycle (capacity preserved, no realloc).\n- `clone_pool_shares_free_list` — drop on one `Pool` clone is visible from another.\n- `writes_round_trip` — `DerefMut` writes are visible through `Deref`.\n- `pool_works_across_threads` — `Pool` cloned to a worker thread; buffer dropped on worker returns to the shared free list.\n- `pool_error_display` — both variants render readable one-liners.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- Fixed-capacity, pre-allocated pool rather than lazy growth.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's \"starts empty, grows on demand\" design.\n  - Pre-allocation makes `NoMsgs` a real, testable condition — the runtime's back-pressure story has to face exhaustion, so surfacing it directly in the pool is the honest shape.\n  - Steady-state behavior for ping-pong is unchanged (two buffers cycling) — the difference is that a burst of concurrent `get_msg` calls returns `NoMsgs` instead of silently allocating beyond the configured budget.\n- `Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` rather than panic on misuse.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's `panic!` on `size > MAX_SIZE`.\n  - `NoMsgs` is runtime-variable — a benchmark or application hitting exhaustion should not crash; it needs to apply back-pressure or report the saturation.\n  - Making the size error the same shape keeps one error path.\n  - `goalzc`'s CLI parser will still validate `--size <= msg_size()` up front so the `SizeTooLarge` variant never surfaces from the hot path; the variant exists for API safety, not for runtime handling in the ping-pong case.\n- `msg_size` / `msg_count` typed as `u32`.\n  - Matches the user's requested shape: explicit byte-range and count limits at the type level.\n  - Internally converted to `usize` for `Vec` / slice APIs; the conversion is widening on every platform actor-x1 targets.\n  - `get_msg(size: usize)` stays `usize` for clean interop with slice/`Vec` lengths; if `size > u32::MAX` the `SizeTooLarge` guard catches it.\n- `Vec<u8>` rather than `Box<[u8]>` for the buffer.\n  - `Vec::resize` within capacity does not reallocate, so the get / drop / get cycle preserves the same allocation.\n  - `Box<[u8]>` would force a fixed length and either a `(Box<[u8]>, usize)` wrapper or pad-and-truncate semantics — more plumbing for no win.\n- `Option<Vec<u8>>` inside `PooledMsg` so `Drop` can move the inner `Vec` out.\n  - The `Option` is only `None` during the brief window inside `Drop::drop`; `Deref`/`DerefMut` cannot observe it.\n  - `unsafe` alternatives (`ManuallyDrop` + a destructor) would shave the `unwrap`s but add unsafety; not worth it for a small struct.\n- No zeroing on drop.\n  - Slots `0..new_size` of a reused buffer carry residual bytes from the previous occupant.\n  - For ping-pong workloads the next consumer either overwrites or ignores those bytes — benign.\n  - Real applications crossing trust domains would need an explicit `zeroize`-on-drop step; documented in the module-level comment.\n- No `Default for Pool`.\n  - There's no sensible default for `msg_size` / `msg_count`; every caller has to choose.\n  - Dropped from the first cut where `Pool::new()` took no args."

> AGENT

Pool redesign done. Changes relative to the earlier `0.5.0-1` cut you reviewed:

- **`Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)`** — pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of `Vec::with_capacity(msg_size as usize)`. No lazy growth.
- **`Pool::msg_size() -> u32`** — getter for the configured max.
- **`Pool::get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>`** — returns `Err(SizeTooLarge { requested, max })` when `size > msg_size`, `Err(NoMsgs)` when every buffer is in flight.
- **`PoolError`** — CamelCase variants, `Display` + `std::error::Error` impls so callers can `?` it through.
- **`MAX_SIZE` const removed** — superseded by per-pool `msg_size`.
- **`Default for Pool` removed** — no sensible default.
- **Tests: 13 total** (was 9). New: `new_preallocates_and_exposes_msg_size`, `get_msg_at_msg_size_ok`, `get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large`, `get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs`, `zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs`, `drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds`, `pool_error_display`. Dropped: `get_msg_over_max_panics`, `drop_returns_buffer_to_pool` (subsumed by `drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds`).
- **`notes/chores-01.md`** — `## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)` section rewritten with the new API + a leading note that the shape deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan and why.

Full workspace: 20 actor-x1 lib tests + 18 tprobe lib tests passing; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean.

> DEVELOPER

I'm confused get_msg is returning:
        Ok(PooledMsg {
            buf: Some(buf),
            pool: self.inner.clone(),
        })


I'm expecting it to return something like:
Ok(&mut [u8])

that can then be the "memory" for some zerocopy message.

> AGENT

`PooledMsg` *is* the `&mut [u8]` — `Deref<Target = [u8]>` + `DerefMut` mean it transparently acts as one. The reason it's a wrapper rather than a bare `&mut [u8]`: a bare slice has no destructor, so dropping it can't return the buffer to the pool. Something has to own the buffer between `get_msg` and the eventual `Drop` that recycles it; that "something" is `PooledMsg`.

In practice, callers use it exactly like `&mut [u8]`:

```rust
let mut msg = pool.get_msg(64)?;           // PooledMsg
msg[0] = 0x42;                              // indexes via DerefMut
msg.copy_from_slice(&payload);              // slice methods work directly
let view: &mut [u8] = &mut msg;             // explicit reborrow if a func wants &mut [u8]

// zerocopy interop — direct, no copy:
let hdr: &mut MyHeader = MyHeader::mut_from_prefix(&mut msg)
    .expect("msg ≥ size_of::<MyHeader>()");
hdr.seq = 7;

// Send it. The buffer travels with the PooledMsg; on the receiving
// actor, its Drop after handle_message returns puts the buffer back
// in the pool.
ctx.send(peer_id, msg);
```

So there's no extra indirection at runtime — `&mut *msg` compiles to the same load as a bare `&mut [u8]` would, and zerocopy's `*_from_prefix` / `*_from_bytes` methods take `&mut [u8]` so they accept `&mut msg` directly via the auto-deref-coercion.

If you'd rather have an explicit `pool.get_msg(...).map(|m| m.into_bytes())` style or some other surface, I can reshape — but the underlying ownership has to live somewhere. Does the Deref pattern work for you, or are you after a different shape?

> DEVELOPER

The egronomics look good, and I now see pub struct PooledMsg and I think the "self.inner.clone()" is incrementing the Arc and buf, I hope is a,  &mut [u8]. Since these are fixed size arrays. But overall it feels odd that to use  Vec<u8> as we know their size and it can't be changed?

> AGENT

Three things to unpack:

1. **`self.inner.clone()`** — yes, that's an `Arc::clone`, which is just an atomic refcount increment (Relaxed ordering). It's the cheapest "back reference" PooledMsg can hold so its `Drop` can find the pool. The buffer payload doesn't get cloned.

2. **"`buf, I hope is a &mut [u8]`"** — it can't be, mechanically. A `&mut [u8]` is a borrow with a lifetime; the buffer needs an *owner* whose `Drop` returns it to the pool. `PooledMsg` *is* that owner. The `&mut [u8]` view comes out via `&mut *pooled` (free, just a `Deref` call → field access). No runtime cost, no extra copy — same as if `buf` were the slice directly.

3. **"feels odd to use `Vec<u8>` when size is fixed"** — fair criticism, and you're right. A `Vec<u8>` carries `(ptr, len, cap)` but our `cap` is always `msg_size` and never changes; the field is dead weight (8 bytes/buffer). And `Vec` *implies* growable to a reader, which is misleading here.

   Cleaner shape:

   ```rust
   struct PoolInner {
       msg_size: usize,
       free: Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>,   // Vec for the free list (does grow/shrink)
                                       //   wrapping Box<[u8]> for each buffer (fixed size)
   }

   pub struct PooledMsg {
       buf: Option<Box<[u8]>>,         // fixed `msg_size` bytes, never resized
       len: usize,                     // logical length, ≤ msg_size
       pool: Arc<PoolInner>,
   }

   impl Deref for PooledMsg {
       type Target = [u8];
       fn deref(&self) -> &[u8] { &self.buf.as_ref().unwrap()[..self.len] }
   }
   ```

   - Communicates "this allocation has a fixed size, full stop" via the type.
   - Saves the redundant `cap` field per buffer.
   - `get_msg` sets `len = size` instead of doing a `Vec::resize` (no zeroing of the tail bytes either — slightly faster get path).
   - Pool init: `vec![0u8; msg_size].into_boxed_slice()` per slot, once.

Want me to make that change to `pool.rs`?

That feels better, just to be clear PoolInner.free isa atomic stack (LIFO) that should be preallocated to hold msg_count buffer pointers and it should never grow or shrink, thus Vec seems overkill. Also, PooledMsg.buf being an Option<Box<[u8]>> I hope is usize NULL or non-null and infact PooledMsg only exists when buf is non-null so Option doesn't seem necessary. Showing my ignorance of Rust but trying to learn.

> AGENT

Both instincts are sharp. Two things to clear up:

### On `Vec<Box<[u8]>>` for the free list

Your instinct is right — the `Vec`'s `cap` field is redundant when capacity is fixed at `msg_count`. On the practical side: `Vec::with_capacity(msg_count)` allocates once at construction, and since we only ever `push` after a corresponding `pop`, the len stays `≤ msg_count` and the backing allocation never gets grown or reallocated. So it *behaves* as a fixed-cap stack; the type just doesn't enforce it.

Clarifying question before I pick: when you say **"atomic stack"** — do you mean

- (a) "thread-safe access, LIFO order" (what `Mutex<Vec<...>>` gives you today — the MVP), or
- (b) **lock-free** atomics (no `Mutex`, true wait-free push/pop)?

If (b), the right shape is a dep on `crossbeam_queue::ArrayQueue<Box<[u8]>>` (bounded, lock-free, uses CAS loops internally) — removes the Mutex entirely. Adds one small dep. If (a), `Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>` stays, and the "never grows" invariant gets a comment. My MVP lean is still (a) until we measure contention, but it's your call.

A truly hand-rolled fixed-cap lock-free LIFO exists but is significantly more code and easy to get wrong; not worth it against a proven crate if you want (b).

### On `Option<Box<[u8]>>` — your intuition is actually right at the bit level

This is one of those Rust details worth internalizing: `Option<Box<T>>` **is** "usize null-or-non-null" at the machine level. `Box<T>` is guaranteed to be a non-null pointer, and the compiler applies **niche optimization** — it represents `None` as the all-zero (null) bit pattern and `Some(box)` as the box's address. `size_of::<Option<Box<[u8]>>>() == size_of::<Box<[u8]>>()` — no discriminant tag, no overhead.

So on the wire it's exactly what you described. The `Option` wrapper exists for language reasons, not runtime reasons:

- `Drop::drop(&mut self)` receives `&mut self`, not `self` — you can't move fields out of `self` by destructuring.
- To move the `Box<[u8]>` out so we can push it to the pool, we need to leave `self.buf` in *some* valid state briefly.
- `Option::take()` is the idiomatic safe way to do this: swap `None` in, get `Some(box)` out, push the box to the pool. The `None` state is only observable during the ~5 instructions of `Drop::drop`; `Deref` / `DerefMut` can't see it (they need `&self`, and `Drop` has exclusive access).

Alternatives:
- `ManuallyDrop<Box<[u8]>>` + `ptr::read` — avoids the Option but requires `unsafe`.
- Leaking the box and rebuilding — wastes the allocation (defeats the pool).

So "Option isn't necessary because PooledMsg only exists when buf is non-null" is *almost* right — the exception is the sub-microsecond window inside `Drop::drop`, and the Option is how Rust expresses that window safely. The runtime representation is identical to a raw non-null pointer.

Your call on **(a)** vs **(b)** above, and I'll make the `Vec<u8>` → `Box<[u8]>` + `len` change.

> AGENT

Both good calls. Here's what I'd propose; still no code changes yet.

### `BufRef` alias

- Add at the top of `pool.rs`:
  ```rust
  /// Nullable buffer handle. Concretely `Option<Box<[u8]>>`, which
  /// thanks to Rust's niche optimization occupies exactly one
  /// pointer at runtime — `None` is the null bit pattern, `Some(box)`
  /// is the non-null heap address of a fixed-size byte allocation.
  /// Used wherever the code needs to express "a buffer, or nothing".
  pub type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;
  ```
- Used in two places:
  - `PooledMsg.buf: BufRef` — nullable during `Drop::drop`.
  - `BufStack::pop(&self) -> BufRef` — `None` if empty.

### Swappable "atomic stack" — `BufStack` trait

- New trait in `pool.rs`:
  ```rust
  pub trait BufStack: Send + Sync {
      fn from_buffers(buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>>) -> Self where Self: Sized;
      fn pop(&self) -> BufRef;
      fn push(&self, buf: Box<[u8]>);
      fn len(&self) -> usize;
  }
  ```
- Initial impl: `MutexStack` — `Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>`, equivalent to today's inline design but behind the trait.
- Future impls slot in without further refactoring:
  - `ArrayQueueStack` (wrapping `crossbeam_queue::ArrayQueue` — bounded, lock-free, FIFO-but-order-doesn't-matter-for-pools).
  - `RingBufferStack` (whatever shape we want to A/B).
  - Hand-rolled atomic-LIFO if we get that far.
- `Pool<S: BufStack = MutexStack>` — generic parameter with a default so ordinary callers write `Pool::new(64, 128)` unchanged; benchmarks swap via `Pool::<ArrayQueueStack>::new(64, 128)`.
- `PooledMsg<S: BufStack = MutexStack>` — also generic (it holds `Arc<PoolInner<S>>`). Default keeps most call sites clean.

### Scope question before I code

Adding the trait + one `MutexStack` impl + generic-ifying `Pool` / `PooledMsg` is a ~100-line expansion of `0.5.0-1`. Two options:

- **A.** Land it all in `0.5.0-1` — `Box<[u8]> + len` refactor *and* the `BufStack` trait + `MutexStack` impl, together. Bigger diff, single review, abstraction locked in now.
- **B.** Split: `0.5.0-1` stays tight (just the `Box<[u8]> + len` refactor); add a new `0.5.0-1b` or `0.5.0-2-pre` step introducing the trait + impl swap. Smaller reviews, slightly more churn.

My lean: **A.** The trait is small and the refactor points for `Pool<S>` / `PooledMsg<S>` touch the same file either way; splitting mostly shifts where the boilerplate lands.

Also: initial commit adds **one** impl (`MutexStack`). Subsequent impls (`ArrayQueueStack`, etc.) each land as their own small step once we want to benchmark against them — no need to ship a bunch of backends up front.

OK on the `BufRef` alias, the trait shape, and option A? Or redirect?

> DEVELOPER

(A.) is fine but I in actual fact we shouldn't assume a stack maybe  BufRefQueue or BufRefStore with two fns get()/ret(). These  can be implemented however the trait wants to

> AGENT

Agreed — "stack" was too specific. Proposed naming:

### Trait

- **`BufRefStore`** — thread-safe store of buffer refs; ordering is implementation-defined (LIFO, FIFO, ring-buffer, etc.).
  ```rust
  pub trait BufRefStore: Send + Sync {
      fn from_buffers(buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>>) -> Self where Self: Sized;
      fn get(&self) -> BufRef;          // None if empty
      fn ret(&self, buf: Box<[u8]>);    // precondition: ret ≤ get + capacity
      fn len(&self) -> usize;
  }
  ```

### Initial impl

- **`MutexLifo`** — `Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>`; `get` = `pop()`, `ret` = `push()`; LIFO by construction.
  - The name says both "how it's synchronized" (Mutex) and "what order you get" (LIFO).
  - Keeps room for future siblings: `MutexFifo` (VecDeque), `ArrayQueueFifo` (crossbeam), `RingBuffer`, etc.

### Pool / PooledMsg

- `Pool<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — default keeps ordinary call sites as `Pool::new(64, 128)`.
- `PooledMsg<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — same story; most code doesn't mention `S`.
- Benchmarks swap backends via `Pool::<ArrayQueueFifo>::new(...)` etc.

### Confirming scope for option A in `0.5.0-1`

- `Vec<u8>` → `Box<[u8]> + len` refactor of `PooledMsg`.
- `type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;` with the niche-optimization comment.
- `trait BufRefStore` + `impl BufRefStore for MutexLifo`.
- `Pool<S>` / `PooledMsg<S>` generic-ified with `MutexLifo` as the default.
- Tests stay on the default `Pool` (which is `Pool<MutexLifo>` implicitly).
- Chores section for `0.5.0-1` rewritten to describe this shape (flagging the further deviation from the `0.5.0-0` plan as a sub-bullet).

OK to proceed?

The\n//! pool's free list is held in a [`BufRefStore`] implementation;\n//! swapping implementations is how this crate will benchmark\n//! different concurrency strategies (Mutex, lock-free queue,\n//! ring buffer, hand-rolled atomics, …).
