Concretely `Option<Box<[u8]>>`, which\n/// thanks to Rust's niche optimization occupies exactly one\n/// pointer at runtime — `None` is the null bit pattern,\n/// `Some(box)` is the non-null heap address of a fixed-size\n/// byte allocation. Used wherever the code needs to express\n/// \"a buffer, or nothing\":\n/// - inside [`PooledMsg`] so [`Drop::drop`] can [`Option::take`]\n///   the box out without leaving `self` in an invalid state;\n/// - as [`BufRefStore::get`]'s return value so stores can\n///   signal \"empty\".\npub type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;\n\n/// Failure modes for [`Pool::get_msg`].\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum PoolError {\n    /// The requested size exceeds the pool's configured\n    /// `msg_size`. `requested` is what the caller asked for;\n    /// `max` is the pool's `msg_size`.\n    SizeTooLarge { requested: usize, max: usize },\n    /// Every buffer the pool owns is currently in flight as a\n    /// live [`PooledMsg`]. Caller must wait for a drop or build\n    /// a pool with more `msg_count`.\n    NoMsgs,\n}\n\nimpl std::fmt::Display for PoolError {\n    /// Render a human-readable one-liner for logs / panics.\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            PoolError::SizeTooLarge { requested, max } => {\n                write!(f, \"requested size {requested} exceeds pool msg_size {max}\")\n            }\n            PoolError::NoMsgs => f.write_str(\"pool exhausted: all buffers are in flight\"),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl std::error::Error for PoolError {}\n\n/// Thread-safe storage backend for a [`Pool`]'s free list.\n/// Implementations choose the retrieval order (LIFO, FIFO,\n/// ring-buffer, …); `Pool` treats the stored buffers as\n/// fungible and does not depend on any particular ordering.\n///\n/// Swapping implementations is the primary extension point\n/// for benchmarking different concurrency strategies. See\n/// [`MutexLifo`] for the default.\npub trait BufRefStore: Send + Sync {\n    /// Build a store that takes ownership of the given\n    /// pre-allocated buffers. The store's capacity\n    /// ([`BufRefStore::size`]) equals `buffers.len()`; its\n    /// initial [`BufRefStore::len`] also equals `buffers.len()`\n    /// — the store starts full.\n    fn from_buffers(buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>>) -> Self\n    where\n        Self: Sized;\n\n    /// Retrieve one buffer from the store, or `None` if the\n    /// store is currently empty.\n    fn get(&self) -> BufRef;\n\n    /// Return a buffer to the store. Precondition: total `ret`\n    /// calls must never exceed total `get` calls plus the\n    /// store's initial capacity. Violating this panics in\n    /// well-behaved implementations.\n    fn ret(&self, buf: Box<[u8]>);\n\n    /// Current number of buffers held by the store. Under\n    /// concurrent load the value is best-effort — it may be\n    /// stale by the time a caller reads it — but it is always\n    /// a valid count (never torn, never beyond `size()`).\n    fn len(&self) -> usize;\n\n    /// Maximum capacity of the store — the count of buffers\n    /// handed to [`BufRefStore::from_buffers`]. Constant for\n    /// the lifetime of the store.\n    fn size(&self) -> usize;\n}\n\n/// Mutex-protected `Vec`-backed LIFO store. The default\n/// [`BufRefStore`] implementation: simple, correct, and\n/// easy to reason about; a baseline to benchmark future\n/// lock-free alternatives against.\n///\n/// `Vec`'s `push` / `pop` give LIFO order at microsecond-ish\n/// cost per op (lock acquire + vector push-or-pop). The\n/// `Vec`'s capacity never changes after `from_buffers` because\n/// `ret` is guaranteed to be matched by a prior `get`.\npub struct MutexLifo {\n    inner: Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>,\n    /// Cached at construction so `size()` is O(1) and\n    /// lock-free.\n    size: usize,\n}\n\nimpl BufRefStore for MutexLifo {\n    /// Take ownership of `buffers` behind a `Mutex`. `size` is\n    /// recorded once here and never changes.\n    fn from_buffers(buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>>) -> Self {\n        let size = buffers.len();\n        Self {\n            inner: Mutex::new(buffers),\n            size,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// LIFO `pop` from the free list; `None` when empty.\n    fn get(&self) -> BufRef {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: Mutex only poisoned by a panic while locked; the\n        //   store's lock holders only push / pop / len / capacity,\n        //   none of which panic.\n        self.inner.lock().unwrap().pop()\n    }\n\n    /// LIFO `push` back onto the free list.\n    fn ret(&self, buf: Box<[u8]>) {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: see MutexLifo::get.\n        self.inner.lock().unwrap().push(buf);\n    }\n\n    /// Current count of buffers sitting in the free list.\n    fn len(&self) -> usize {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: see MutexLifo::get.\n        self.inner.lock().unwrap().len()\n    }\n\n    /// Maximum capacity — fixed at construction.\n    fn size(&self) -> usize {\n        self.size\n    }\n}\n\n/// Internal pool storage; held by `Arc` so actor threads share\n/// one free list. Keep this private — callers see only\n/// [`Pool`] and [`PooledMsg`].\nstruct PoolInner<S: BufRefStore> {\n    msg_size: usize,\n    free: S,\n}\n\n/// Cheaply-cloneable handle to a shared, fixed-capacity buffer\n/// pool. Clones share the same free list (internally `Arc`).\n///\n/// The `S` type parameter selects the underlying\n/// [`BufRefStore`] implementation; it defaults to\n/// [`MutexLifo`] so ordinary callers write `Pool::new(64, 128)`\n/// without annotations. Benchmarks opt in to a different\n/// backend via `Pool::<SomeOtherStore>::new(64, 128)`.\npub struct Pool<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {\n    inner: Arc<PoolInner<S>>,\n}\n\n// Manual `Clone` — `#[derive(Clone)]` would add a spurious\n// `S: Clone` bound, but `Arc<T>` is always clone regardless of T.\nimpl<S: BufRefStore> Clone for Pool<S> {\n    /// Cheap — clones the `Arc`, not the buffers.\n    fn clone(&self) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            inner: self.inner.clone(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore> Pool<S> {\n    /// Build a pool that pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of\n    /// capacity `msg_size` bytes each, hands them to the\n    /// store via [`BufRefStore::from_buffers`], and wraps\n    /// everything in an `Arc`.\n    ///\n    /// Both parameters are `u32` so the pool's total footprint\n    /// is bounded at the type level at `u32::MAX * u32::MAX`\n    /// bytes — well beyond any realistic usage.\n    ///\n    /// `msg_count == 0` is allowed — the pool immediately\n    /// returns [`PoolError::NoMsgs`] on every `get_msg`, useful\n    /// for exhaustion tests.\n    pub fn new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32) -> Self {\n        let msg_size = msg_size as usize;\n        let msg_count = msg_count as usize;\n        let buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>> = (0..msg_count)\n            .map(|_| vec![0u8; msg_size].into_boxed_slice())\n            .collect();\n        let free = S::from_buffers(buffers);\n        Self {\n            inner: Arc::new(PoolInner { msg_size, free }),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Configured maximum byte length of a single message.\n    pub fn msg_size(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.inner.msg_size as u32\n    }\n\n    /// Total buffer capacity across the pool's underlying\n    /// store(s) — `msg_count` as passed to [`Pool::new`].\n    /// Once multi-size sub-pools arrive this will sum across\n    /// all sub-store capacities.\n    pub fn size(&self) -> usize {\n        self.inner.free.size()\n    }\n\n    /// Take a buffer of logical length `size` from the pool.\n    /// Returns [`PoolError::SizeTooLarge`] if\n    /// `size > msg_size`, or [`PoolError::NoMsgs`] if every\n    /// buffer is currently in flight.\n    ///\n    /// The returned [`PooledMsg`] derefs to `[u8]` of length\n    /// `size`; the underlying allocation is always exactly\n    /// `msg_size` bytes. For popped buffers, slots `0..size`\n    /// retain whatever bytes the previous occupant wrote\n    /// (no zeroing — see module docs).\n    pub fn get_msg(&self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError> {\n        if size > self.inner.msg_size {\n            return Err(PoolError::SizeTooLarge {\n                requested: size,\n                max: self.inner.msg_size,\n            });\n        }\n        let buf = self.inner.free.get().ok_or(PoolError::NoMsgs)?;\n        Ok(PooledMsg {\n            buf: Some(buf),\n            len: size,\n            pool: self.inner.clone(),\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Current count of buffers sitting unused in the pool.\n    /// Useful for tests; semantics under concurrent access are\n    /// best-effort.\n    #[cfg(test)]\n    pub(crate) fn free_len(&self) -> usize {\n        self.inner.free.len()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Owned byte buffer handed out by a [`Pool`]. Implements\n/// [`Deref`] / [`DerefMut`] over `[u8]` so handlers can take\n/// `&*msg` for an `&[u8]` view (or `&mut *msg` for\n/// `&mut [u8]`). On `Drop`, the buffer returns to its\n/// originating pool's store via [`BufRefStore::ret`].\n///\n/// The underlying allocation is always `Pool::msg_size`\n/// bytes (fixed); `len` records the logical length the caller\n/// requested and is what `Deref` / `DerefMut` expose.\npub struct PooledMsg<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {\n    /// The owning buffer. `BufRef` = `Option<Box<[u8]>>`; the\n    /// `None` state is only observed transiently inside\n    /// [`Drop::drop`].\n    buf: BufRef,\n    /// Logical message length, `≤ buf.as_ref().unwrap().len()`.\n    len: usize,\n    /// Back-reference to the pool so `Drop` can return the\n    /// buffer. Cloning this `Arc` is the only per-`get_msg`\n    /// synchronization cost beyond the store's own op.\n    pool: Arc<PoolInner<S>>,\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore> Deref for PooledMsg<S> {\n    type Target = [u8];\n    /// Borrow the buffer's first `len` bytes as an `&[u8]`.\n    fn deref(&self) -> &[u8] {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: `buf` is only `None` between `Drop::drop`'s `take`\n        //   and the end of `drop`; `Deref` cannot run during that\n        //   window.\n        &self.buf.as_ref().unwrap()[..self.len]\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore> DerefMut for PooledMsg<S> {\n    /// Mutably borrow the buffer's first `len` bytes.\n    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut [u8] {\n        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n        // OK: see `Deref::deref`.\n        &mut self.buf.as_mut().unwrap()[..self.len]\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore> Drop for PooledMsg<S> {\n    /// Return the underlying `Box<[u8]>` to the pool's store.\n    /// `buf` is left as `None`; no further access to this\n    /// value is possible once `Drop` runs.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        if let Some(buf) = self.buf.take() {\n            self.pool.free.ret(buf);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// A freshly-built pool reports its `msg_size` and its\n    /// `size` (= `msg_count`); the underlying store matches.\n    #[test]\n    fn new_reports_msg_size_and_size() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        assert_eq!(p.msg_size(), 64);\n        assert_eq!(p.size(), 4);\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 4);\n    }\n\n    /// `Pool::size` matches the underlying `BufRefStore::size`\n    /// and is constant even as buffers go in flight.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        assert_eq!(p.size(), 4);\n        let _m = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"get_msg\");\n        // 1 buffer in flight; size() is still 4 (capacity),\n        // even though len() is 3.\n        assert_eq!(p.size(), 4);\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 3);\n    }\n\n    /// `get_msg(size)` returns a buffer of logical length `size`.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_returns_requested_size() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 2);\n        let m = p.get_msg(16).expect(\"get_msg should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), 16);\n    }\n\n    /// Boundary: `size == msg_size` is allowed.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_at_msg_size_ok() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let m = p.get_msg(64).expect(\"get_msg should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), 64);\n    }\n\n    /// Boundary: `size == 0` is allowed and yields an empty\n    /// slice. Cheap to allow; no reason to forbid.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_zero_size_ok() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let m = p.get_msg(0).expect(\"get_msg should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(m.len(), 0);\n    }\n\n    /// `size > msg_size` yields `SizeTooLarge` with the\n    /// requested and max values set.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        match p.get_msg(65) {\n            Err(PoolError::SizeTooLarge { requested, max }) => {\n                assert_eq!(requested, 65);\n                assert_eq!(max, 64);\n            }\n            other => panic!(\"expected SizeTooLarge, got {:?}\", other.err()),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Once all `msg_count` buffers are in flight, `get_msg`\n    /// returns `NoMsgs`.\n    #[test]\n    fn get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 2);\n        let _a = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"first get_msg\");\n        let _b = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"second get_msg\");\n        assert!(matches!(p.get_msg(8), Err(PoolError::NoMsgs)));\n    }\n\n    /// A zero-capacity pool always returns `NoMsgs`.\n    #[test]\n    fn zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.size(), 0);\n        assert!(matches!(p.get_msg(8), Err(PoolError::NoMsgs)));\n    }\n\n    /// After dropping a live `PooledMsg`, the pool recovers a\n    /// slot and `get_msg` succeeds again.\n    #[test]\n    fn drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let m = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"first get_msg\");\n        assert!(matches!(p.get_msg(8), Err(PoolError::NoMsgs)));\n        drop(m);\n        let _m2 = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"post-drop get_msg\");\n    }\n\n    /// The same `Box<[u8]>` allocation is reused across a\n    /// get / drop / get cycle (LIFO, no reallocation).\n    #[test]\n    fn buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let m1 = p.get_msg(8).expect(\"first get_msg\");\n        let ptr1 = m1.as_ptr();\n        drop(m1);\n        let m2 = p.get_msg(16).expect(\"second get_msg\");\n        assert_eq!(m2.as_ptr(), ptr1, \"expected reuse of the same allocation\");\n        assert_eq!(m2.len(), 16);\n    }\n\n    /// Cloned `Pool` handles share the same free list.\n    #[test]\n    fn clone_pool_shares_free_list() {\n        let p1: Pool = Pool::new(64, 2);\n        let p2 = p1.clone();\n        let m = p1.get_msg(8).expect(\"get_msg\");\n        assert_eq!(p2.free_len(), 1);\n        drop(m);\n        assert_eq!(p2.free_len(), 2);\n    }\n\n    /// Writes through `DerefMut` round-trip via `Deref`. The\n    /// logical length caps what's visible.\n    #[test]\n    fn writes_round_trip() {\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let mut m = p.get_msg(4).expect(\"get_msg\");\n        m.copy_from_slice(&[1, 2, 3, 4]);\n        assert_eq!(&*m, &[1, 2, 3, 4]);\n    }\n\n    /// Sharing a pool across threads is sound; buffers dropped\n    /// on a worker thread return to the same free list visible\n    /// from the main thread.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_works_across_threads() {\n        use std::thread;\n        let p: Pool = Pool::new(64, 1);\n        let p2 = p.clone();\n        let h = thread::spawn(move || {\n            let m = p2.get_msg(8).expect(\"worker get_msg\");\n            drop(m);\n        });\n        h.join().expect(\"worker thread panicked\");\n        assert_eq!(p.free_len(), 1);\n    }\n\n    /// `PoolError` renders a readable `Display` string for\n    /// both variants.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_error_display() {\n        let e1 = PoolError::SizeTooLarge {\n            requested: 128,\n            max: 64,\n        };\n        assert_eq!(\n            e1.to_string(),\n            \"requested size 128 exceeds pool msg_size 64\"\n        );\n        let e2 = PoolError::NoMsgs;\n        assert_eq!(e2.to_string(), \"pool exhausted: all buffers are in flight\");\n    }\n\n    /// Direct tests for `MutexLifo` as a `BufRefStore`: the\n    /// trait contract (`get` / `ret` / `len` / `size` /\n    /// `from_buffers`) works without a `Pool` wrapped around it.\n    #[test]\n    fn mutex_lifo_trait_contract() {\n        let buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>> = (0..3)\n            .map(|_| vec![0u8; 16].into_boxed_slice())\n            .collect();\n        let s = MutexLifo::from_buffers(buffers);\n        assert_eq!(s.size(), 3);\n        assert_eq!(s.len(), 3);\n\n        // Drain.\n        let b1 = s.get().expect(\"first get\");\n        let b2 = s.get().expect(\"second get\");\n        let b3 = s.get().expect(\"third get\");\n        assert_eq!(s.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(s.size(), 3); // size is constant\n        assert!(s.get().is_none());\n\n        // Refill.\n        s.ret(b1);\n        s.ret(b2);\n        s.ret(b3);\n        assert_eq!(s.len(), 3);\n        assert_eq!(s.size(), 3);\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "old_string": "## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)\n\nImplements the fixed-capacity buffer pool that `RuntimeZC`\nwill use to ship zerocopy messages between actors. The API\ndeviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in two ways, both decided\nduring review of the first cut:\n\n- Constructor takes explicit size / count: `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)` — pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of capacity `msg_size`, rather than starting empty and growing lazily.\n- `get_msg` returns `Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` rather than panicking — the two failure modes (oversized request, exhaustion) are normal runtime conditions for a fixed-capacity pool and callers are expected to handle them.\n\nThe plan-marker entry (`0.5.0-0`) described a lazy,\nMAX_SIZE-constant pool that the `get_msg` panicked on misuse;\nthat shape is superseded here.\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-0` → `0.5.0-1`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;` added before `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub enum PoolError` — two variants:\n    - `SizeTooLarge { requested: usize, max: usize }` — `size > msg_size`.\n    - `NoMsgs` — free list empty; every buffer is in flight.\n    - `impl Display` / `impl std::error::Error` so callers can bubble it through `?` on a `Box<dyn Error>` or print it in logs.\n  - `pub struct Pool` — `#[derive(Clone)]` `Arc<PoolInner>` handle.\n    - Internal `PoolInner { msg_size: usize, free: Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>> }` (private).\n    - `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)` — pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of `Vec::with_capacity(msg_size)`.\n    - `Pool::msg_size() -> u32` — exposes the configured max for callers who need to validate up front.\n    - `Pool::get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` — pops one buffer from the free list; `resize(size, 0)` for logical length; errors as above.\n    - `Pool::free_len() -> usize` — `#[cfg(test)]` only.\n  - `pub struct PooledMsg { buf: Option<Vec<u8>>, pool: Arc<PoolInner> }` — `Option` so `Drop` can `take()` the inner `Vec`.\n    - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` / `DerefMut` — handler view is `&*msg` / `&mut *msg`.\n    - `Drop` — pushes the inner `Vec` back onto the pool's free list.\n\n### Tests added (all `#[cfg(test)]` in `pool.rs`, 13 total)\n\n- `new_preallocates_and_exposes_msg_size` — fresh pool reports `msg_size` and has `msg_count` buffers ready.\n- `get_msg_returns_requested_size` — `len()` matches the requested size.\n- `get_msg_at_msg_size_ok` — `size == msg_size` boundary works.\n- `get_msg_zero_size_ok` — `size == 0` yields an empty slice.\n- `get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large` — errors with both `requested` and `max` set correctly.\n- `get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs` — after all slots are in flight, `get_msg` yields `NoMsgs`.\n- `zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs` — `Pool::new(_, 0)` always errors; sanity check for the zero edge.\n- `drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds` — dropping an in-flight msg re-opens a slot.\n- `buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get` — pointer equality survives a get / drop / get cycle (capacity preserved, no realloc).\n- `clone_pool_shares_free_list` — drop on one `Pool` clone is visible from another.\n- `writes_round_trip` — `DerefMut` writes are visible through `Deref`.\n- `pool_works_across_threads` — `Pool` cloned to a worker thread; buffer dropped on worker returns to the shared free list.\n- `pool_error_display` — both variants render readable one-liners.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- Fixed-capacity, pre-allocated pool rather than lazy growth.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's \"starts empty, grows on demand\" design.\n  - Pre-allocation makes `NoMsgs` a real, testable condition — the runtime's back-pressure story has to face exhaustion, so surfacing it directly in the pool is the honest shape.\n  - Steady-state behavior for ping-pong is unchanged (two buffers cycling) — the difference is that a burst of concurrent `get_msg` calls returns `NoMsgs` instead of silently allocating beyond the configured budget.\n- `Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` rather than panic on misuse.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's `panic!` on `size > MAX_SIZE`.\n  - `NoMsgs` is runtime-variable — a benchmark or application hitting exhaustion should not crash; it needs to apply back-pressure or report the saturation.\n  - Making the size error the same shape keeps one error path.\n  - `goalzc`'s CLI parser will still validate `--size <= msg_size()` up front so the `SizeTooLarge` variant never surfaces from the hot path; the variant exists for API safety, not for runtime handling in the ping-pong case.\n- `msg_size` / `msg_count` typed as `u32`.\n  - Matches the user's requested shape: explicit byte-range and count limits at the type level.\n  - Internally converted to `usize` for `Vec` / slice APIs; the conversion is widening on every platform actor-x1 targets.\n  - `get_msg(size: usize)` stays `usize` for clean interop with slice/`Vec` lengths; if `size > u32::MAX` the `SizeTooLarge` guard catches it.\n- `Vec<u8>` rather than `Box<[u8]>` for the buffer.\n  - `Vec::resize` within capacity does not reallocate, so the get / drop / get cycle preserves the same allocation.\n  - `Box<[u8]>` would force a fixed length and either a `(Box<[u8]>, usize)` wrapper or pad-and-truncate semantics — more plumbing for no win.\n- `Option<Vec<u8>>` inside `PooledMsg` so `Drop` can move the inner `Vec` out.\n  - The `Option` is only `None` during the brief window inside `Drop::drop`; `Deref`/`DerefMut` cannot observe it.\n  - `unsafe` alternatives (`ManuallyDrop` + a destructor) would shave the `unwrap`s but add unsafety; not worth it for a small struct.\n- No zeroing on drop.\n  - Slots `0..new_size` of a reused buffer carry residual bytes from the previous occupant.\n  - For ping-pong workloads the next consumer either overwrites or ignores those bytes — benign.\n  - Real applications crossing trust domains would need an explicit `zeroize`-on-drop step; documented in the module-level comment.\n- No `Default for Pool`.\n  - There's no sensible default for `msg_size` / `msg_count`; every caller has to choose.\n  - Dropped from the first cut where `Pool::new()` took no args.",
  "new_string": "## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)\n\nImplements the fixed-capacity buffer pool that `RuntimeZC`\nwill use to ship zerocopy messages between actors. The\nshipped shape deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in several\nways — all decided during review of successive design cuts:\n\n- Constructor takes explicit byte / count: `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)`. Pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of `msg_size` bytes each; no lazy growth, no global `MAX_SIZE` const.\n- `get_msg` returns `Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` rather than panicking. Two variants: `SizeTooLarge { requested, max }` and `NoMsgs`.\n- Buffers are `Box<[u8]>` (fixed-size allocation) plus a separate `len: usize` on `PooledMsg` for the logical message length, instead of `Vec<u8>`'s redundant `cap` field.\n- The free list lives behind a `BufRefStore` trait so different concurrency strategies (Mutex, lock-free queue, ring buffer, hand-rolled atomics, …) can be benchmarked against each other. The default is `MutexLifo`.\n- `Pool` and `PooledMsg` are generic over `S: BufRefStore`, with `MutexLifo` as the default type — ordinary call sites write `Pool::new(64, 128)` unchanged.\n- A `BufRef` type alias documents the `Option<Box<[u8]>>` niche-optimization idiom in one place.\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-0` → `0.5.0-1`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;` added before `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;` — module-level alias documenting the niche-optimized \"null or non-null pointer\" pattern.\n  - `pub enum PoolError { SizeTooLarge { requested, max }, NoMsgs }` — `Display` + `std::error::Error` impls.\n  - `pub trait BufRefStore: Send + Sync` — `from_buffers`, `get`, `ret`, `len`, `size`, `is_empty` (default-impl over `len`).\n  - `pub struct MutexLifo` — initial impl: `Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>` + cached `size: usize` for O(1) lock-free `size()`.\n  - `pub struct Pool<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `Arc<PoolInner<S>>` handle; manual `Clone` impl avoids spurious `S: Clone` bound.\n    - `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)` — allocates buffers, calls `S::from_buffers`.\n    - `Pool::msg_size() -> u32` — bytes per buffer.\n    - `Pool::size() -> usize` — total buffer capacity (delegates to the store; will sum across sub-stores once multi-size pools land).\n    - `Pool::get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` — validates `size`, pops via `S::get`, returns the wrapped buffer.\n    - `Pool::free_len()` — `#[cfg(test)]` only.\n  - `pub struct PooledMsg<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `{ buf: BufRef, len: usize, pool: Arc<PoolInner<S>> }`.\n    - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` / `DerefMut` — slice the underlying `Box<[u8]>` to `[..len]`.\n    - `Drop` — `S::ret(self.buf.take().unwrap())` returns the buffer to the store.\n\n### Tests added (all `#[cfg(test)]` in `pool.rs`, 15 total)\n\n- `new_reports_msg_size_and_size` — fresh pool reports `msg_size`, `size`, and free-list `len`.\n- `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` — `size()` stays at `msg_count` while in-flight buffers reduce `len()`.\n- `get_msg_returns_requested_size` — `len()` of the returned slice matches the requested size.\n- `get_msg_at_msg_size_ok` — `size == msg_size` boundary works.\n- `get_msg_zero_size_ok` — `size == 0` yields an empty slice.\n- `get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large` — error carries `requested` + `max` correctly.\n- `get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs` — after all slots are in flight, `get_msg` yields `NoMsgs`.\n- `zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs` — `Pool::new(_, 0)` always errors; sanity check for the zero edge.\n- `drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds` — dropping an in-flight msg re-opens a slot.\n- `buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get` — pointer equality survives a get / drop / get cycle (LIFO, no realloc).\n- `clone_pool_shares_free_list` — drop on one `Pool` clone is visible from another.\n- `writes_round_trip` — `DerefMut` writes are visible through `Deref`.\n- `pool_works_across_threads` — `Pool` cloned to a worker thread; buffer dropped on worker returns to the shared free list.\n- `pool_error_display` — both variants render readable one-liners.\n- `mutex_lifo_trait_contract` — `BufRefStore` contract direct on `MutexLifo` without a `Pool` wrapper: `from_buffers` / `get` / `ret` / `len` / `size` round-trip end-to-end.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- `BufRefStore` trait + generic `Pool<S>` rather than a hardcoded backend.\n  - Primary motivation: the user wants to swap concurrency strategies and benchmark them — Mutex baseline today, lock-free `ArrayQueue`, ring buffers, hand-rolled atomics later.\n  - Generic-with-default (`S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo`) keeps the common call site unchanged (`Pool::new(64, 128)`) while letting benchmarks opt into other backends with `Pool::<X>::new(64, 128)`.\n  - Trait surface stays minimal: `from_buffers` / `get` / `ret` / `len` / `size`. No \"ordering\" or \"is_lockfree\" methods; ordering is implementation-defined and `Pool` treats buffers as fungible.\n  - Dyn-dispatch via `Box<dyn BufRefStore>` was considered and rejected for benchmark fidelity — a `dyn` indirection in the hot path would contaminate the very thing we're trying to compare.\n- `BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>` alias.\n  - Documents the \"safe null/non-null pointer\" idiom in one place rather than re-explaining at each use site.\n  - Used in `PooledMsg.buf` (nullable transiently during `Drop::drop`) and as `BufRefStore::get`'s return (signaling empty store).\n- Fixed-capacity pre-allocation rather than lazy growth.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's \"starts empty, grows on demand\" design.\n  - Makes `NoMsgs` a real, testable condition — back-pressure is exposed instead of hidden behind silent allocation.\n  - Steady-state behavior for ping-pong is unchanged (two buffers cycling).\n- `Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` rather than panic on misuse.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's panic on `size > MAX_SIZE`.\n  - `NoMsgs` is a runtime condition; a benchmark hitting exhaustion should not crash, it should report saturation.\n  - `SizeTooLarge` follows the same shape for one error path.\n- `Box<[u8]>` + separate `len` rather than `Vec<u8>`.\n  - `Vec`'s `cap` field is dead weight when capacity is fixed at `msg_size`; `Box<[u8]>` is `(ptr, len)` only.\n  - `Vec` also implies \"growable\", which is misleading here.\n  - `len` lives on `PooledMsg` instead of in the store, so the store treats buffers as identical fixed-size allocations.\n- `Option<Box<[u8]>>` inside `PooledMsg` so `Drop` can move the inner `Box` out.\n  - Niche-optimized to a single pointer at runtime (no discriminant tag).\n  - `None` only observed transiently inside `Drop::drop`; `Deref` / `DerefMut` can't see it.\n  - `unsafe` alternatives (`ManuallyDrop` + a destructor) would shave the `unwrap`s but add unsafety; not worth it.\n- `BufRefStore::size()` returns the constant store capacity.\n  - Distinct from `len()` (current count), which fluctuates as buffers go in flight.\n  - Cached at `from_buffers` time so `MutexLifo::size()` is O(1) lock-free.\n  - Test `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` validates the invariant.\n  - Future-proofs the multi-size sub-pool extension: `Pool::size()` already sums \"across sub-stores\" (one for now).\n- `msg_size` / `msg_count` typed as `u32`.\n  - Matches the user's requested shape: explicit byte-range and count limits at the type level.\n  - Internally converted to `usize` for `Vec` / slice APIs; widening on every platform actor-x1 targets.\n  - `get_msg(size: usize)` stays `usize` for clean interop with slice/`Vec` lengths; if `size > u32::MAX` the `SizeTooLarge` guard catches it.\n- `is_empty` as a default-impl trait method.\n  - Required by clippy's `len_without_is_empty` lint when a type / trait exposes `len()`.\n  - Default body `len() == 0` is correct for any conforming impl; concrete impls can override if they have a faster path.\n- No zeroing on drop.\n  - Slots `0..new_size` of a reused buffer carry residual bytes from the previous occupant.\n  - For ping-pong workloads the next consumer either overwrites or ignores those bytes — benign.\n  - Real applications crossing trust domains would need an explicit `zeroize`-on-drop step; documented in the module-level comment.\n- No `Default for Pool`.\n  - There's no sensible default for `msg_size` / `msg_count`; every caller has to choose."
}
```

> AGENT

`0.5.0-1` complete with the `BufRefStore` trait + `MutexLifo` initial impl. Summary of what's now on disk:

- **`BufRef`** — `pub type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;` with niche-optimization comment.
- **`BufRefStore` trait** — `from_buffers`, `get`, `ret`, `len`, `size`, `is_empty` (default-impl). `Send + Sync`.
- **`MutexLifo`** — initial backend: `Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>` + cached `size: usize` for O(1) lock-free `size()`.
- **`Pool<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>`** — generic, with default; manual `Clone` impl avoids spurious `S: Clone` bound. Methods: `new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)`, `msg_size() -> u32`, `size() -> usize`, `get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>`.
- **`PooledMsg<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>`** — `{ buf: BufRef, len: usize, pool: Arc<PoolInner<S>> }`. Fixed-size `Box<[u8]>` storage; `Deref`/`DerefMut` slice to `[..len]`; `Drop` calls `S::ret`.
- **`PoolError`** — `SizeTooLarge { requested, max }`, `NoMsgs`. `Display` + `std::error::Error`.
- **15 tests** — all passing. New since the previous cut: `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` (validates `Pool::size()` == store's `size()`, constant under in-flight churn) and `mutex_lifo_trait_contract` (direct `BufRefStore` round-trip without a `Pool` wrapper).

Pre-commit: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, full workspace `cargo test` 22 actor-x1 + 18 tprobe lib tests passing.

Chores section rewritten to describe the actual shipped shape, with sub-bullet design-decisions covering the `BufRefStore`-trait choice, `BufRef` alias rationale, fixed-capacity vs lazy, `Result` vs panic, `Box<[u8]>` vs `Vec<u8>`, `Option<Box<[u8]>>` niche-opt, `size()` constancy, and the multi-size sub-pool future direction.

Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

  "old_string": "## Notes writing style: prefer sub-bullets\n\nDurable text the bot writes — `notes/chores-*.md`, `notes/todo.md`,\ncommitted READMEs, design notes — should itemize with sub-bullets\nrather than run as large prose paragraphs. Short intro sentences\n(1–3) at the top of a section are fine; detail goes into\nhierarchical bullet lists.\n\n**Why:** easier to scan and cross-reference during review;\nmatches how the user reads and edits committed reference\nmaterial. A wall of prose hides structure that a nested list\nmakes visible.\n\n**How to apply:** default to bullet structure for any non-trivial\nsection. Reserve paragraphs for brief intros and occasional\ncontextual framing. If a paragraph runs past ~3 sentences, look\nfor the implicit list inside it and break it out. Design-decision\nentries in particular benefit — the *reason* / *what-was-rejected*\n/ *how-it-applies* decomposition reads naturally as sub-bullets.",
  "new_string": "## Writing style: prefer sub-bullets\n\nDurable text the bot writes should itemize with sub-bullets\nrather than run as large prose paragraphs. Applies to:\n\n- `notes/chores-*.md`, `notes/todo.md`, committed READMEs, design notes.\n- Module `//!` docstrings, item `///` doc comments on traits / structs / functions / methods, and any other doc comment that runs more than a few sentences.\n\nShort intro sentences (1–3) at the top of a section / item are\nfine; detail goes into hierarchical bullet lists.\n\n**Why:** easier to scan and cross-reference during review;\nmatches how the user reads and edits committed reference\nmaterial. A wall of prose hides structure that a nested list\nmakes visible — and the same is true inside doc comments,\nwhere rustdoc renders the list cleanly.\n\n**How to apply:**\n\n- Default to bullet structure for any non-trivial section or\n  doc comment. Reserve paragraphs for brief intros and\n  occasional contextual framing.\n- If a paragraph runs past ~3 sentences, look for the implicit\n  list inside it and break it out.\n- Design-decision entries benefit most — the *reason* /\n  *what-was-rejected* / *how-it-applies* decomposition reads\n  naturally as sub-bullets.\n- Single-line doc comments on trivial methods stay one line —\n  bullets are not mandatory, just *available* when content is\n  long enough to warrant them."

248	This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
249	default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the

258	
259	- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files.
260	- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md`.
261	- Todo entries in `notes/todo.md` (when an item needs more than
262	  one line of detail; pure one-liners are still fine).
263	
264	**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive
265	`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets
266	into running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on
267	any field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on
268	its own line in the rendered help.
269	
270	### `// OK: …` comments on `unwrap*` calls (Rust)
271	
272	Non-test code that calls `.unwrap()`, `.unwrap_or(…)`,
273	`.unwrap_or_default()`, or `.unwrap_or_else(…)` must have a trailing
274	`// OK: …` comment that justifies why the call is acceptable.
```

1	//! Fixed-capacity buffer pool for `RuntimeZC` zerocopy messages.
2	//!
3	//! A [`Pool`] is configured at construction with `msg_size` (the
4	//! maximum byte length of any buffer it hands out) and
5	//! `msg_count` (the number of buffers it pre-allocates). The
6	//! pool's free list is held in a [`BufRefStore`] implementation;
7	//! swapping implementations is how this crate will benchmark
8	//! different concurrency strategies (Mutex, lock-free queue,
9	//! ring buffer, hand-rolled atomics, …). The default is
10	//! [`MutexLifo`].
11	//!
12	//! Retrieval order is implementation-defined — callers treat
13	//! buffers as fungible. [`Pool::get_msg`] returns either a
14	//! [`PooledMsg`] (a fixed-size `Box<[u8]>` that derefs to
15	//! `[u8]`) or a [`PoolError`]: [`PoolError::SizeTooLarge`] if
16	//! the requested size exceeds `msg_size`, or
17	//! [`PoolError::NoMsgs`] if the store is currently empty.
18	//! Buffers return to the store when their owning `PooledMsg` is
19	//! dropped.
20	//!
21	//! No zeroing happens on drop. A reused buffer's bytes
22	//! `0..new_size` may contain residual data from the previous
23	//! occupant; ping-pong handlers either overwrite or ignore
24	//! those bytes, so the leak is benign for the workloads this
25	//! crate measures. Real applications that ship pooled messages
26	//! between trust domains would need an explicit zeroize step.
27	//!
28	//! Future extension (not yet implemented): once `Pool` supports
29	//! multiple size classes — small / medium / large sub-pools so
30	//! the many-small-few-large case is cheap — a single `Pool`
31	//! will hold several `BufRefStore` instances, one per class.
32	//! `BufRefStore::size()` on each still returns that sub-store's
33	//! capacity; [`Pool::size`] sums across them for the total.
34	
35	use std::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};
36	use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
37	
38	/// Nullable buffer handle. Concretely `Option<Box<[u8]>>`, which
39	/// thanks to Rust's niche optimization occupies exactly one
40	/// pointer at runtime — `None` is the null bit pattern,
41	/// `Some(box)` is the non-null heap address of a fixed-size
42	/// byte allocation. Used wherever the code needs to express
43	/// "a buffer, or nothing":
44	/// - inside [`PooledMsg`] so [`Drop::drop`] can [`Option::take`]
45	///   the box out without leaving `self` in an invalid state;
46	/// - as [`BufRefStore::get`]'s return value so stores can
47	///   signal "empty".
48	pub type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;
49	
50	/// Failure modes for [`Pool::get_msg`].
51	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
52	pub enum PoolError {
53	    /// The requested size exceeds the pool's configured
54	    /// `msg_size`. `requested` is what the caller asked for;
55	    /// `max` is the pool's `msg_size`.
56	    SizeTooLarge { requested: usize, max: usize },
57	    /// Every buffer the pool owns is currently in flight as a
58	    /// live [`PooledMsg`]. Caller must wait for a drop or build
59	    /// a pool with more `msg_count`.
60	    NoMsgs,
61	}
62	
63	impl std::fmt::Display for PoolError {
64	    /// Render a human-readable one-liner for logs / panics.
65	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
66	        match self {
67	            PoolError::SizeTooLarge { requested, max } => {
68	                write!(f, "requested size {requested} exceeds pool msg_size {max}")
69	            }
70	            PoolError::NoMsgs => f.write_str("pool exhausted: all buffers are in flight"),
71	        }
72	    }
73	}
74	
75	impl std::error::Error for PoolError {}
76	
77	/// Thread-safe storage backend for a [`Pool`]'s free list.
78	/// Implementations choose the retrieval order (LIFO, FIFO,
79	/// ring-buffer, …); `Pool` treats the stored buffers as
80	/// fungible and does not depend on any particular ordering.
81	///
82	/// Swapping implementations is the primary extension point
83	/// for benchmarking different concurrency strategies. See
84	/// [`MutexLifo`] for the default.
85	pub trait BufRefStore: Send + Sync {
86	    /// Build a store that takes ownership of the given
87	    /// pre-allocated buffers. The store's capacity
88	    /// ([`BufRefStore::size`]) equals `buffers.len()`; its
89	    /// initial [`BufRefStore::len`] also equals `buffers.len()`
90	    /// — the store starts full.
91	    fn from_buffers(buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>>) -> Self
92	    where
93	        Self: Sized;
94	
95	    /// Retrieve one buffer from the store, or `None` if the
96	    /// store is currently empty.
97	    fn get(&self) -> BufRef;
98	
99	    /// Return a buffer to the store. Precondition: total `ret`
100	    /// calls must never exceed total `get` calls plus the
101	    /// store's initial capacity. Violating this panics in
102	    /// well-behaved implementations.
103	    fn ret(&self, buf: Box<[u8]>);
104	
105	    /// Current number of buffers held by the store. Under
106	    /// concurrent load the value is best-effort — it may be
107	    /// stale by the time a caller reads it — but it is always
108	    /// a valid count (never torn, never beyond `size()`).
109	    fn len(&self) -> usize;
110	
111	    /// Maximum capacity of the store — the count of buffers
112	    /// handed to [`BufRefStore::from_buffers`]. Constant for
113	    /// the lifetime of the store.
114	    fn size(&self) -> usize;
115	
116	    /// `len() == 0`. Provided as a default; impls can override
117	    /// with a faster path if their `len()` is not already O(1).
118	    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
119	        self.len() == 0
120	    }
121	}
122	
123	/// Mutex-protected `Vec`-backed LIFO store. The default
124	/// [`BufRefStore`] implementation: simple, correct, and
125	/// easy to reason about; a baseline to benchmark future
126	/// lock-free alternatives against.
127	///
128	/// `Vec`'s `push` / `pop` give LIFO order at microsecond-ish
129	/// cost per op (lock acquire + vector push-or-pop). The
130	/// `Vec`'s capacity never changes after `from_buffers` because
131	/// `ret` is guaranteed to be matched by a prior `get`.
132	pub struct MutexLifo {
133	    inner: Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>,
134	    /// Cached at construction so `size()` is O(1) and
135	    /// lock-free.
136	    size: usize,
137	}
138	
139	impl BufRefStore for MutexLifo {
140	    /// Take ownership of `buffers` behind a `Mutex`. `size` is
141	    /// recorded once here and never changes.
142	    fn from_buffers(buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>>) -> Self {
143	        let size = buffers.len();
144	        Self {
145	            inner: Mutex::new(buffers),
146	            size,
147	        }
148	    }
149	
150	    /// LIFO `pop` from the free list; `None` when empty.
151	    fn get(&self) -> BufRef {
152	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
153	        // OK: Mutex only poisoned by a panic while locked; the
154	        //   store's lock holders only push / pop / len / capacity,
155	        //   none of which panic.
156	        self.inner.lock().unwrap().pop()
157	    }
158	
159	    /// LIFO `push` back onto the free list.
160	    fn ret(&self, buf: Box<[u8]>) {
161	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
162	        // OK: see MutexLifo::get.
163	        self.inner.lock().unwrap().push(buf);
164	    }
165	
166	    /// Current count of buffers sitting in the free list.
167	    fn len(&self) -> usize {
168	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
169	        // OK: see MutexLifo::get.
170	        self.inner.lock().unwrap().len()
171	    }
172	
173	    /// Maximum capacity — fixed at construction.
174	    fn size(&self) -> usize {
175	        self.size
176	    }
177	}
178	
179	/// Internal pool storage; held by `Arc` so actor threads share
180	/// one free list. Keep this private — callers see only
181	/// [`Pool`] and [`PooledMsg`].
182	struct PoolInner<S: BufRefStore> {
183	    msg_size: usize,
184	    free: S,
185	}
186	
187	/// Cheaply-cloneable handle to a shared, fixed-capacity buffer
188	/// pool. Clones share the same free list (internally `Arc`).
189	///
190	/// The `S` type parameter selects the underlying
191	/// [`BufRefStore`] implementation; it defaults to
192	/// [`MutexLifo`] so ordinary callers write `Pool::new(64, 128)`
193	/// without annotations. Benchmarks opt in to a different
194	/// backend via `Pool::<SomeOtherStore>::new(64, 128)`.
195	pub struct Pool<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {
196	    inner: Arc<PoolInner<S>>,
197	}
198	
199	// Manual `Clone` — `#[derive(Clone)]` would add a spurious
200	// `S: Clone` bound, but `Arc<T>` is always clone regardless of T.
```

200	// `S: Clone` bound, but `Arc<T>` is always clone regardless of T.
201	impl<S: BufRefStore> Clone for Pool<S> {
202	    /// Cheap — clones the `Arc`, not the buffers.
203	    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
204	        Self {
205	            inner: self.inner.clone(),
206	        }
207	    }
208	}
209	
210	impl<S: BufRefStore> Pool<S> {
211	    /// Build a pool that pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of
212	    /// capacity `msg_size` bytes each, hands them to the
213	    /// store via [`BufRefStore::from_buffers`], and wraps
214	    /// everything in an `Arc`.
215	    ///
216	    /// Both parameters are `u32` so the pool's total footprint
217	    /// is bounded at the type level at `u32::MAX * u32::MAX`
218	    /// bytes — well beyond any realistic usage.
219	    ///
220	    /// `msg_count == 0` is allowed — the pool immediately
221	    /// returns [`PoolError::NoMsgs`] on every `get_msg`, useful
222	    /// for exhaustion tests.
223	    pub fn new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32) -> Self {
224	        let msg_size = msg_size as usize;
225	        let msg_count = msg_count as usize;
226	        let buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>> = (0..msg_count)
227	            .map(|_| vec![0u8; msg_size].into_boxed_slice())
228	            .collect();
229	        let free = S::from_buffers(buffers);
230	        Self {
231	            inner: Arc::new(PoolInner { msg_size, free }),
232	        }
233	    }
234	
235	    /// Configured maximum byte length of a single message.
236	    pub fn msg_size(&self) -> u32 {
237	        self.inner.msg_size as u32
238	    }
239	
240	    /// Total buffer capacity across the pool's underlying
241	    /// store(s) — `msg_count` as passed to [`Pool::new`].
242	    /// Once multi-size sub-pools arrive this will sum across
243	    /// all sub-store capacities.
244	    pub fn size(&self) -> usize {
245	        self.inner.free.size()
246	    }
247	
248	    /// Take a buffer of logical length `size` from the pool.
249	    /// Returns [`PoolError::SizeTooLarge`] if
250	    /// `size > msg_size`, or [`PoolError::NoMsgs`] if every
251	    /// buffer is currently in flight.
252	    ///
253	    /// The returned [`PooledMsg`] derefs to `[u8]` of length
254	    /// `size`; the underlying allocation is always exactly
255	    /// `msg_size` bytes. For popped buffers, slots `0..size`
256	    /// retain whatever bytes the previous occupant wrote
257	    /// (no zeroing — see module docs).
258	    pub fn get_msg(&self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError> {
259	        if size > self.inner.msg_size {
260	            return Err(PoolError::SizeTooLarge {
261	                requested: size,
262	                max: self.inner.msg_size,
263	            });
264	        }
265	        let buf = self.inner.free.get().ok_or(PoolError::NoMsgs)?;
266	        Ok(PooledMsg {
267	            buf: Some(buf),
268	            len: size,
269	            pool: self.inner.clone(),
270	        })
271	    }
272	
273	    /// Current count of buffers sitting unused in the pool.
274	    /// Useful for tests; semantics under concurrent access are
275	    /// best-effort.
276	    #[cfg(test)]
277	    pub(crate) fn free_len(&self) -> usize {
278	        self.inner.free.len()
279	    }
280	}
281	
282	/// Owned byte buffer handed out by a [`Pool`]. Implements
283	/// [`Deref`] / [`DerefMut`] over `[u8]` so handlers can take
284	/// `&*msg` for an `&[u8]` view (or `&mut *msg` for
285	/// `&mut [u8]`). On `Drop`, the buffer returns to its
286	/// originating pool's store via [`BufRefStore::ret`].
287	///
288	/// The underlying allocation is always `Pool::msg_size`
289	/// bytes (fixed); `len` records the logical length the caller
290	/// requested and is what `Deref` / `DerefMut` expose.
291	pub struct PooledMsg<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {
292	    /// The owning buffer. `BufRef` = `Option<Box<[u8]>>`; the
293	    /// `None` state is only observed transiently inside
294	    /// [`Drop::drop`].
295	    buf: BufRef,
296	    /// Logical message length, `≤ buf.as_ref().unwrap().len()`.
297	    len: usize,
298	    /// Back-reference to the pool so `Drop` can return the
299	    /// buffer. Cloning this `Arc` is the only per-`get_msg`
300	    /// synchronization cost beyond the store's own op.
301	    pool: Arc<PoolInner<S>>,
302	}
303	
304	impl<S: BufRefStore> Deref for PooledMsg<S> {
305	    type Target = [u8];
306	    /// Borrow the buffer's first `len` bytes as an `&[u8]`.
307	    fn deref(&self) -> &[u8] {
308	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
309	        // OK: `buf` is only `None` between `Drop::drop`'s `take`

Precondition: total `ret`\n    /// calls must never exceed total `get` calls plus the\n    /// store's initial capacity. Violating this panics in\n    /// well-behaved implementations.\n    fn ret(&self, buf: Box<[u8]>);\n\n    /// Current number of buffers held by the store. Under\n    /// concurrent load the value is best-effort — it may be\n    /// stale by the time a caller reads it — but it is always\n    /// a valid count (never torn, never beyond `size()`).\n    fn len(&self) -> usize;\n\n    /// Maximum capacity of the store — the count of buffers\n    /// handed to [`BufRefStore::from_buffers`]. Constant for\n    /// the lifetime of the store.\n    fn size(&self) -> usize;\n\n    /// `len() == 0`. Provided as a default; impls can override\n    /// with a faster path if their `len()` is not already O(1).\n    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {\n        self.len() == 0\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Thread-safe storage backend for a [`Pool`]'s free list.\n///\n/// - Implementations choose the retrieval order (LIFO, FIFO,\n///   ring-buffer, …).\n/// - [`Pool`] treats stored buffers as fungible and does not\n///   depend on any particular ordering.\n/// - Swapping implementations is the primary extension point\n///   for benchmarking concurrency strategies.\n/// - Default impl: [`MutexLifo`].\npub trait BufRefStore: Send + Sync {\n    /// Build a store owning `buffers`.\n    ///\n    /// - Store capacity ([`BufRefStore::size`]) = `buffers.len()`.\n    /// - Initial [`BufRefStore::len`] = `buffers.len()` —\n    ///   the store starts full.\n    fn from_buffers(buffers: Vec<Box<[u8]>>) -> Self\n    where\n        Self: Sized;\n\n    /// Retrieve one buffer from the store, or `None` if empty.\n    fn get(&self) -> BufRef;\n\n    /// Return a buffer to the store.\n    ///\n    /// - Precondition: total `ret` calls never exceed total\n    ///   `get` calls plus the store's initial capacity.\n    /// - Violating the precondition panics in well-behaved\n    ///   implementations.\n    fn ret(&self, buf: Box<[u8]>);\n\n    /// Current number of buffers held by the store.\n    ///\n    /// - Under concurrent load the value is best-effort — it\n    ///   may be stale by the time the caller reads it.\n    /// - Always a valid count (never torn, never beyond\n    ///   [`BufRefStore::size`]).\n    fn len(&self) -> usize;\n\n    /// Maximum capacity — the count of buffers handed to\n    /// [`BufRefStore::from_buffers`]. Constant for the lifetime\n    /// of the store.\n    fn size(&self) -> usize;\n\n    /// `len() == 0`. Default-impl'd; override with a faster\n    /// path if an impl's `len()` is not already O(1).\n    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {\n        self.len() == 0\n    }\n}"
}

The default\n/// [`BufRefStore`] implementation: simple, correct, and\n/// easy to reason about; a baseline to benchmark future\n/// lock-free alternatives against.\n///\n/// `Vec`'s `push` / `pop` give LIFO order at microsecond-ish\n/// cost per op (lock acquire + vector push-or-pop). The\n/// `Vec`'s capacity never changes after `from_buffers` because\n/// `ret` is guaranteed to be matched by a prior `get`.\npub struct MutexLifo {",
  "new_string": "/// Default [`BufRefStore`] — Mutex-protected `Vec`-backed LIFO.\n///\n/// - Simple, correct, easy to reason about.\n/// - Baseline to benchmark future lock-free alternatives\n///   against.\n/// - `Vec::push` / `Vec::pop` give LIFO order; cost per op is\n///   lock acquire + vector push-or-pop.\n/// - `Vec` capacity never changes after `from_buffers` because\n///   `ret` is guaranteed to be matched by a prior `get`.\npub struct MutexLifo {"
}

For popped buffers, slots `0..size`\n    /// retain whatever bytes the previous occupant wrote\n    /// (no zeroing — see module docs).\n    pub fn get_msg(&self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError> {",

> AGENT

Doc-comment refactor done. Two changes this turn:

- **`CLAUDE.md` "Writing style: prefer sub-bullets"** — section retitled (was "Notes writing style"); scope expanded to cover module `//!` docstrings and item `///` doc comments, with explicit clap-derive caveat (`#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` needed when bullet doc comments live on `#[arg(...)]` fields, since clap reflows by default and collapses bullets into prose). Cross-references `vc-x1/src/init.rs` for examples.
- **`crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`** — refactored eight doc comment blocks from prose paragraphs to intro + sub-bullets:
  - Module `//!` (Shape / Call contract / Non-guarantees / Future extension subsections).
  - `BufRef` alias.
  - `BufRefStore` trait + each of its methods.
  - `MutexLifo` struct.
  - `Pool` struct, `Pool::new`, `Pool::size`, `Pool::get_msg`.
  - `PooledMsg` struct.

All 22 actor-x1 lib tests still passing; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean.

Note for `0.5.0-3`: when `goalzc.rs` is built with bullet-using doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields, those will need `verbatim_doc_comment` per the new CLAUDE.md rule.

Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

}</code></pre><details class="toggle top-doc" open><summary class="hideme"><span>Expand description</span></summary><div class="docblock"><p>Thread-safe storage backend for a <a href="struct.Pool.html" title="struct actor_x1::pool::Pool"><code>Pool</code></a>’s free list.</p>
<ul>
<li>Implementations choose the retrieval order (LIFO, FIFO,
ring-buffer, …).</li>
<li><a href="struct.Pool.html" title="struct actor_x1::pool::Pool"><code>Pool</code></a> treats stored buffers as fungible and does not
depend on any particular ordering.</li>
<li>Swapping implementations is the primary extension point
for benchmarking concurrency strategies.</li>
<li>Default impl: <a href="struct.MutexLifo.html" title="struct actor_x1::pool::MutexLifo"><code>MutexLifo</code></a>.</li>

<p>Thread-safe storage backend for a <code>Pool</code>'s free list.</p>
<ul>
<li>Implementations choose the retrieval order (LIFO, FIFO, ring-buffer, …).</li>
<li><code>Pool</code> treats stored buffers as fungible and does not depend on any particular ordering.</li>

> AGENT

Proposed commit message:

**Title:**
```
feat: Pool + BufRefStore + MutexLifo (0.5.0-1)
```

**Body:**
```
Implements the fixed-capacity buffer pool that RuntimeZC will
use for zerocopy messages. The free list lives behind a
`BufRefStore` trait so concurrency strategies can be swapped
and benchmarked; initial impl is `MutexLifo`. `Pool` and
`PooledMsg` are generic with `MutexLifo` as the default, so
ordinary call sites write `Pool::new(64, 128)` unchanged.

Shape deviates from the 0.5.0-0 plan (fixed-capacity
preallocation, `Result<_, PoolError>`, `Box<[u8]>` + separate
`len`, swappable store via the trait) — each deviation
recorded in the chores section's design-decisions sub-bullets.

- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: 0.5.0-0 → 0.5.0-1.
- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;`.
- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module — `BufRef` alias, `PoolError`, `BufRefStore` trait, `MutexLifo` impl, generic `Pool<S>` / `PooledMsg<S>`, 15 unit tests.
- `notes/chores-01.md`: new `## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)` section in intro + sub-bullet shape.
- `CLAUDE.md`: "Notes writing style" section retitled to "Writing style: prefer sub-bullets" and expanded to cover module `//!` and item `///` doc comments; clap-derive `verbatim_doc_comment` caveat added.
- `Cargo.lock`: regenerated for the version bump.
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs",
  "content": "//! Multi-threaded zerocopy runtime — the home of [`ActorZC`],\n//! [`ContextZC`], and [`RuntimeZC`].\n//!\n//! Shape:\n//!\n//! - 1:1 thread-per-actor; messages flow over per-actor\n//!   `mpsc` channels.\n//! - Channel payload is [`PooledMsg<S>`]; buffers come from a\n//!   shared [`Pool<S>`] held by the runtime and cloned to each\n//!   actor thread.\n//! - Handler signature is `fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx,\n//!   msg: &[u8])`. The runtime owns the inbound `PooledMsg`,\n//!   hands the handler an `&[u8]` view via `Deref`, and drops\n//!   the message after the handler returns — its buffer goes\n//!   back to the pool.\n//! - Actors fabricate replies via [`ContextZC::get_msg`] and\n//!   forward them with [`ContextZC::send`]. A failed send (peer\n//!   already shut down) drops the `PooledMsg` and returns the\n//!   buffer to the pool.\n//!\n//! Two run entry points:\n//!\n//! - [`RuntimeZC::run`] — `tprobe`-instrumented; returns\n//!   `(count, TProbe)` per actor. Used by the `goalzc` binary.\n//! - [`RuntimeZC::run_no_probe`] — probe-free; returns `count`\n//!   per actor. Used by the `goalzc-crit` criterion bench so\n//!   measurement is not contaminated by probe overhead.\n//!\n//! Lifecycle:\n//!\n//! - Caller registers actor factories via [`RuntimeZC::add_actor`].\n//! - Caller seeds the channel(s) with one or more\n//!   [`PooledMsg`]s via [`RuntimeZC::seed`].\n//! - `run` / `run_no_probe`:\n//!   - spawns one thread per actor (with optional CPU pin),\n//!   - delivers seeds, sleeps `warmup`,\n//!   - sends `ClearProbe` to every actor,\n//!   - sleeps `measurement`,\n//!   - sends `Shutdown` to every actor,\n//!   - drops main-thread senders, joins, returns results.\n//! - On shutdown, every in-flight `PooledMsg` drops back to\n//!   the pool — `pool.free_len() == pool.size()` after the\n//!   join completes.\n\nuse std::sync::mpsc::{self, Receiver, Sender};\nuse std::thread::{self, JoinHandle};\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse crate::pool::{BufRefStore, MutexLifo, Pool, PoolError, PooledMsg};\nuse tprobe::TProbe;\n\n/// Actor behavior in the zerocopy runtime.\n///\n/// - Single method: handle one inbound message at a time;\n///   never block.\n/// - `msg: &[u8]` is the runtime-owned [`PooledMsg`]'s slice\n///   view. The buffer returns to the pool after the handler\n///   returns.\n/// - Replies are fabricated via [`ContextZC::get_msg`] and\n///   forwarded with [`ContextZC::send`].\n/// - The trait requires `Send` so factories can ship actors\n///   into spawned threads.\npub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send {\n    /// Handle one inbound message. Use `ctx` to fabricate /\n    /// dispatch any outbound messages.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]);\n}\n\n/// Send-side handle the runtime supplies to each\n/// [`ActorZC::handle_message`] call.\n///\n/// - Carries access to the shared [`Pool<S>`] via\n///   [`ContextZC::get_msg`].\n/// - Dispatches outbound messages to other actors via\n///   [`ContextZC::send`].\n/// - Drops the `PooledMsg` if the destination channel is\n///   closed (peer already shut down) so the buffer never\n///   leaks.\npub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {\n    /// Acquire a buffer of length `size` from the pool.\n    /// Forwards [`PoolError`] on failure (`SizeTooLarge` /\n    /// `NoMsgs`); the actor handler decides how to react\n    /// (typically `expect` for ping-pong workloads).\n    fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>;\n\n    /// Send `msg` to the actor identified by `dst_id`. A\n    /// closed channel silently drops the message (matches the\n    /// shutdown semantics in `runtime.rs`'s [`Context`]); the\n    /// dropped `PooledMsg`'s buffer returns to the pool.\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>);\n}\n\n/// Boxed factory: produces a `Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>`\n/// once, called inside the actor's dedicated thread.\ntype ActorFactoryZC<S> = Box<dyn FnOnce() -> Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send> + Send>;\n\n/// Internal channel payload.\n///\n/// - `User(msg)` — caller-visible traffic.\n/// - `ClearProbe` — runtime injects at warmup → measurement\n///   boundary so the report only sees steady-state data.\n/// - `Shutdown` — runtime injects after the measurement\n///   window; actor exits its `recv` loop on receipt.\nenum SignalZC<S: BufRefStore> {\n    User(PooledMsg<S>),\n    ClearProbe,\n    Shutdown,\n}\n\n/// Multi-threaded zerocopy actor runtime.\n///\n/// - Generic over the store backend `S` so concurrency\n///   strategies can be swapped and benchmarked. Default\n///   [`MutexLifo`].\n/// - Owns the [`Pool<S>`] (cloned cheaply to each actor\n///   thread).\n/// - Two run entry points: `run` (tprobe-instrumented) and\n///   `run_no_probe` (probe-clean for criterion benches).\npub struct RuntimeZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {\n    factories: Vec<ActorFactoryZC<S>>,\n    probe_name_prefix: String,\n    pool: Pool<S>,\n    pending_seeds: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>,\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore + 'static> RuntimeZC<S> {\n    /// Build an empty runtime.\n    ///\n    /// - `probe_name_prefix` is combined with the actor id to\n    ///   form each per-thread probe's name (e.g.\n    ///   `\"goalzc.dispatch.actor0\"`).\n    /// - `pool` is the shared [`Pool<S>`] all actors will\n    ///   draw buffers from. The runtime stores a clone (cheap\n    ///   `Arc`); the caller keeps the original handle so it\n    ///   can inspect (`free_len`, `size`) or share with other\n    ///   consumers.\n    pub fn new(probe_name_prefix: &str, pool: Pool<S>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            factories: Vec::new(),\n            probe_name_prefix: probe_name_prefix.to_string(),\n            pool,\n            pending_seeds: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Borrow the runtime's pool handle — useful for tests or\n    /// code that wants to share the same pool for\n    /// out-of-band buffer allocation.\n    pub fn pool(&self) -> &Pool<S> {\n        &self.pool\n    }\n\n    /// Register an actor factory.\n    ///\n    /// - Returns the assigned id (0-indexed).\n    /// - `factory` is called inside the spawned thread that\n    ///   will drive this actor — the actor type itself does\n    ///   not need to be `Send` before construction; only the\n    ///   factory closure does.\n    pub fn add_actor<F, A>(&mut self, factory: F) -> u32\n    where\n        F: FnOnce() -> A + Send + 'static,\n        A: ActorZC<S> + Send + 'static,\n    {\n        let id = self.factories.len() as u32;\n        self.factories.push(Box::new(move || {\n            Box::new(factory()) as Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>\n        }));\n        id\n    }\n\n    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`.\n    ///\n    /// - Delivered after all threads spawn and before warmup\n    ///   begins.\n    /// - The runtime takes ownership of the [`PooledMsg`];\n    ///   when its handler eventually drops it, the buffer\n    ///   returns to the pool.\n    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>) {\n        self.pending_seeds.push((dst_id, msg));\n    }\n\n    /// Spawn / warmup / measure / shutdown with `tprobe`\n    /// instrumentation.\n    ///\n    /// - Each actor thread owns a [`TProbe`] recording per-\n    ///   `handle_message` latency at `inner=1`.\n    /// - Returns `(count, probe)` per actor in id order.\n    /// - Consumes the runtime's registered factories and\n    ///   pending seeds.\n    pub fn run(\n        &mut self,\n        warmup: Duration,\n        measurement: Duration,\n        pin_cores: &[usize],\n    ) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)> {\n        self.run_inner(warmup, measurement, pin_cores, actor_loop_probe::<S>)\n    }\n\n    /// Same orchestration as [`RuntimeZC::run`], minus the\n    /// per-message `TProbe` calls in the hot loop. Returns\n    /// the per-actor message count only.\n    ///\n    /// - Used by the `goalzc-crit` criterion bench so the\n    ///   measurement is probe-clean.\n    /// - The lesson learned from `goal2-crit`'s probe\n    ///   contamination: keeping a probe-free path on the\n    ///   runtime from the start avoids the trap of a bench\n    ///   that secretly measures `work + tprobe`.\n    pub fn run_no_probe(\n        &mut self,\n        warmup: Duration,\n        measurement: Duration,\n        pin_cores: &[usize],\n    ) -> Vec<u64> {\n        self.run_inner(warmup, measurement, pin_cores, actor_loop_no_probe::<S>)\n    }\n\n    /// Shared orchestration body: spawn N threads, deliver\n    /// seeds, run warmup → measurement → shutdown, join.\n    /// Per-thread work is delegated to `actor_loop`, a\n    /// function pointer so this method monomorphizes per\n    /// `R` without the closure / Fn-bound overhead.\n    fn run_inner<R: Send + 'static>(\n        &mut self,\n        warmup: Duration,\n        measurement: Duration,\n        pin_cores: &[usize],\n        actor_loop: ActorLoopFn<S, R>,\n    ) -> Vec<R> {\n        let factories = std::mem::take(&mut self.factories);\n        let seeds = std::mem::take(&mut self.pending_seeds);\n        let n = factories.len();\n        let pool = self.pool.clone();\n        let probe_name_prefix = self.probe_name_prefix.clone();\n\n        let mut senders: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        let mut receivers: Vec<Option<Receiver<SignalZC<S>>>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        for _ in 0..n {\n            let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();\n            senders.push(tx);\n            receivers.push(Some(rx));\n        }\n\n        let mut handles: Vec<JoinHandle<R>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        for (id, factory) in factories.into_iter().enumerate() {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: receivers[id] was set to `Some(...)` in the loop above.\n            let rx = receivers[id].take().unwrap();\n            let peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>> = senders.to_vec();\n            let probe_name = format!(\"{}.actor{}\", probe_name_prefix, id);\n            let pin_target = if pin_cores.is_empty() {\n                None\n            } else {\n                Some(pin_cores[id % pin_cores.len()])\n            };\n            let actor_pool = pool.clone();\n            let h = thread::Builder::new()\n                .name(format!(\"actor-zc-{id}\"))\n                .spawn(move || {\n                    tprobe::pin::pin_current(pin_target);\n                    let actor = factory();\n                    actor_loop(actor, rx, peers, actor_pool, probe_name)\n                })\n                .expect(\"failed to spawn actor thread\");\n            handles.push(h);\n        }\n\n        for (dst, msg) in seeds {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: receivers are alive (threads just spawned); a send\n            //   failure indicates an immediate thread panic at startup\n            //   which the join below surfaces with a clearer message.\n            senders[dst as usize].send(SignalZC::User(msg)).unwrap();\n        }\n\n        thread::sleep(warmup);\n        for tx in &senders {\n            let _ = tx.send(SignalZC::ClearProbe);\n        }\n\n        thread::sleep(measurement);\n        for tx in &senders {\n            let _ = tx.send(SignalZC::Shutdown);\n        }\n        drop(senders);\n\n        let mut results = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        for h in handles {\n            results.push(h.join().expect(\"actor thread panicked\"));\n        }\n        results\n    }\n}\n\n/// Function-pointer type for the per-thread actor loop, used\n/// by [`RuntimeZC::run_inner`]. `fn(...) -> R` is `Copy +\n/// Send + Sync`, which keeps the orchestration scaffold\n/// generic without closure / `Fn`-bound complications.\ntype ActorLoopFn<S, R> = fn(\n    Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,\n    Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,\n    Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,\n    Pool<S>,\n    String,\n) -> R;\n\n/// `ContextZC` impl used inside each actor thread.\n///\n/// - Holds borrowed references to the actor's peer senders\n///   and the shared pool — all live for the duration of\n///   `handle_message`.\n/// - `get_msg` delegates to [`Pool::get_msg`].\n/// - `send` ships a `PooledMsg` over the appropriate channel;\n///   on closed channel the message drops back to the pool.\nstruct MultiCtxZC<'a, S: BufRefStore> {\n    senders: &'a [Sender<SignalZC<S>>],\n    pool: &'a Pool<S>,\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore> ContextZC<S> for MultiCtxZC<'_, S> {\n    fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError> {\n        self.pool.get_msg(size)\n    }\n\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>) {\n        // Ignored Err contains the un-sent SignalZC::User(msg);\n        // dropping it returns the buffer to the pool.\n        let _ = self.senders[dst_id as usize].send(SignalZC::User(msg));\n    }\n}\n\n/// Per-thread loop with `tprobe` instrumentation.\n///\n/// - One scope per `handle_message` (`inner=1`).\n/// - `Signal::User` records.\n/// - `Signal::ClearProbe` resets probe + count.\n/// - `Signal::Shutdown` (or closed channel) exits.\nfn actor_loop_probe<S: BufRefStore + 'static>(\n    mut actor: Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,\n    rx: Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,\n    peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,\n    pool: Pool<S>,\n    probe_name: String,\n) -> (u64, TProbe) {\n    let mut probe = TProbe::new(&probe_name);\n    let mut count: u64 = 0;\n    loop {\n        match rx.recv() {\n            Ok(SignalZC::User(msg)) => {\n                let id = probe.start(0);\n                let mut ctx = MultiCtxZC {\n                    senders: &peers,\n                    pool: &pool,\n                };\n                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg);\n                probe.end(id);\n                drop(msg);\n                count += 1;\n            }\n            Ok(SignalZC::ClearProbe) => {\n                probe.clear();\n                count = 0;\n            }\n            Ok(SignalZC::Shutdown) | Err(_) => break,\n        }\n    }\n    (count, probe)\n}\n\n/// Per-thread loop with no probe involvement.\n///\n/// - Identical to [`actor_loop_probe`] minus `probe.start` /\n///   `probe.end` / `probe.clear`.\n/// - `_probe_name` is unused but kept in the signature so the\n///   function pointer matches [`ActorLoopFn`] without\n///   special-casing.\nfn actor_loop_no_probe<S: BufRefStore + 'static>(\n    mut actor: Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,\n    rx: Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,\n    peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,\n    pool: Pool<S>,\n    _probe_name: String,\n) -> u64 {\n    let mut count: u64 = 0;\n    loop {\n        match rx.recv() {\n            Ok(SignalZC::User(msg)) => {\n                let mut ctx = MultiCtxZC {\n                    senders: &peers,\n                    pool: &pool,\n                };\n                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg);\n                drop(msg);\n                count += 1;\n            }\n            Ok(SignalZC::ClearProbe) => {\n                count = 0;\n            }\n            Ok(SignalZC::Shutdown) | Err(_) => break,\n        }\n    }\n    count\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::pool::Pool;\n\n    /// Generic ping-pong actor reusable across `S` choices.\n    /// On each message: get a same-sized reply buffer, send\n    /// to the configured peer, drop the inbound `PooledMsg`\n    /// (returns its buffer to the pool).\n    struct PingPong {\n        peer: u32,\n    }\n\n    impl<S: BufRefStore> ActorZC<S> for PingPong {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]) {\n            let reply = ctx.get_msg(msg.len()).expect(\"get_msg should succeed\");\n            ctx.send(self.peer, reply);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Two-actor ping-pong drives traffic and shuts down\n    /// cleanly; both actors record nonzero counts.\n    #[test]\n    fn ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(\"test.zc\", pool.clone());\n        rt.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 1 });\n        rt.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 0 });\n        let seed = pool.get_msg(8).expect(\"seed\");\n        rt.seed(0, seed);\n        let results = rt.run(Duration::from_millis(40), Duration::from_millis(40), &[]);\n        assert_eq!(results.len(), 2);\n        let total: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();\n        assert!(\n            total > 0,\n            \"expected nonzero throughput, got count={total}\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// After `run` returns, every buffer the pool ever\n    /// handed out has come back. Tests the drain story\n    /// across the channel-close path.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_is_full_after_shutdown() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(\"test.drain\", pool.clone());\n        rt.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 1 });\n        rt.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 0 });\n        let seed = pool.get_msg(8).expect(\"seed\");\n        rt.seed(0, seed);\n        let _ = rt.run(Duration::from_millis(20), Duration::from_millis(20), &[]);\n        assert_eq!(\n            pool.free_len(),\n            pool.size() as usize,\n            \"all buffers should return to pool after shutdown\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// `run_no_probe` orchestrates identically and returns\n    /// per-actor counts (no probes). Validates that the\n    /// probe-clean path runs the same workload.\n    #[test]\n    fn run_no_probe_returns_counts() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(\"test.np\", pool.clone());\n        rt.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 1 });\n        rt.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 0 });\n        let seed = pool.get_msg(8).expect(\"seed\");\n        rt.seed(0, seed);\n        let results = rt.run_no_probe(Duration::from_millis(20), Duration::from_millis(20), &[]);\n        assert_eq!(results.len(), 2);\n        let total: u64 = results.iter().sum();\n        assert!(total > 0, \"expected nonzero throughput, got {total}\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            pool.free_len(),\n            pool.size() as usize,\n            \"all buffers should return to pool after no-probe shutdown\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Pool exposed via `RuntimeZC::pool` is the same handle\n    /// the caller passed in.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_accessor_returns_same_handle() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(32, 2);\n        let rt = RuntimeZC::new(\"test.pool-accessor\", pool.clone());\n        assert_eq!(rt.pool().msg_size(), 32);\n        assert_eq!(rt.pool().size(), 2);\n    }\n}\n"
}
```
