  "new_string": "- No `Default for Pool`.\n  - There's no sensible default for `msg_size` / `msg_count`; every caller has to choose.\n\n## RuntimeZC: zerocopy ping-pong runtime (0.5.0-2)\n\nAdds the multi-threaded zerocopy actor runtime that the\n`goalzc` binary and `goalzc-crit` bench will drive. Mirrors\n`MultiThreadRuntime` (1:1 thread-per-actor, mpsc channels),\nbut messages are pool-backed `PooledMsg<S>` rather than the\nunit `Message`. Two run entry points — `run` (tprobe-\ninstrumented) and `run_no_probe` (probe-clean) — apply the\ngoal2-crit lesson from the start.\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-1` → `0.5.0-2`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod runtime_zc;` added after `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send` — single method `handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8])`. Generic over the store backend with default `MutexLifo` so concrete actors can be written for one store or generic across stores.\n  - `pub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `get_msg(size) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` and `send(dst_id, msg: PooledMsg<S>)`. Sends to closed channels silently drop the buffer (returns to pool via `PooledMsg::Drop`).\n  - `pub struct RuntimeZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `{ factories, probe_name_prefix, pool: Pool<S>, pending_seeds }`.\n    - `RuntimeZC::new(probe_name_prefix: &str, pool: Pool<S>)` — caller brings the pool so it can also inspect `free_len` / `size` from the outside.\n    - `RuntimeZC::pool() -> &Pool<S>` — accessor for the same handle.\n    - `RuntimeZC::add_actor<F, A>(factory) -> u32` — closure-factory pattern (matches `MultiThreadRuntime`).\n    - `RuntimeZC::seed(dst_id, msg: PooledMsg<S>)` — runtime takes ownership; delivered after thread spawn, before warmup.\n    - `RuntimeZC::run(warmup, measurement, pin_cores)` — tprobe-instrumented; returns `Vec<(u64, TProbe)>`.\n    - `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe(warmup, measurement, pin_cores)` — probe-clean; returns `Vec<u64>`.\n  - Internal:\n    - `enum SignalZC<S> { User(PooledMsg<S>), ClearProbe, Shutdown }` — channel payload.\n    - `actor_loop_probe::<S>` / `actor_loop_no_probe::<S>` — the two per-thread loops.\n    - `RuntimeZC::run_inner<R>` — shared orchestration body; takes the actor-loop function pointer (`ActorLoopFn<S, R>`) so `run` / `run_no_probe` differ only in which fn-ptr they pass. Function pointers are `Copy + Send + Sync`, which keeps the orchestration generic without `Fn`-bound complications.\n    - `MultiCtxZC<'a, S>` — borrows peer senders + pool; impls `ContextZC<S>`.\n\n### Tests added (4 new in `runtime_zc::tests`, 26 total in lib)\n\n- `ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down` — two-actor ping-pong drives traffic for 40 ms each phase; both actors record nonzero counts.\n- `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` — after `run` returns, every buffer that was ever in flight has come back; `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.\n- `run_no_probe_returns_counts` — probe-clean path runs the same workload, returns counts only, drains the pool the same way.\n- `pool_accessor_returns_same_handle` — `RuntimeZC::pool()` exposes the caller's handle (verifies `msg_size` / `size`).\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- Generic `ActorZC<S>` / `ContextZC<S>` rather than type-erased buffer handles.\n  - Pool is generic over `S` since `0.5.0-1`; static dispatch all the way down preserves benchmark fidelity.\n  - Type-erasure (e.g. `Box<dyn AnyBuffer>` in `ContextZC::send`) would add a vtable hop per send, contaminating the very measurement we built `BufRefStore` to enable.\n  - Default `S = MutexLifo` keeps ergonomic call sites (`impl ActorZC for PingPong` works) while letting benchmarks parameterize.\n- Handler takes `msg: &[u8]`; runtime owns the inbound `PooledMsg<S>`.\n  - Matches the shape decided in the `0.5.0-0` plan and re-confirmed during pool review.\n  - Runtime's `actor_loop` calls `actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg)` then `drop(msg)` — the buffer returns to the pool one channel hop later than the visible handler line, but predictably and once per dispatch.\n- Two run methods rather than one with a `bool probe_enabled` parameter.\n  - A runtime branch on every dispatch would itself be a cost goalzc might want to measure or eliminate.\n  - Function-pointer dispatch (`ActorLoopFn`) is monomorphized per `R`, so the actor loops monomorphize cleanly without runtime branching.\n- Caller-supplied `Pool<S>` rather than runtime-built pool.\n  - Lets tests inspect `free_len` after shutdown to verify drain.\n  - Lets `goalzc-crit` keep the pool across iter_custom calls (long-lived) while the runtime is rebuilt each call.\n  - Matches the pattern of \"user knows the workload sizing\".\n- `Send` bound on `ActorZC` (not `ContextZC`).\n  - Actors get moved into spawned threads; must be `Send`.\n  - `ContextZC` lives only inside one thread (constructed per-recv inside `actor_loop`); no `Send` needed.\n- `let _ = sender.send(...)` in `MultiCtxZC::send` — closed channel drops the message silently.\n  - Matches `runtime.rs`'s `Context::send` shutdown semantics.\n  - The `Err(SendError(SignalZC::User(msg)))` case drops the `SignalZC`, which drops the inner `PooledMsg`, which returns the buffer to the pool — no leak.\n- Drain assertion as part of the test contract.\n  - `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` codifies \"every PooledMsg that was ever in flight returns to the pool by the time `run` joins\". This is non-obvious because it depends on:\n    - `Drop for PooledMsg` returning the buffer.\n    - mpsc receivers dropping their queued messages on close.\n    - main thread dropping its senders before joining.\n  - Codifying it now means a future change that breaks one of those steps gets caught immediately.\n\n## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness"
}

`0.5.0-2` complete. Summary:

- **`crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`** — `0.5.0-1 → 0.5.0-2`.

  - `pub trait ActorZC<S = MutexLifo>: Send` and `pub trait ContextZC<S = MutexLifo>` — generic over the store; default keeps simple call sites simple.
  - `pub struct RuntimeZC<S = MutexLifo>` — `new(probe_name_prefix, pool)`, `pool()`, `add_actor(factory)`, `seed(dst, msg)`, `run(...)` (tprobe), `run_no_probe(...)` (probe-clean).
  - Internal `actor_loop_probe` / `actor_loop_no_probe` selected via `ActorLoopFn` function-pointer in shared `run_inner`.
  - `MultiCtxZC` impls `ContextZC<S>`; failed sends drop `PooledMsg` back to the pool.
  - 4 unit tests: `ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down`, `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` (drain assertion), `run_no_probe_returns_counts`, `pool_accessor_returns_same_handle`.
- **`notes/chores-01.md`** — new `## RuntimeZC: zerocopy ping-pong runtime (0.5.0-2)` section in intro + sub-bullet shape with file-by-file, tests, and design decisions (generic vs type-erased, two run methods vs branch flag, caller-supplied pool, `Send` placement, drain assertion).
- **`Cargo.lock`** — regenerated for the version bump.

Pre-commit checklist: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --lib` 26 actor-x1 + 18 tprobe tests passing, `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1` succeeded, `goal1 --version` / `goal2 --version` both report `actor-x1 0.5.0-2`.

Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

> DEVELOPER

I don't like the notion of an ActorFactory that implies actors are similar, that happens to be the case for these trivial tests. I'd rather use a ActorManager model where you an user/bot creates an actor that executes some State Machine definition and adds gives an instance to the manager for other enties can then discover and connect with. I suspect the Manager will have a method based API but also be an Actor itself likely it'll be multiple actors for instance an actor for communicating to a GUI another for CLI another to drain logs or gather probe info ... So for now a simple method API for a simple ActorManager, thoughts.

> AGENT

Three things to think through, since this is a meaningful reshape of what's currently on disk:

### Rename + simpler registration

- `RuntimeZC` → `ActorManager` (no `ZC` suffix; the module name `runtime_zc` already contextualizes it, and `Pool` / `PooledMsg` already follow the no-suffix pattern).
- Drop the factory closure: `add_actor<F: FnOnce() -> A>(factory) -> u32` becomes `add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(actor: A) -> u32`. Caller constructs the actor and hands the instance over.
- `ActorFactoryZC` type alias goes away; storage becomes `Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>>`.

### Trade-off

- The factory pattern existed in `MultiThreadRuntime` because `Actor` (the non-ZC trait) doesn't require `Send` — so factories let actors be built inside their destination thread. `ActorZC` already requires `Send`, so the workaround buys nothing here. Direct registration is strictly more ergonomic for our case.
- One caveat: any future `ActorZC` impl that needs to be constructed *only* on its own thread (e.g. holds a `!Send` resource like a thread-local handle) won't fit. We don't have any today; if/when we do, an `add_with_factory` escape hatch can be added without changing existing callers.

### Future direction (noted, not built today)

- `ActorManager` itself becomes an `ActorZC` later — receives messages from sibling control-plane actors (GUI, CLI, log drainer, probe collector).
- Discovery/lookup beyond `u32` ids (named handles, type-indexed lookup, etc.) is also future work — today, the id returned by `add` is the discovery primitive.

### What I'll change in this `0.5.0-2` cut

- Rename `RuntimeZC` → `ActorManager` throughout `runtime_zc.rs`.
- Replace `add_actor` signature; tests use `mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 })`.

> DEVELOPER

I see ActorManager separate from the Runtime, the primary job of the Runtime is moving messages as efficiently as possible.  The ActorManager doesn't care about the Runtime other that what services it needs they need to interact with each other.

> AGENT

Got it — clean separation. Proposed shape:

### Two types

- **`RuntimeZC<S>`** — message-moving infrastructure.
  - Owns the [`Pool<S>`].
  - Spawns actor threads, wires channels, runs warmup/measure/shutdown.
  - Public surface:
    - `new(pool: Pool<S>) -> Self`
    - `pool() -> &Pool<S>`
    - `run(&mut self, mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>, warmup, measurement, pin_cores) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)>`
    - `run_no_probe(&mut self, mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>, warmup, measurement, pin_cores) -> Vec<u64>`
  - No knowledge of named actors, registration, or lifecycle beyond "actor is alive on a thread, drain its channel".

- **`ActorManager<S>`** — actor catalog / lifecycle.
  - Owns actor instances and pending seeds.
  - Public surface:
    - `new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self`
    - `add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(actor: A) -> u32` — direct registration; no factory.
    - `seed(dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>)`
    - Drain accessors used by the Runtime: `take_actors()`, `take_seeds()`, `probe_name_prefix()`.
  - No knowledge of threads, channels, or the pool. Doesn't even hold a Runtime reference.

### Interaction surface

- The user constructs both: `let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool); let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("...");`
- `rt.run(&mut mgr, ...)` is the verb. Runtime drains actors + seeds from the Manager, spawns threads, runs the lifecycle, returns per-actor results.
- That's the only contact point between them — the four `take_*` / `probe_name_prefix` accessors. If we later wanted a different Runtime backend (e.g. M:N scheduler), the Manager wouldn't change.

### User-facing flow

```rust
let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);
let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("goalzc.dispatch");
let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });
let b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });
let seed = pool.get_msg(8)?;
mgr.seed(a, seed);
let results = rt.run(&mut mgr, warmup, measurement, &[]);
```

### Future direction (noted, not built today)

- `ActorManager` itself becomes an `ActorZC` that handles control-plane messages (add new actor, lookup by name, drain probes, …).
- Multiple manager-actors for different concerns: GUI actor, CLI actor, log drainer, probe collector — all sit on the same Runtime as ordinary actors.
- Discovery beyond `u32` ids — name-based, type-indexed.

OK to refactor `runtime_zc.rs` along these lines for `0.5.0-2`?

The "app" decides what factory and what runtime to use. Those area all conflated in goal1 and goal2 but in goalzc we're exploring a more realistic model, may not work but that's how we learn.

> AGENT

Yes — pulling seeds out into the app makes the boundaries crisper. Proposed shape:

### `ActorManager<S>` (catalog only)

- Owns actor instances + the probe-name prefix. Nothing else.
- API:
  - `new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self`
  - `add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(actor: A) -> u32`
  - `take_actors() -> Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>>` (Runtime drains it at run time)
  - `probe_name_prefix() -> &str`
- Knows nothing about seeds, channels, threads, the pool, or what's being measured.

### `RuntimeZC<S>` (transport only)

- Owns the [`Pool<S>`].
- Spawns / channels / lifecycle. Knows nothing about seeds *as a concept* — they're just messages the app asks it to deliver.
- API:
  - `new(pool: Pool<S>) -> Self`
  - `pool() -> &Pool<S>`
  - `run(&mut self, mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>, initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>, warmup, measurement, pin_cores) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)>`
  - `run_no_probe(...)` — same shape, returns `Vec<u64>`.
- `initial_messages` is the neutral name; Runtime delivers them after spawn, before warmup, then forgets them.

### App-level composition (e.g. `goalzc.rs` later)

```rust
let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);
let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("goalzc.dispatch");
let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });
let _ = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });

let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(8)?)];
let results = rt.run(&mut mgr, initial, warmup, measurement, &[]);
```

The app is the only place that knows: "this is a ping-pong benchmark; kickstart it with one message".

### Future direction (noted, not built today)

- Closure-based init for richer post-spawn / pre-warmup work (`init: FnOnce(&Mailboxes<S>)`) if a workload ever needs more than a static list.
- `ActorManager` itself becomes an `ActorZC` for control-plane (GUI / CLI / log drain / probe collect) sub-actors.
- Discovery beyond `u32` ids — name-based, type-indexed.

OK to refactor `runtime_zc.rs` along these lines for `0.5.0-2`?

Initial messages are passed to [`RuntimeZC::run`] directly\n//! by the app; the Runtime delivers them after spawn / before\n//! warmup, then forgets them.\n//!\n//! Lifecycle inside `run` / `run_no_probe`:\n//!\n//! - Drain actors from the Manager.\n//! - Build per-actor channels.\n//! - Spawn one thread per actor, optionally pinned.\n//! - Deliver `initial_messages` over the senders.\n//! - Sleep `warmup`.\n//! - Send `ClearProbe` to every actor.\n//! - Sleep `measurement`.\n//! - Send `Shutdown` to every actor.\n//! - Drop main-thread senders so the channels close.\n//! - Join, return per-actor results.\n//!\n//! Drain invariant: by the time the join completes, every\n//! [`PooledMsg`] that was ever in flight has been dropped, so\n//! `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.\n\nuse std::sync::mpsc::{self, Receiver, Sender};\nuse std::thread::{self, JoinHandle};\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse crate::pool::{BufRefStore, MutexLifo, Pool, PoolError, PooledMsg};\nuse tprobe::TProbe;\n\n/// Actor behavior in the zerocopy runtime.\n///\n/// - Single method: handle one inbound message at a time;\n///   never block.\n/// - `msg: &[u8]` is the runtime-owned [`PooledMsg`]'s slice\n///   view. The buffer returns to the pool after the handler\n///   returns.\n/// - Replies are fabricated via [`ContextZC::get_msg`] and\n///   forwarded with [`ContextZC::send`].\n/// - The trait requires `Send` so actors can ship into\n///   spawned threads.\npub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send {\n    /// Handle one inbound message. Use `ctx` to fabricate /\n    /// dispatch any outbound messages.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]);\n}\n\n/// Send-side handle the runtime supplies to each\n/// [`ActorZC::handle_message`] call.\n///\n/// - Carries access to the shared [`Pool<S>`] via\n///   [`ContextZC::get_msg`].\n/// - Dispatches outbound messages to other actors via\n///   [`ContextZC::send`].\n/// - Drops the `PooledMsg` if the destination channel is\n///   closed (peer already shut down) so the buffer never\n///   leaks.\npub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {\n    /// Acquire a buffer of length `size` from the pool.\n    /// Forwards [`PoolError`] on failure (`SizeTooLarge` /\n    /// `NoMsgs`); the actor handler decides how to react\n    /// (typically `expect` for ping-pong workloads).\n    fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>;\n\n    /// Send `msg` to the actor identified by `dst_id`. A\n    /// closed channel silently drops the message; the dropped\n    /// `PooledMsg`'s buffer returns to the pool.\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>);\n}\n\n/// Internal channel payload.\n///\n/// - `User(msg)` — caller-visible traffic.\n/// - `ClearProbe` — runtime injects at warmup → measurement\n///   boundary so the report only sees steady-state data.\n/// - `Shutdown` — runtime injects after the measurement\n///   window; actor exits its `recv` loop on receipt.\nenum SignalZC<S: BufRefStore> {\n    User(PooledMsg<S>),\n    ClearProbe,\n    Shutdown,\n}\n\n/// Actor catalog — registration and lifecycle, nothing else.\n///\n/// - Holds actor instances and the probe-name prefix.\n/// - Hands out `u32` ids on `add`; that's the discovery\n///   primitive other actors use as `dst_id` in `Context::send`.\n/// - Drained by a [`RuntimeZC`] at run time via\n///   [`ActorManager::take_actors`]; single-shot for now.\n/// - Knows nothing about threads, channels, the pool, or\n///   what's being measured.\npub struct ActorManager<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {\n    actors: Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>>,\n    probe_name_prefix: String,\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore + 'static> ActorManager<S> {\n    /// Build an empty manager.\n    ///\n    /// `probe_name_prefix` is combined with each actor's id by\n    /// the runtime to form per-thread probe names (e.g.\n    /// `\"goalzc.dispatch.actor0\"`).\n    pub fn new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            actors: Vec::new(),\n            probe_name_prefix: probe_name_prefix.to_string(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register an actor instance.\n    ///\n    /// - Returns the assigned id (0-indexed; usable as\n    ///   `dst_id` in [`ContextZC::send`]).\n    /// - The actor must be `Send + 'static`. The trait already\n    ///   requires `Send`; `'static` is the usual no-borrow\n    ///   bound for moving across threads.\n    pub fn add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(&mut self, actor: A) -> u32 {\n        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;\n        self.actors.push(Box::new(actor));\n        id\n    }\n\n    /// Drain the registered actors, leaving the manager empty.\n    /// Called by [`RuntimeZC::run`] at the start of a run.\n    pub fn take_actors(&mut self) -> Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>> {\n        std::mem::take(&mut self.actors)\n    }\n\n    /// The probe-name prefix the runtime combines with each\n    /// actor's id to name its `TProbe`.\n    pub fn probe_name_prefix(&self) -> &str {\n        &self.probe_name_prefix\n    }\n}\n\n/// Multi-threaded zerocopy runtime — message-moving\n/// infrastructure.\n///\n/// - Owns the [`Pool<S>`] (cloned cheaply to each actor\n///   thread).\n/// - Spawns threads, wires channels, runs the warmup /\n///   measure / shutdown lifecycle.\n/// - Knows nothing about \"seeds\", named lookups, or what's\n///   being measured. &'a [Sender<SignalZC<S>>],\n    pool: &'a Pool<S>,\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore> ContextZC<S> for MultiCtxZC<'_, S> {\n    fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError> {\n ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());\n        let mut mgr = ActorManager::new(\"test.zc\");\n        let a Tests the drain story\n    /// across the channel-close path.\n    #[test]\n    fn pool_is_full_after_shutdown() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());\n        let mut mgr = ActorManager::new(\"test.drain\");\n        let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });\n        let _b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });\n        let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(8).expect(\"seed\"))];\n        let _ = rt.run(\n            &mut mgr,\n            initial,\n            Duration::from_millis(20),\n            Duration::from_millis(20),\n            &[],\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            pool.free_len(),\n            pool.size(),\n            \"all buffers should return to pool after shutdown\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// `run_no_probe` orchestrates identically and returns\n    /// per-actor counts (no probes). Validates that the\n    /// probe-clean path runs the same workload.\n    #[test]\n    fn run_no_probe_returns_counts() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);\n        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());\n        let mut mgr = ActorManager::new(\"test.np\");\n        let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });\n        let _b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });\n        let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(8).expect(\"seed\"))];\n        let results = rt.run_no_probe(\n            &mut mgr,\n            initial,\n            Duration::from_millis(20),\n            Duration::from_millis(20),\n            &[],\n        );\n        assert_eq!(results.len(), 2);\n        let total: u64 = results.iter().sum();\n        assert!(total > 0, \"expected nonzero throughput, got {total}\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            pool.free_len(),\n            pool.size(),\n            \"all buffers should return to pool after no-probe shutdown\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// `take_actors` drains the manager and resets it to\n    /// empty so a follow-up registration starts from id 0.\n    #[test]\n    fn take_actors_drains_and_resets() {\n        let mut mgr: ActorManager = ActorManager::new(\"test.drain-mgr\");\n        assert_eq!(mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 }), 0);\n        assert_eq!(mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 }), 1);\n        let drained = mgr.take_actors();\n        assert_eq!(drained.len(), 2);\n        // Manager is now empty; new registrations restart from 0.\n        assert_eq!(mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 99 }), 0);\n    }\n\n    /// `Runtime::pool` exposes the caller's handle; the\n    /// caller can build initial messages from it without\n    /// holding a separate clone.\n    #[test]\n    fn runtime_pool_accessor_returns_same_handle() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(32, 2);\n        let rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());\n        assert_eq!(rt.pool().msg_size(), 32);\n        assert_eq!(rt.pool().size(), 2);\n    }\n\n    /// The probe-name prefix surfaces in each actor's probe\n    /// name as `\"<prefix>.actor<id>\"`.\n    #[test]\n    fn probe_names_use_manager_prefix() {\n        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(32, 4);\n        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());\n        let mut mgr = ActorManager::new(\"custom.prefix\");\n        let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });\n        let _b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });\n        let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(4).expect(\"seed\"))];\n        let results = rt.run(\n            &mut mgr,\n            initial,\n            Duration::from_millis(10),\n            Duration::from_millis(10),\n            &[],\n        );\n        assert_eq!(results[0].1.name(), \"custom.prefix.actor0\");\n        assert_eq!(results[1].1.name(), \"custom.prefix.actor1\");\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

  "old_string": "## RuntimeZC: zerocopy ping-pong runtime (0.5.0-2)\n\nAdds the multi-threaded zerocopy actor runtime that the\n`goalzc` binary and `goalzc-crit` bench will drive. Mirrors\n`MultiThreadRuntime` (1:1 thread-per-actor, mpsc channels),\nbut messages are pool-backed `PooledMsg<S>` rather than the\nunit `Message`. Two run entry points — `run` (tprobe-\ninstrumented) and `run_no_probe` (probe-clean) — apply the\ngoal2-crit lesson from the start.\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-1` → `0.5.0-2`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod runtime_zc;` added after `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send` — single method `handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8])`. Generic over the store backend with default `MutexLifo` so concrete actors can be written for one store or generic across stores.\n  - `pub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `get_msg(size) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` and `send(dst_id, msg: PooledMsg<S>)`. Sends to closed channels silently drop the buffer (returns to pool via `PooledMsg::Drop`).\n  - `pub struct RuntimeZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `{ factories, probe_name_prefix, pool: Pool<S>, pending_seeds }`.\n    - `RuntimeZC::new(probe_name_prefix: &str, pool: Pool<S>)` — caller brings the pool so it can also inspect `free_len` / `size` from the outside.\n    - `RuntimeZC::pool() -> &Pool<S>` — accessor for the same handle.\n    - `RuntimeZC::add_actor<F, A>(factory) -> u32` — closure-factory pattern (matches `MultiThreadRuntime`).\n    - `RuntimeZC::seed(dst_id, msg: PooledMsg<S>)` — runtime takes ownership; delivered after thread spawn, before warmup.\n    - `RuntimeZC::run(warmup, measurement, pin_cores)` — tprobe-instrumented; returns `Vec<(u64, TProbe)>`.\n    - `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe(warmup, measurement, pin_cores)` — probe-clean; returns `Vec<u64>`.\n  - Internal:\n    - `enum SignalZC<S> { User(PooledMsg<S>), ClearProbe, Shutdown }` — channel payload.\n    - `actor_loop_probe::<S>` / `actor_loop_no_probe::<S>` — the two per-thread loops.\n    - `RuntimeZC::run_inner<R>` — shared orchestration body; takes the actor-loop function pointer (`ActorLoopFn<S, R>`) so `run` / `run_no_probe` differ only in which fn-ptr they pass. Function pointers are `Copy + Send + Sync`, which keeps the orchestration generic without `Fn`-bound complications.\n    - `MultiCtxZC<'a, S>` — borrows peer senders + pool; impls `ContextZC<S>`.\n\n### Tests added (4 new in `runtime_zc::tests`, 26 total in lib)\n\n- `ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down` — two-actor ping-pong drives traffic for 40 ms each phase; both actors record nonzero counts.\n- `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` — after `run` returns, every buffer that was ever in flight has come back; `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.\n- `run_no_probe_returns_counts` — probe-clean path runs the same workload, returns counts only, drains the pool the same way.\n- `pool_accessor_returns_same_handle` — `RuntimeZC::pool()` exposes the caller's handle (verifies `msg_size` / `size`).\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- Generic `ActorZC<S>` / `ContextZC<S>` rather than type-erased buffer handles.\n  - Pool is generic over `S` since `0.5.0-1`; static dispatch all the way down preserves benchmark fidelity.\n  - Type-erasure (e.g. `Box<dyn AnyBuffer>` in `ContextZC::send`) would add a vtable hop per send, contaminating the very measurement we built `BufRefStore` to enable.\n  - Default `S = MutexLifo` keeps ergonomic call sites (`impl ActorZC for PingPong` works) while letting benchmarks parameterize.\n- Handler takes `msg: &[u8]`; runtime owns the inbound `PooledMsg<S>`.\n  - Matches the shape decided in the `0.5.0-0` plan and re-confirmed during pool review.\n  - Runtime's `actor_loop` calls `actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg)` then `drop(msg)` — the buffer returns to the pool one channel hop later than the visible handler line, but predictably and once per dispatch.\n- Two run methods rather than one with a `bool probe_enabled` parameter.\n  - A runtime branch on every dispatch would itself be a cost goalzc might want to measure or eliminate.\n  - Function-pointer dispatch (`ActorLoopFn`) is monomorphized per `R`, so the actor loops monomorphize cleanly without runtime branching.\n- Caller-supplied `Pool<S>` rather than runtime-built pool.\n  - Lets tests inspect `free_len` after shutdown to verify drain.\n  - Lets `goalzc-crit` keep the pool across iter_custom calls (long-lived) while the runtime is rebuilt each call.\n  - Matches the pattern of \"user knows the workload sizing\".\n- `Send` bound on `ActorZC` (not `ContextZC`).\n  - Actors get moved into spawned threads; must be `Send`.\n  - `ContextZC` lives only inside one thread (constructed per-recv inside `actor_loop`); no `Send` needed.\n- `let _ = sender.send(...)` in `MultiCtxZC::send` — closed channel drops the message silently.\n  - Matches `runtime.rs`'s `Context::send` shutdown semantics.\n  - The `Err(SendError(SignalZC::User(msg)))` case drops the `SignalZC`, which drops the inner `PooledMsg`, which returns the buffer to the pool — no leak.\n- Drain assertion as part of the test contract.\n  - `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` codifies \"every PooledMsg that was ever in flight returns to the pool by the time `run` joins\". This is non-obvious because it depends on:\n    - `Drop for PooledMsg` returning the buffer.\n    - mpsc receivers dropping their queued messages on close.\n    - main thread dropping its senders before joining.\n  - Codifying it now means a future change that breaks one of those steps gets caught immediately.",
  "new_string": "## ActorManager + RuntimeZC: split catalog from transport (0.5.0-2)\n\nAdds the multi-threaded zerocopy actor runtime — split into\ntwo types with disjoint responsibilities — plus the\n`ActorZC` / `ContextZC` traits actors implement and use.\nThe split deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in three ways,\nall decided during review of an initial single-`RuntimeZC`\ncut:\n\n- Two types instead of one.\n  - `ActorManager<S>` — actor catalog. Holds instances and the probe-name prefix; assigns ids; nothing else.\n  - `RuntimeZC<S>` — message-moving infrastructure. Owns the pool, threads, channels.\n- Actors are registered as instances, not as factory closures.\n  - The `0.5.0-0` plan called for `add_actor<F: FnOnce() -> A>(factory)`. `MultiThreadRuntime` needs that pattern because its `Actor` trait isn't `Send`; `ActorZC` already requires `Send`, so direct instance registration is strictly more ergonomic and the factory bought nothing here.\n- Initial messages live in the app, not the runtime.\n  - The `0.5.0-0` plan had `RuntimeZC::seed`. Seeds are an app-level concern (this benchmark wants ping-pong; another might want a burst pattern); the Runtime just delivers what the app passes to `run`. Manager doesn't store them either — it has no notion of \"kickoff messages\".\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-1` → `0.5.0-2`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod runtime_zc;` added after `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send` — `fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8])`.\n  - `pub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `get_msg(size) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>`, `send(dst_id, msg: PooledMsg<S>)`. Send to closed channel silently drops the message (its `PooledMsg`'s buffer returns to the pool).\n  - `pub struct ActorManager<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — actor catalog.\n    - `new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self`.\n    - `add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(actor: A) -> u32` — instance registration, no factory.\n    - `take_actors() -> Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>>` — drained by Runtime at run time.\n    - `probe_name_prefix() -> &str`.\n  - `pub struct RuntimeZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — transport.\n    - `new(pool: Pool<S>) -> Self`.\n    - `pool() -> &Pool<S>`.\n    - `run(&mut self, mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>, initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>, warmup, measurement, pin_cores) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)>` — tprobe-instrumented.\n    - `run_no_probe(...)` — probe-clean; returns `Vec<u64>`.\n    - `run_inner<R>` — shared orchestration body taking an `ActorLoopFn<S, R>` function pointer; `run` / `run_no_probe` differ only in which fn-ptr they pass. Function pointers are `Copy + Send + Sync`, so the orchestration stays generic without closure / `Fn`-bound complications.\n  - Internal:\n    - `enum SignalZC<S> { User(PooledMsg<S>), ClearProbe, Shutdown }`.\n    - `actor_loop_probe::<S>` / `actor_loop_no_probe::<S>` — per-thread loops.\n    - `MultiCtxZC<'a, S>` — borrows peer senders + pool; impls `ContextZC<S>`.\n\n### Tests added (6 new in `runtime_zc::tests`, 28 total in lib)\n\n- `ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down` — two-actor ping-pong drives traffic for 40 ms each phase; both actors record nonzero counts.\n- `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` — after `run` returns, every buffer that was ever in flight has come back; `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.\n- `run_no_probe_returns_counts` — probe-clean path runs the same workload, returns counts only, drains the pool the same way.\n- `take_actors_drains_and_resets` — after `take_actors`, the manager is empty and follow-up `add` restarts ids from 0.\n- `runtime_pool_accessor_returns_same_handle` — `RuntimeZC::pool()` exposes the caller's handle (verifies `msg_size` / `size`).\n- `manager_exposes_probe_name_prefix` — verifies the prefix accessor; full `\"<prefix>.actor<id>\"` composition is verified end-to-end via `goalzc`'s output later (TProbe's name is private).\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- Two-type split: `ActorManager` (catalog) + `RuntimeZC` (transport).\n  - The runtime's job is \"move messages efficiently\"; nothing else.\n  - The manager's job is \"hold actor instances and assign ids\"; nothing else.\n  - The two only touch each other through `take_actors` + `probe_name_prefix` when `run` is called. A future swap of either side (M:N runtime, name-based discovery) leaves the other untouched.\n- Initial messages as a parameter to `run`, not state on either type.\n  - `run` neither stores them nor knows what they're for — it just delivers them after spawn / before warmup, then forgets.\n  - The benchmark / app is what knows \"ping-pong needs one seed message\"; that knowledge stays in the binary rather than leaking into runtime types.\n- Direct instance registration rather than factory closures.\n  - `ActorZC: Send` is already on the trait, so the factory pattern's \"don't require Send on the trait\" benefit is moot.\n  - `mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 })` reads cleaner than `mgr.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 1 })`.\n  - Future escape hatch (`add_with_factory(closure)`) is easy to add if a `!Send`-during-construction actor type ever appears.\n- Generic `ActorZC<S>` / `ContextZC<S>` over the store backend.\n  - Pool is generic over `S` since `0.5.0-1`; static dispatch all the way down preserves benchmark fidelity.\n  - Type-erasure in `ContextZC::send` would add a vtable hop per send, contaminating the measurement.\n  - Default `S = MutexLifo` keeps ergonomic call sites unannotated.\n- Handler takes `msg: &[u8]`; runtime owns the inbound `PooledMsg<S>`.\n  - The runtime's `actor_loop` calls `actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg)` then `drop(msg)` — buffer returns to the pool one channel hop later, predictably and once per dispatch.\n- Two run methods rather than one with a `bool probe_enabled` parameter.\n  - A runtime branch on every dispatch would itself be a cost goalzc might want to measure or eliminate.\n  - Function-pointer dispatch (`ActorLoopFn`) monomorphizes cleanly per `R`.\n- Caller-supplied `Pool<S>`.\n  - Lets the app build initial messages from the same pool the runtime uses.\n  - Lets tests inspect `free_len` after shutdown to verify drain.\n  - Lets `goalzc-crit` keep the pool across `iter_custom` calls (long-lived) while the runtime is rebuilt each call.\n- `Send` bound on `ActorZC` (not `ContextZC`).\n  - Actors get moved into spawned threads; must be `Send`.\n  - `ContextZC` lives only inside one thread (constructed per-recv inside `actor_loop`); no `Send` needed.\n- `let _ = sender.send(...)` in `MultiCtxZC::send` — closed channel drops the message silently.\n  - The `Err(SendError(SignalZC::User(msg)))` case drops the `SignalZC`, which drops the inner `PooledMsg`, which returns the buffer to the pool — no leak.\n- Drain assertion as part of the test contract.\n  - `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` codifies \"every PooledMsg that was ever in flight returns to the pool by the time `run` joins\".\n  - Non-obvious because it depends on `Drop for PooledMsg`, mpsc receivers dropping queued messages on close, and main-thread senders being dropped before join.\n  - Codifying it now means a future change that breaks one of those steps gets caught immediately.\n\n### Future direction (noted, not built today)\n\n- `ActorManager` itself becomes an `ActorZC` for control-plane work — a sibling actor on the same Runtime that handles \"register a new actor at runtime\", \"lookup by name\", \"drain probes\", etc.\n- Multiple manager-actors for separate concerns (GUI, CLI, log drain, probe collect) all running on one Runtime.\n- Discovery beyond `u32` ids — name-based, type-indexed.\n- Closure-based init for richer post-spawn / pre-warmup work (`init: FnOnce(&Mailboxes<S>)`) if a workload ever needs more than a static `initial_messages` list.\n- `add_with_factory` escape hatch if a `!Send`-during-construction actor type ever appears."
}

> AGENT

0.5.0-2 work is on disk. Summary of what changed:

**`crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`** — bumped 0.5.0-1 → 0.5.0-2.

**`crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`** — added `pub mod runtime_zc;`.

**`crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`** (new) — split cleanly into:
- `ActorManager<S>` — catalog only: `new(prefix)`, `add(actor)`, `take_actors()`, `probe_name_prefix()`.
- `RuntimeZC<S>` — transport only: `new(pool)`, `pool()`, `run(&mut mgr, initial_messages, warmup, measurement, pin_cores)`, `run_no_probe(...)`.
- Direct `add(actor)` registration — factory pattern dropped.
- `initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>` passed in by the app, not built by the runtime or manager.
- Internal plumbing: `SignalZC<S>::{User, ClearProbe, Shutdown}`, `actor_loop_probe` / `actor_loop_no_probe`, `MultiCtxZC`, `ActorLoopFn<S, R>` function pointer, shared `run_inner<R>`.
- 6 tests: `ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down`, `pool_is_full_after_shutdown`, `run_no_probe_returns_counts`, `take_actors_drains_and_resets`, `runtime_pool_accessor_returns_same_handle`, `manager_exposes_probe_name_prefix`.

**`notes/chores-01.md`** — new section "ActorManager + RuntimeZC: split catalog from transport (0.5.0-2)" describing the shipped shape and noting deviations from the 0.5.0-0 plan (two types vs one; instance registration; initial_messages owned by app).

Status: 28 lib tests passing, fmt/clippy clean, `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1` done, reinstalled binaries report 0.5.0-2.

Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

1962	## ActorManager + RuntimeZC: split catalog from transport (0.5.0-2)
1963	
1964	Adds the multi-threaded zerocopy actor runtime — split into
1965	two types with disjoint responsibilities — plus the
1966	`ActorZC` / `ContextZC` traits actors implement and use.
1967	The split deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in three ways,
1968	all decided during review of an initial single-`RuntimeZC`
1969	cut:
1970	
1971	- Two types instead of one.
1972	  - `ActorManager<S>` — actor catalog. Holds instances and the probe-name prefix; assigns ids; nothing else.
1973	  - `RuntimeZC<S>` — message-moving infrastructure. Owns the pool, threads, channels.
1974	- Actors are registered as instances, not as factory closures.
1975	  - The `0.5.0-0` plan called for `add_actor<F: FnOnce() -> A>(factory)`. `MultiThreadRuntime` needs that pattern because its `Actor` trait isn't `Send`; `ActorZC` already requires `Send`, so direct instance registration is strictly more ergonomic and the factory bought nothing here.
1976	- Initial messages live in the app, not the runtime.
1977	  - The `0.5.0-0` plan had `RuntimeZC::seed`. Seeds are an app-level concern (this benchmark wants ping-pong; another might want a burst pattern); the Runtime just delivers what the app passes to `run`. Manager doesn't store them either — it has no notion of "kickoff messages".
1978	
1979	### File-by-file
1980	
1981	- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-1` → `0.5.0-2`.
1982	- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod runtime_zc;` added after `pub mod runtime;`.
1983	- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`: new module.
1984	  - `pub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send` — `fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8])`.
1985	  - `pub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `get_msg(size) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>`, `send(dst_id, msg: PooledMsg<S>)`. Send to closed channel silently drops the message (its `PooledMsg`'s buffer returns to the pool).
1986	  - `pub struct ActorManager<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — actor catalog.
1987	    - `new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self`.
1988	    - `add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(actor: A) -> u32` — instance registration, no factory.
1989	    - `take_actors() -> Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>>` — drained by Runtime at run time.
1990	    - `probe_name_prefix() -> &str`.
1991	  - `pub struct RuntimeZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — transport.
1992	    - `new(pool: Pool<S>) -> Self`.
1993	    - `pool() -> &Pool<S>`.
1994	    - `run(&mut self, mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>, initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>, warmup, measurement, pin_cores) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)>` — tprobe-instrumented.
1995	    - `run_no_probe(...)` — probe-clean; returns `Vec<u64>`.
1996	    - `run_inner<R>` — shared orchestration body taking an `ActorLoopFn<S, R>` function pointer; `run` / `run_no_probe` differ only in which fn-ptr they pass. Function pointers are `Copy + Send + Sync`, so the orchestration stays generic without closure / `Fn`-bound complications.
1997	  - Internal:
1998	    - `enum SignalZC<S> { User(PooledMsg<S>), ClearProbe, Shutdown }`.
1999	    - `actor_loop_probe::<S>` / `actor_loop_no_probe::<S>` — per-thread loops.
2000	    - `MultiCtxZC<'a, S>` — borrows peer senders + pool; impls `ContextZC<S>`.
2001	
2002	### Tests added (6 new in `runtime_zc::tests`, 28 total in lib)
2003	
2004	- `ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down` — two-actor ping-pong drives traffic for 40 ms each phase; both actors record nonzero counts.
2005	- `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` — after `run` returns, every buffer that was ever in flight has come back; `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.
2006	- `run_no_probe_returns_counts` — probe-clean path runs the same workload, returns counts only, drains the pool the same way.
2007	- `take_actors_drains_and_resets` — after `take_actors`, the manager is empty and follow-up `add` restarts ids from 0.
2008	- `runtime_pool_accessor_returns_same_handle` — `RuntimeZC::pool()` exposes the caller's handle (verifies `msg_size` / `size`).
2009	- `manager_exposes_probe_name_prefix` — verifies the prefix accessor; full `"<prefix>.actor<id>"` composition is verified end-to-end via `goalzc`'s output later (TProbe's name is private).
2010	
2011	### Design decisions recorded here
2012	
2013	- Two-type split: `ActorManager` (catalog) + `RuntimeZC` (transport).
2014	  - The runtime's job is "move messages efficiently"; nothing else.
2015	  - The manager's job is "hold actor instances and assign ids"; nothing else.
2016	  - The two only touch each other through `take_actors` + `probe_name_prefix` when `run` is called. A future swap of either side (M:N runtime, name-based discovery) leaves the other untouched.
2017	- Initial messages as a parameter to `run`, not state on either type.
2018	  - `run` neither stores them nor knows what they're for — it just delivers them after spawn / before warmup, then forgets.
2019	  - The benchmark / app is what knows "ping-pong needs one seed message"; that knowledge stays in the binary rather than leaking into runtime types.
2020	- Direct instance registration rather than factory closures.
2021	  - `ActorZC: Send` is already on the trait, so the factory pattern's "don't require Send on the trait" benefit is moot.
2022	  - `mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 })` reads cleaner than `mgr.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 1 })`.
2023	  - Future escape hatch (`add_with_factory(closure)`) is easy to add if a `!Send`-during-construction actor type ever appears.
2024	- Generic `ActorZC<S>` / `ContextZC<S>` over the store backend.
2025	  - Pool is generic over `S` since `0.5.0-1`; static dispatch all the way down preserves benchmark fidelity.
2026	  - Type-erasure in `ContextZC::send` would add a vtable hop per send, contaminating the measurement.
2027	  - Default `S = MutexLifo` keeps ergonomic call sites unannotated.
2028	- Handler takes `msg: &[u8]`; runtime owns the inbound `PooledMsg<S>`.
2029	  - The runtime's `actor_loop` calls `actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg)` then `drop(msg)` — buffer returns to the pool one channel hop later, predictably and once per dispatch.
2030	- Two run methods rather than one with a `bool probe_enabled` parameter.
2031	  - A runtime branch on every dispatch would itself be a cost goalzc might want to measure or eliminate.
2032	  - Function-pointer dispatch (`ActorLoopFn`) monomorphizes cleanly per `R`.
2033	- Caller-supplied `Pool<S>`.
2034	  - Lets the app build initial messages from the same pool the runtime uses.
2035	  - Lets tests inspect `free_len` after shutdown to verify drain.
2036	  - Lets `goalzc-crit` keep the pool across `iter_custom` calls (long-lived) while the runtime is rebuilt each call.
2037	- `Send` bound on `ActorZC` (not `ContextZC`).
2038	  - Actors get moved into spawned threads; must be `Send`.
2039	  - `ContextZC` lives only inside one thread (constructed per-recv inside `actor_loop`); no `Send` needed.
2040	- `let _ = sender.send(...)` in `MultiCtxZC::send` — closed channel drops the message silently.
2041	  - The `Err(SendError(SignalZC::User(msg)))` case drops the `SignalZC`, which drops the inner `PooledMsg`, which returns the buffer to the pool — no leak.
2042	- Drain assertion as part of the test contract.
2043	  - `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` codifies "every PooledMsg that was ever in flight returns to the pool by the time `run` joins".
2044	  - Non-obvious because it depends on `Drop for PooledMsg`, mpsc receivers dropping queued messages on close, and main-thread senders being dropped before join.
2045	  - Codifying it now means a future change that breaks one of those steps gets caught immediately.
2046	
2047	### Future direction (noted, not built today)
2048	
2049	- `ActorManager` itself becomes an `ActorZC` for control-plane work — a sibling actor on the same Runtime that handles "register a new actor at runtime", "lookup by name", "drain probes", etc.
2050	- Multiple manager-actors for separate concerns (GUI, CLI, log drain, probe collect) all running on one Runtime.
2051	- Discovery beyond `u32` ids — name-based, type-indexed.
2052	- Closure-based init for richer post-spawn / pre-warmup work (`init: FnOnce(&Mailboxes<S>)`) if a workload ever needs more than a static `initial_messages` list.
2053	- `add_with_factory` escape hatch if a `!Send`-during-construction actor type ever appears.
2054	
2055	## Future work: linkme/inventory benchmark harness
2056	
2057	Idea captured during `0.3.0-0`; not scheduled for
2058	implementation yet. Explores using compile-time static
2059	registration (`linkme` distributed slices or `inventory` via
2060	`ctor`) to build a `tprobe`-driven benchmark runner where
2061	individual benchmarks declare themselves in separate files
2062	and the runner picks them up with no `main()` edits per
2063	benchmark — the ergonomic pattern criterion-rs offers.
2064	
2065	### Fit for benchmarks
2066	
2067	The bot thinks this is a strong fit. Each benchmark file
2068	drops in a `bench!(...)` invocation; the runner iterates
2069	the registered entries, sets up warmup/calibration/pin
2070	config once, and prints a `tprobe` band table per
2071	benchmark. The measurement primitive already exists
2072	(`tprobe`); the missing piece is the registration and
2073	iteration layer. Result: adding a benchmark is a
2074	one-file change with no central table to edit.
2075	
2076	### Fit for actor applications
2077	
2078	The bot thinks this is a partial fit. Registration gives
2079	discovery ("enumerate every `Actor` type the binary
2080	knows"), but actor construction still needs runtime config
2081	— id, name, channel endpoints, thread placement — which
```

1835	### Design decisions recorded here
1836	
1837	- Handler takes `&[u8]`; runtime owns the `PooledMsg`.
1838	  - Runtime receives `Signal::User(PooledMsg)` off the channel.
1839	  - Hands the handler `&*pooled` for the duration of `handle_message`.
1840	  - Drops the `PooledMsg` after the handler returns; buffer goes back to the pool.
1841	  - Pool traffic is one `get` + one `put` per dispatched message — exactly the cost goalzc exists to measure against goal2's unit-`Message` baseline.
1842	  - Passing `PooledMsg` by value into the handler would let a clever actor reuse the incoming buffer (avoiding pool churn).
1843	    - Rejected: `&[u8]` matches the shape of real zerocopy receivers and is the cleaner cross-validation target.
1844	- Fixed-size buffers (`MAX_SIZE`) for the MVP.

Yes — splitting at 0.5.0 makes sense. Reasoning:

- **Semantic boundary**: 0.1.0–0.4.0 is "establish baseline measurements" (Stage 1 runtime, tprobe extraction, band-table polish, criterion cross-check). 0.5.0+ is the zerocopy/pooled/`ActorZC` track — genuinely a different architectural direction. Different file fits.
- **chores-01 is conceptually closed at 0.4.0**: the "Measurement precision" and "Future work: linkme/inventory" sections (lines 1653 and 2055) belong with the earlier-arc material.
- **Low cross-ref churn**: only `notes/todo.md` ref `[20]` points into the 0.5.0 ladder; trivial to retarget. No code references either chores file.
- **chores-01 stops growing**: future 0.5.0-3, 0.5.0-4, 0.5.0 (close), and any 0.6.x lands in chores-02 cleanly without bloating the older record.

For trim *within* chores-01 (the historical sections): I'd be cautious. Those capture decision rationale and are the source-of-truth for "why did we do X six versions ago". Aggressively rewriting them risks losing context that the bullet structure is already holding compactly. The biggest cheap win is the split itself plus the 0.5.0-2 file-by-file drop we already discussed (which moves to chores-02 in trimmed form anyway).

**Proposed plan**:

1. **Create `notes/chores-02.md`** — short header naming its scope, then move `## goalzc + RuntimeZC ... (0.5.0-0)`, `## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)`, `## ActorManager + RuntimeZC ... (0.5.0-2)` from chores-01.
2. **Trim 0.5.0-2 during the move** — drop `### File-by-file`, tighten the intro, keep tests / design decisions / future direction.
3. **Refresh the stale `0.5.0-2` ladder line** inside the 0.5.0-0 plan marker section so it matches the shipped catalog/transport split.
4. **Update `notes/todo.md` ref [20]** from `chores-01.md#...` → `chores-02.md#...`.
5. Leave "Measurement precision" (line 1653) and "Future work: linkme/inventory" (line 2055) in chores-01 — both are about earlier-arc material.

This all lands as part of the 0.5.0-2 commit (it's the natural moment — the trim and the split serve the same review).

Sound good? If yes I'll make the changes and re-summarize for review.

1779	## goalzc + RuntimeZC: pooled zerocopy ping-pong — plan marker (0.5.0-0)
1780	
1781	Adds a multi-threaded ping-pong workload whose messages are
1782	pool-backed byte buffers, rather than the unit `Message` that
1783	goal1 / goal2 exchange. Actors handle `&[u8]`; callers can
1784	cast inside their handlers via the `zerocopy` crate if they
1785	want typed views. A companion `goalzc-crit` criterion bench
1786	measures exactly the same work, probe-free.
1787	
1788	### Shape
1789	
1790	- `Pool` + `PooledMsg` — new `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`.
1791	  - Buffers are fixed-size (`MAX_SIZE`) `Vec<u8>` owned by `PooledMsg`.
1792	  - `PooledMsg::Drop` returns the buffer to an `Arc<PoolInner>` shared across actor threads.
1793	  - Inner storage: `Mutex<Vec<Vec<u8>>>` — simple, some contention, upgradeable to lock-free later.
1794	  - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` so the handler's `&[u8]` is just `&*pooled`.
1795	  - Lazy allocation: pool starts empty; grows to steady-state (2 buffers for ping-pong) on demand.
1796	- `ActorZC` + `ContextZC` — new `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`.
1797	  - `trait ActorZC { fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC, msg: &[u8]); }`
1798	  - `trait ContextZC { fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> PooledMsg; fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg); }`
1799	- `RuntimeZC` — same file.
1800	  - 1:1 thread-per-actor, mirroring `MultiThreadRuntime`.
1801	  - Channel payload is `Signal::User(PooledMsg)`.
1802	  - Two entry points:
1803	    - `run(warmup, measurement, pins) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)>` — tprobe-instrumented; for `goalzc`.
1804	    - `run_no_probe(warmup, measurement, pins) -> Vec<u64>` — probe-free; for `goalzc-crit`.
1805	- `goalzc` binary — new `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goalzc.rs`.
1806	  - CLI mirrors `goal2`'s (`duration_s`, `--warmup`, `--ticks`, `--decimals`, `--pin`).
1807	  - Adds `--size N` — message byte size; default 64; validated `1..=MAX_SIZE`.
1808	  - Prints throughput + tprobe band-table per actor.
1809	- `goalzc-crit` bench — new `crates/actor-x1/benches/goalzc-crit.rs`.
1810	  - Criterion `iter_custom` over `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe`.
1811	  - Reuses the `measurement * iters / total_count` scaling trick from `goal2-crit`.
1812	  - `Throughput::Elements(1)`.
1813	
1814	### Multi-step ladder
1815	
1816	- `0.5.0-0` — Plan marker.
1817	  - Bump `actor-x1` to `0.5.0-0`.
1818	  - This chores section.
1819	  - `notes/todo.md` entry under `## In Progress`.
1820	  - `CLAUDE.md`: add "Notes writing style: prefer sub-bullets" section (captures in-session feedback).
1821	  - No code change.
1822	- `0.5.0-1` — `pool.rs`: `Pool` + `PooledMsg` with `Drop` return path.
1823	  - Unit tests: round-trip, drop-returns, size enforcement.
1824	- `0.5.0-2` — `runtime_zc.rs`: `ActorZC` / `ContextZC` + `RuntimeZC` with both `run` and `run_no_probe`.
1825	  - Unit tests: ping-pong handling, clean shutdown, pool drain on exit.
1826	- `0.5.0-3` — `bin/goalzc.rs`.
1827	  - Install + smoke-run.
1828	- `0.5.0-4` — `benches/goalzc-crit.rs`.
1829	  - `cargo bench` + spot-check throughput matches `goalzc`'s.
1830	- `0.5.0` — Closing marker.
1831	  - Drop `-N` suffix.
1832	  - Update `crates/actor-x1/README.md` Binaries + Benches.
1833	  - Move `notes/todo.md` entries to `## Done`.
1834	
1835	### Design decisions recorded here
1836	
1837	- Handler takes `&[u8]`; runtime owns the `PooledMsg`.
1838	  - Runtime receives `Signal::User(PooledMsg)` off the channel.
1839	  - Hands the handler `&*pooled` for the duration of `handle_message`.
1840	  - Drops the `PooledMsg` after the handler returns; buffer goes back to the pool.
1841	  - Pool traffic is one `get` + one `put` per dispatched message — exactly the cost goalzc exists to measure against goal2's unit-`Message` baseline.
1842	  - Passing `PooledMsg` by value into the handler would let a clever actor reuse the incoming buffer (avoiding pool churn).
1843	    - Rejected: `&[u8]` matches the shape of real zerocopy receivers and is the cleaner cross-validation target.
1844	- Fixed-size buffers (`MAX_SIZE`) for the MVP.
1845	  - Simpler pool (`Vec<Vec<u8>>`).
1846	  - Matches a ping-pong workload where every message is the same size.
1847	  - Size-classed sub-pools are future work; mentioned here explicitly so the extension isn't lost.
1848	- Lazy pool allocation.
1849	  - Pool starts empty; `get_msg` allocates a fresh `Vec<u8>` if the free list is empty.
1850	  - Steady state settles to 2 buffers for ping-pong after the first couple of dispatches.
1851	  - Pre-populating a fixed capacity is a micro-optimization that distorts first-dispatch latency (already dropped by warmup); deferred.
1852	- `--size N` CLI flag, not a compile-time const.
1853	  - goalzc is a benchmark; size sensitivity is exactly what a reader wants to sweep.
1854	  - `N` is validated against `MAX_SIZE` at startup.
1855	  - If `N > MAX_SIZE`, the binary errors cleanly rather than silently truncating.
1856	- `RuntimeZC` is a parallel runtime, not a replacement.
1857	  - `MultiThreadRuntime` stays untouched — existing `goal2` / `goal2-crit` keep working.
1858	  - `RuntimeZC` lives in its own module; no shared code with `MultiThreadRuntime`.
1859	  - Duplication is the price for a clean split that's easy to revisit once the shape stabilizes.
1860	  - The goal2-crit probe-contamination bug stays un-patched in `MultiThreadRuntime` for now; `run_no_probe` exists here from the start instead.
1861	- Multi-step `0.5.0` ladder.
1862	  - Five natural checkpoints: pool, runtime, bin, bench, close.
1863	  - Each reviewable on its own.
1864	  - A single-step `0.5.0` would be a large diff to review in one pass.
1865	
1866	## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)
1867	
1868	Implements the fixed-capacity buffer pool that `RuntimeZC`

1866	## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)
1867	
1868	Implements the fixed-capacity buffer pool that `RuntimeZC`
1869	will use to ship zerocopy messages between actors. The
1870	shipped shape deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in several
1871	ways — all decided during review of successive design cuts:
1872	
1873	- Constructor takes explicit byte / count: `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)`. Pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of `msg_size` bytes each; no lazy growth, no global `MAX_SIZE` const.
1874	- `get_msg` returns `Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` rather than panicking. Two variants: `SizeTooLarge { requested, max }` and `NoMsgs`.
1875	- Buffers are `Box<[u8]>` (fixed-size allocation) plus a separate `len: usize` on `PooledMsg` for the logical message length, instead of `Vec<u8>`'s redundant `cap` field.
1876	- The free list lives behind a `BufRefStore` trait so different concurrency strategies (Mutex, lock-free queue, ring buffer, hand-rolled atomics, …) can be benchmarked against each other. The default is `MutexLifo`.
1877	- `Pool` and `PooledMsg` are generic over `S: BufRefStore`, with `MutexLifo` as the default type — ordinary call sites write `Pool::new(64, 128)` unchanged.
1878	- A `BufRef` type alias documents the `Option<Box<[u8]>>` niche-optimization idiom in one place.
1879	
1880	### File-by-file
1881	
1882	- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-0` → `0.5.0-1`.
1883	- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;` added before `pub mod runtime;`.
1884	- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module.
1885	  - `pub type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;` — module-level alias documenting the niche-optimized "null or non-null pointer" pattern.
1886	  - `pub enum PoolError { SizeTooLarge { requested, max }, NoMsgs }` — `Display` + `std::error::Error` impls.
1887	  - `pub trait BufRefStore: Send + Sync` — `from_buffers`, `get`, `ret`, `len`, `size`, `is_empty` (default-impl over `len`).
1888	  - `pub struct MutexLifo` — initial impl: `Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>` + cached `size: usize` for O(1) lock-free `size()`.
1889	  - `pub struct Pool<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `Arc<PoolInner<S>>` handle; manual `Clone` impl avoids spurious `S: Clone` bound.
1890	    - `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)` — allocates buffers, calls `S::from_buffers`.
1891	    - `Pool::msg_size() -> u32` — bytes per buffer.
1892	    - `Pool::size() -> usize` — total buffer capacity (delegates to the store; will sum across sub-stores once multi-size pools land).
1893	    - `Pool::get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` — validates `size`, pops via `S::get`, returns the wrapped buffer.
1894	    - `Pool::free_len()` — `#[cfg(test)]` only.
1895	  - `pub struct PooledMsg<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `{ buf: BufRef, len: usize, pool: Arc<PoolInner<S>> }`.
1896	    - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` / `DerefMut` — slice the underlying `Box<[u8]>` to `[..len]`.
1897	    - `Drop` — `S::ret(self.buf.take().unwrap())` returns the buffer to the store.
1898	
1899	### Tests added (all `#[cfg(test)]` in `pool.rs`, 15 total)
1900	
1901	- `new_reports_msg_size_and_size` — fresh pool reports `msg_size`, `size`, and free-list `len`.
1902	- `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` — `size()` stays at `msg_count` while in-flight buffers reduce `len()`.
1903	- `get_msg_returns_requested_size` — `len()` of the returned slice matches the requested size.
1904	- `get_msg_at_msg_size_ok` — `size == msg_size` boundary works.
1905	- `get_msg_zero_size_ok` — `size == 0` yields an empty slice.
1906	- `get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large` — error carries `requested` + `max` correctly.
1907	- `get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs` — after all slots are in flight, `get_msg` yields `NoMsgs`.
1908	- `zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs` — `Pool::new(_, 0)` always errors; sanity check for the zero edge.
1909	- `drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds` — dropping an in-flight msg re-opens a slot.
1910	- `buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get` — pointer equality survives a get / drop / get cycle (LIFO, no realloc).
1911	- `clone_pool_shares_free_list` — drop on one `Pool` clone is visible from another.
1912	- `writes_round_trip` — `DerefMut` writes are visible through `Deref`.
1913	- `pool_works_across_threads` — `Pool` cloned to a worker thread; buffer dropped on worker returns to the shared free list.
1914	- `pool_error_display` — both variants render readable one-liners.
1915	- `mutex_lifo_trait_contract` — `BufRefStore` contract direct on `MutexLifo` without a `Pool` wrapper: `from_buffers` / `get` / `ret` / `len` / `size` round-trip end-to-end.
1916	
1917	### Design decisions recorded here
1918	
1919	- `BufRefStore` trait + generic `Pool<S>` rather than a hardcoded backend.
1920	  - Primary motivation: the user wants to swap concurrency strategies and benchmark them — Mutex baseline today, lock-free `ArrayQueue`, ring buffers, hand-rolled atomics later.
1921	  - Generic-with-default (`S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo`) keeps the common call site unchanged (`Pool::new(64, 128)`) while letting benchmarks opt into other backends with `Pool::<X>::new(64, 128)`.
1922	  - Trait surface stays minimal: `from_buffers` / `get` / `ret` / `len` / `size`. No "ordering" or "is_lockfree" methods; ordering is implementation-defined and `Pool` treats buffers as fungible.
1923	  - Dyn-dispatch via `Box<dyn BufRefStore>` was considered and rejected for benchmark fidelity — a `dyn` indirection in the hot path would contaminate the very thing we're trying to compare.
1924	- `BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>` alias.
1925	  - Documents the "safe null/non-null pointer" idiom in one place rather than re-explaining at each use site.
1926	  - Used in `PooledMsg.buf` (nullable transiently during `Drop::drop`) and as `BufRefStore::get`'s return (signaling empty store).
1927	- Fixed-capacity pre-allocation rather than lazy growth.
1928	  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's "starts empty, grows on demand" design.
1929	  - Makes `NoMsgs` a real, testable condition — back-pressure is exposed instead of hidden behind silent allocation.
1930	  - Steady-state behavior for ping-pong is unchanged (two buffers cycling).
1931	- `Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` rather than panic on misuse.
1932	  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's panic on `size > MAX_SIZE`.
1933	  - `NoMsgs` is a runtime condition; a benchmark hitting exhaustion should not crash, it should report saturation.
1934	  - `SizeTooLarge` follows the same shape for one error path.
1935	- `Box<[u8]>` + separate `len` rather than `Vec<u8>`.
1936	  - `Vec`'s `cap` field is dead weight when capacity is fixed at `msg_size`; `Box<[u8]>` is `(ptr, len)` only.
1937	  - `Vec` also implies "growable", which is misleading here.
1938	  - `len` lives on `PooledMsg` instead of in the store, so the store treats buffers as identical fixed-size allocations.
1939	- `Option<Box<[u8]>>` inside `PooledMsg` so `Drop` can move the inner `Box` out.
1940	  - Niche-optimized to a single pointer at runtime (no discriminant tag).
1941	  - `None` only observed transiently inside `Drop::drop`; `Deref` / `DerefMut` can't see it.
1942	  - `unsafe` alternatives (`ManuallyDrop` + a destructor) would shave the `unwrap`s but add unsafety; not worth it.
1943	- `BufRefStore::size()` returns the constant store capacity.
1944	  - Distinct from `len()` (current count), which fluctuates as buffers go in flight.
1945	  - Cached at `from_buffers` time so `MutexLifo::size()` is O(1) lock-free.
1946	  - Test `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` validates the invariant.
1947	  - Future-proofs the multi-size sub-pool extension: `Pool::size()` already sums "across sub-stores" (one for now).
1948	- `msg_size` / `msg_count` typed as `u32`.
1949	  - Matches the user's requested shape: explicit byte-range and count limits at the type level.
1950	  - Internally converted to `usize` for `Vec` / slice APIs; widening on every platform actor-x1 targets.
1951	  - `get_msg(size: usize)` stays `usize` for clean interop with slice/`Vec` lengths; if `size > u32::MAX` the `SizeTooLarge` guard catches it.
1952	- `is_empty` as a default-impl trait method.
1953	  - Required by clippy's `len_without_is_empty` lint when a type / trait exposes `len()`.
1954	  - Default body `len() == 0` is correct for any conforming impl; concrete impls can override if they have a faster path.
1955	- No zeroing on drop.
1956	  - Slots `0..new_size` of a reused buffer carry residual bytes from the previous occupant.
1957	  - For ping-pong workloads the next consumer either overwrites or ignores those bytes — benign.
1958	  - Real applications crossing trust domains would need an explicit `zeroize`-on-drop step; documented in the module-level comment.
1959	- No `Default for Pool`.
1960	  - There's no sensible default for `msg_size` / `msg_count`; every caller has to choose.
1961

1964	Adds the multi-threaded zerocopy actor runtime — split into
1965	two types with disjoint responsibilities — plus the

grows to steady-state (2 buffers for ping-pong) on demand.\n- `ActorZC` + `ContextZC` — new `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`.\n  - `trait ActorZC { fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC, msg: &[u8]);
