for `goalzc`.\n    - `run_no_probe(warmup, measurement, pins) -> Vec<u64>` — probe-free; for `goalzc-crit`.\n- `goalzc` binary — new `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goalzc.rs`.\n  - CLI mirrors `goal2`'s (`duration_s`, `--warmup`, `--ticks`, `--decimals`, `--pin`).\n  - Adds `--size N` — message byte size; default 64; validated `1..=MAX_SIZE`.\n  - Prints throughput + tprobe band-table per actor.\n- `goalzc-crit` bench — new `crates/actor-x1/benches/goalzc-crit.rs`.\n  - Criterion `iter_custom` over `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe`.\n  - Reuses the `measurement * iters / total_count` scaling trick from `goal2-crit`.\n  - `Throughput::Elements(1)`.\n\n### Multi-step ladder\n\n- `0.5.0-0` — Plan marker.\n  - Bump `actor-x1` to `0.5.0-0`.\n  - This chores section.\n  - `notes/todo.md` entry under `## In Progress`.\n  - `CLAUDE.md`: add \"Notes writing style: prefer sub-bullets\" section (captures in-session feedback).\n  - No code change.\n- `0.5.0-1` — `pool.rs`: `Pool` + `PooledMsg` with `Drop` return path.\n  - Unit tests: round-trip, drop-returns, size enforcement.\n- `0.5.0-2` — `runtime_zc.rs`: `ActorManager` + `RuntimeZC` (catalog/transport split), `ActorZC` / `ContextZC` traits, `run` + `run_no_probe`.\n  - Unit tests: ping-pong handling, clean shutdown, pool drain on exit.\n- `0.5.0-3` — `bin/goalzc.rs`.\n  - Install + smoke-run.\n- `0.5.0-4` — `benches/goalzc-crit.rs`.\n  - `cargo bench` + spot-check throughput matches `goalzc`'s.\n- `0.5.0` — Closing marker.\n  - Drop `-N` suffix.\n  - Update `crates/actor-x1/README.md` Binaries + Benches.\n  - Move `notes/todo.md` entries to `## Done`.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- Handler takes `&[u8]`; runtime owns the `PooledMsg`.\n  - Runtime receives `Signal::User(PooledMsg)` off the channel.\n  - Hands the handler `&*pooled` for the duration of `handle_message`.\n  - Drops the `PooledMsg` after the handler returns; buffer goes back to the pool.\n  - Pool traffic is one `get` + one `put` per dispatched message — exactly the cost goalzc exists to measure against goal2's unit-`Message` baseline.\n  - Passing `PooledMsg` by value into the handler would let a clever actor reuse the incoming buffer (avoiding pool churn).\n    - Rejected: `&[u8]` matches the shape of real zerocopy receivers and is the cleaner cross-validation target.\n- Fixed-size buffers (`MAX_SIZE`) for the MVP.\n  - Simpler pool (`Vec<Vec<u8>>`).\n  - Matches a ping-pong workload where every message is the same size.\n  - Size-classed sub-pools are future work; mentioned here explicitly so the extension isn't lost.\n- Lazy pool allocation.\n  - Pool starts empty; `get_msg` allocates a fresh `Vec<u8>` if the free list is empty.\n  - Steady state settles to 2 buffers for ping-pong after the first couple of dispatches.\n  - Pre-populating a fixed capacity is a micro-optimization that distorts first-dispatch latency (already dropped by warmup); deferred.\n- `--size N` CLI flag, not a compile-time const.\n  - goalzc is a benchmark; size sensitivity is exactly what a reader wants to sweep.\n  - `N` is validated against `MAX_SIZE` at startup.\n  - If `N > MAX_SIZE`, the binary errors cleanly rather than silently truncating.\n- `RuntimeZC` is a parallel runtime, not a replacement.\n  - `MultiThreadRuntime` stays untouched — existing `goal2` / `goal2-crit` keep working.\n  - `RuntimeZC` lives in its own module; no shared code with `MultiThreadRuntime`.\n  - Duplication is the price for a clean split that's easy to revisit once the shape stabilizes.\n  - The goal2-crit probe-contamination bug stays un-patched in `MultiThreadRuntime` for now; `run_no_probe` exists here from the start instead.\n- Multi-step `0.5.0` ladder.\n  - Five natural checkpoints: pool, runtime, bin, bench, close.\n  - Each reviewable on its own.\n  - A single-step `0.5.0` would be a large diff to review in one pass.\n\n## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)\n\nImplements the fixed-capacity buffer pool that `RuntimeZC`\nwill use to ship zerocopy messages between actors. The\nshipped shape deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in several\nways — all decided during review of successive design cuts:\n\n- Constructor takes explicit byte / count: `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)`. Pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of `msg_size` bytes each; no lazy growth, no global `MAX_SIZE` const.\n- `get_msg` returns `Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` rather than panicking. Two variants: `SizeTooLarge { requested, max }` and `NoMsgs`.\n- Buffers are `Box<[u8]>` (fixed-size allocation) plus a separate `len: usize` on `PooledMsg` for the logical message length, instead of `Vec<u8>`'s redundant `cap` field.\n- The free list lives behind a `BufRefStore` trait so different concurrency strategies (Mutex, lock-free queue, ring buffer, hand-rolled atomics, …) can be benchmarked against each other. The default is `MutexLifo`.\n- `Pool` and `PooledMsg` are generic over `S: BufRefStore`, with `MutexLifo` as the default type — ordinary call sites write `Pool::new(64, 128)` unchanged.\n- A `BufRef` type alias documents the `Option<Box<[u8]>>` niche-optimization idiom in one place.\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-0` → `0.5.0-1`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;` added before `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;` — module-level alias documenting the niche-optimized \"null or non-null pointer\" pattern.\n  - `pub enum PoolError { SizeTooLarge { requested, max }, NoMsgs }` — `Display` + `std::error::Error` impls.\n  - `pub trait BufRefStore: Send + Sync` — `from_buffers`, `get`, `ret`, `len`, `size`, `is_empty` (default-impl over `len`).\n  - `pub struct MutexLifo` — initial impl: `Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>` + cached `size: usize` for O(1) lock-free `size()`.\n  - `pub struct Pool<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `Arc<PoolInner<S>>` handle; manual `Clone` impl avoids spurious `S: Clone` bound.\n    - `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)` — allocates buffers, calls `S::from_buffers`.\n    - `Pool::msg_size() -> u32` — bytes per buffer.\n    - `Pool::size() -> usize` — total buffer capacity (delegates to the store; will sum across sub-stores once multi-size pools land).\n    - `Pool::get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` — validates `size`, pops via `S::get`, returns the wrapped buffer.\n    - `Pool::free_len()` — `#[cfg(test)]` only.\n  - `pub struct PooledMsg<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `{ buf: BufRef, len: usize, pool: Arc<PoolInner<S>> }`.\n    - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` / `DerefMut` — slice the underlying `Box<[u8]>` to `[..len]`.\n    - `Drop` — `S::ret(self.buf.take().unwrap())` returns the buffer to the store.\n\n### Tests added (all `#[cfg(test)]` in `pool.rs`, 15 total)\n\n- `new_reports_msg_size_and_size` — fresh pool reports `msg_size`, `size`, and free-list `len`.\n- `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` — `size()` stays at `msg_count` while in-flight buffers reduce `len()`.\n- `get_msg_returns_requested_size` — `len()` of the returned slice matches the requested size.\n- `get_msg_at_msg_size_ok` — `size == msg_size` boundary works.\n- `get_msg_zero_size_ok` — `size == 0` yields an empty slice.\n- `get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large` — error carries `requested` + `max` correctly.\n- `get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs` — after all slots are in flight, `get_msg` yields `NoMsgs`.\n- `zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs` — `Pool::new(_, 0)` always errors; sanity check for the zero edge.\n- `drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds` — dropping an in-flight msg re-opens a slot.\n- `buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get` — pointer equality survives a get / drop / get cycle (LIFO, no realloc).\n- `clone_pool_shares_free_list` — drop on one `Pool` clone is visible from another.\n- `writes_round_trip` — `DerefMut` writes are visible through `Deref`.\n- `pool_works_across_threads` — `Pool` cloned to a worker thread; buffer dropped on worker returns to the shared free list.\n- `pool_error_display` — both variants render readable one-liners.\n- `mutex_lifo_trait_contract` — `BufRefStore` contract direct on `MutexLifo` without a `Pool` wrapper: `from_buffers` / `get` / `ret` / `len` / `size` round-trip end-to-end.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- `BufRefStore` trait + generic `Pool<S>` rather than a hardcoded backend.\n  - Primary motivation: the user wants to swap concurrency strategies and benchmark them — Mutex baseline today, lock-free `ArrayQueue`, ring buffers, hand-rolled atomics later.\n  - Generic-with-default (`S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo`) keeps the common call site unchanged (`Pool::new(64, 128)`) while letting benchmarks opt into other backends with `Pool::<X>::new(64, 128)`.\n  - Trait surface stays minimal: `from_buffers` / `get` / `ret` / `len` / `size`. No \"ordering\" or \"is_lockfree\" methods; ordering is implementation-defined and `Pool` treats buffers as fungible.\n  - Dyn-dispatch via `Box<dyn BufRefStore>` was considered and rejected for benchmark fidelity — a `dyn` indirection in the hot path would contaminate the very thing we're trying to compare.\n- `BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>` alias.\n  - Documents the \"safe null/non-null pointer\" idiom in one place rather than re-explaining at each use site.\n  - Used in `PooledMsg.buf` (nullable transiently during `Drop::drop`) and as `BufRefStore::get`'s return (signaling empty store).\n- Fixed-capacity pre-allocation rather than lazy growth.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's \"starts empty, grows on demand\" design.\n  - Makes `NoMsgs` a real, testable condition — back-pressure is exposed instead of hidden behind silent allocation.\n  - Steady-state behavior for ping-pong is unchanged (two buffers cycling).\n- `Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` rather than panic on misuse.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's panic on `size > MAX_SIZE`.\n  - `NoMsgs` is a runtime condition; a benchmark hitting exhaustion should not crash, it should report saturation.\n  - `SizeTooLarge` follows the same shape for one error path.\n- `Box<[u8]>` + separate `len` rather than `Vec<u8>`.\n  - `Vec`'s `cap` field is dead weight when capacity is fixed at `msg_size`; `Box<[u8]>` is `(ptr, len)` only.\n  - `Vec` also implies \"growable\", which is misleading here.\n  - `len` lives on `PooledMsg` instead of in the store, so the store treats buffers as identical fixed-size allocations.\n- `Option<Box<[u8]>>` inside `PooledMsg` so `Drop` can move the inner `Box` out.\n  - Niche-optimized to a single pointer at runtime (no discriminant tag).\n  - `None` only observed transiently inside `Drop::drop`; `Deref` / `DerefMut` can't see it.\n  - `unsafe` alternatives (`ManuallyDrop` + a destructor) would shave the `unwrap`s but add unsafety; not worth it.\n- `BufRefStore::size()` returns the constant store capacity.\n  - Distinct from `len()` (current count), which fluctuates as buffers go in flight.\n  - Cached at `from_buffers` time so `MutexLifo::size()` is O(1) lock-free.\n  - Test `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` validates the invariant.\n  - Future-proofs the multi-size sub-pool extension: `Pool::size()` already sums \"across sub-stores\" (one for now).\n- `msg_size` / `msg_count` typed as `u32`.\n  - Matches the user's requested shape: explicit byte-range and count limits at the type level.\n  - Internally converted to `usize` for `Vec` / slice APIs; widening on every platform actor-x1 targets.\n  - `get_msg(size: usize)` stays `usize` for clean interop with slice/`Vec` lengths; if `size > u32::MAX` the `SizeTooLarge` guard catches it.\n- `is_empty` as a default-impl trait method.\n  - Required by clippy's `len_without_is_empty` lint when a type / trait exposes `len()`.\n  - Default body `len() == 0` is correct for any conforming impl; concrete impls can override if they have a faster path.\n- No zeroing on drop.\n  - Slots `0..new_size` of a reused buffer carry residual bytes from the previous occupant.\n  - For ping-pong workloads the next consumer either overwrites or ignores those bytes — benign.\n  - Real applications crossing trust domains would need an explicit `zeroize`-on-drop step; documented in the module-level comment.\n- No `Default for Pool`.\n  - There's no sensible default for `msg_size` / `msg_count`; every caller has to choose.\n\n## ActorManager + RuntimeZC: split catalog from transport (0.5.0-2)\n\nAdds the multi-threaded zerocopy actor runtime — `ActorZC` /\n`ContextZC` traits, an `ActorManager<S>` catalog, a `RuntimeZC<S>`\ntransport, with `run` and `run_no_probe` entry points. The shipped\nshape deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in three ways, all decided\nduring review:\n\n- Two types instead of one — `ActorManager<S>` (catalog) + `RuntimeZC<S>` (transport). The runtime moves messages; the manager holds instances and assigns ids; they only touch through `take_actors` + `probe_name_prefix` when `run` is called.\n- Direct instance registration rather than factory closures. `ActorZC: Send` is on the trait, so the factory pattern's \"don't require Send\" benefit is moot; `mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 })` reads cleaner than `mgr.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 1 })`.\n- Initial messages live in the app, not the runtime. `run(&mut mgr, initial_messages, …)` takes them as a parameter; neither manager nor runtime stores them. The benchmark / app is what knows \"ping-pong needs one seed message\".\n\n### Tests added (6 new in `runtime_zc::tests`, 28 total in lib)\n\n- `ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down` — two-actor ping-pong drives traffic for 40 ms each phase; both actors record nonzero counts.\n- `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` — after `run` returns, every buffer that was ever in flight has come back; `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.\n- `run_no_probe_returns_counts` — probe-clean path runs the same workload, returns counts only, drains the pool the same way.\n- `take_actors_drains_and_resets` — after `take_actors`, the manager is empty and follow-up `add` restarts ids from 0.\n- `runtime_pool_accessor_returns_same_handle` — `RuntimeZC::pool()` exposes the caller's handle (verifies `msg_size` / `size`).\n- `manager_exposes_probe_name_prefix` — verifies the prefix accessor; full `\"<prefix>.actor<id>\"` composition is verified end-to-end via `goalzc`'s output later (TProbe's name is private).\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- Generic `ActorZC<S>` / `ContextZC<S>` over the store backend.\n  - Pool is generic over `S` since `0.5.0-1`; static dispatch all the way down preserves benchmark fidelity.\n  - Type-erasure in `ContextZC::send` would add a vtable hop per send, contaminating the measurement.\n  - Default `S = MutexLifo` keeps ergonomic call sites unannotated.\n- Handler takes `msg: &[u8]`; runtime owns the inbound `PooledMsg<S>`.\n  - The runtime's `actor_loop` calls `actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg)` then `drop(msg)` — buffer returns to the pool one channel hop later, predictably and once per dispatch.\n- Two run methods rather than one with a `bool probe_enabled` parameter.\n  - A runtime branch on every dispatch would itself be a cost goalzc might want to measure or eliminate.\n  - Function-pointer dispatch (`ActorLoopFn`) monomorphizes cleanly per `R`.\n- Caller-supplied `Pool<S>`.\n  - Lets the app build initial messages from the same pool the runtime uses.\n  - Lets tests inspect `free_len` after shutdown to verify drain.\n  - Lets `goalzc-crit` keep the pool across `iter_custom` calls (long-lived) while the runtime is rebuilt each call.\n- `Send` bound on `ActorZC` (not `ContextZC`).\n  - Actors get moved into spawned threads; must be `Send`.\n  - `ContextZC` lives only inside one thread (constructed per-recv inside `actor_loop`); no `Send` needed.\n- `let _ = sender.send(...)` in `MultiCtxZC::send` — closed channel drops the message silently.\n  - The `Err(SendError(SignalZC::User(msg)))` case drops the `SignalZC`, which drops the inner `PooledMsg`, which returns the buffer to the pool — no leak.\n- Drain assertion as part of the test contract.\n  - `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` codifies \"every PooledMsg that was ever in flight returns to the pool by the time `run` joins\".\n  - Non-obvious because it depends on `Drop for PooledMsg`, mpsc receivers dropping queued messages on close, and main-thread senders being dropped before join.\n  - Codifying it now means a future change that breaks one of those steps gets caught immediately.\n\n### Future direction (noted, not built today)\n\n- `ActorManager` itself becomes an `ActorZC` for control-plane work — a sibling actor on the same Runtime that handles \"register a new actor at runtime\", \"lookup by name\", \"drain probes\", etc.\n- Multiple manager-actors for separate concerns (GUI, CLI, log drain, probe collect) all running on one Runtime.\n- Discovery beyond `u32` ids — name-based, type-indexed.\n- Closure-based init for richer post-spawn / pre-warmup work (`init: FnOnce(&Mailboxes<S>)`) if a workload ever needs more than a static `initial_messages` list.\n- `add_with_factory` escape hatch if a `!Send`-during-construction actor type ever appears.\n"
}

runtime owns the `PooledMsg`.\n  - Runtime receives `Signal::User(PooledMsg)` off the channel.\n  - Hands the handler `&*pooled` for the duration of `handle_message`.\n  - Drops the `PooledMsg` after the handler returns; buffer goes back to the pool.\n  - Pool traffic is one `get` + one `put` per dispatched message — exactly the cost goalzc exists to measure against goal2's unit-`Message` baseline.\n  - Passing `PooledMsg` by value into the handler would let a clever actor reuse the incoming buffer (avoiding pool churn).\n    - Rejected: `&[u8]` matches the shape of real zerocopy receivers and is the cleaner cross-validation target.\n- Fixed-size buffers (`MAX_SIZE`) for the MVP.\n  - Simpler pool (`Vec<Vec<u8>>`).\n  - Matches a ping-pong workload where every message is the same size.\n  - Size-classed sub-pools are future work; mentioned here explicitly so the extension isn't lost.\n- Lazy pool allocation.\n  - Pool starts empty; `get_msg` allocates a fresh `Vec<u8>` if the free list is empty.\n  - Steady state settles to 2 buffers for ping-pong after the first couple of dispatches.\n  - Pre-populating a fixed capacity is a micro-optimization that distorts first-dispatch latency (already dropped by warmup); deferred.\n- `--size N` CLI flag, not a compile-time const.\n  - goalzc is a benchmark; size sensitivity is exactly what a reader wants to sweep.\n  - `N` is validated against `MAX_SIZE` at startup.\n  - If `N > MAX_SIZE`, the binary errors cleanly rather than silently truncating.\n- `RuntimeZC` is a parallel runtime, not a replacement.\n  - `MultiThreadRuntime` stays untouched — existing `goal2` / `goal2-crit` keep working.\n  - `RuntimeZC` lives in its own module; no shared code with `MultiThreadRuntime`.\n  - Duplication is the price for a clean split that's easy to revisit once the shape stabilizes.\n  - The goal2-crit probe-contamination bug stays un-patched in `MultiThreadRuntime` for now; `run_no_probe` exists here from the start instead.\n- Multi-step `0.5.0` ladder.\n  - Five natural checkpoints: pool, runtime, bin, bench, close.\n  - Each reviewable on its own.\n  - A single-step `0.5.0` would be a large diff to review in one pass.\n\n",
  "new_string": ""
}
```

{

  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "## Pool + PooledMsg (0.5.0-1)\n\nImplements the fixed-capacity buffer pool that `RuntimeZC`\nwill use to ship zerocopy messages between actors. The\nshipped shape deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in several\nways — all decided during review of successive design cuts:\n\n- Constructor takes explicit byte / count: `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)`. Pre-allocates `msg_count` buffers of `msg_size` bytes each; no lazy growth, no global `MAX_SIZE` const.\n- `get_msg` returns `Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` rather than panicking. Two variants: `SizeTooLarge { requested, max }` and `NoMsgs`.\n- Buffers are `Box<[u8]>` (fixed-size allocation) plus a separate `len: usize` on `PooledMsg` for the logical message length, instead of `Vec<u8>`'s redundant `cap` field.\n- The free list lives behind a `BufRefStore` trait so different concurrency strategies (Mutex, lock-free queue, ring buffer, hand-rolled atomics, …) can be benchmarked against each other. The default is `MutexLifo`.\n- `Pool` and `PooledMsg` are generic over `S: BufRefStore`, with `MutexLifo` as the default type — ordinary call sites write `Pool::new(64, 128)` unchanged.\n- A `BufRef` type alias documents the `Option<Box<[u8]>>` niche-optimization idiom in one place.\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-0` → `0.5.0-1`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod pool;` added before `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/pool.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub type BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>;` — module-level alias documenting the niche-optimized \"null or non-null pointer\" pattern.\n  - `pub enum PoolError { SizeTooLarge { requested, max }, NoMsgs }` — `Display` + `std::error::Error` impls.\n  - `pub trait BufRefStore: Send + Sync` — `from_buffers`, `get`, `ret`, `len`, `size`, `is_empty` (default-impl over `len`).\n  - `pub struct MutexLifo` — initial impl: `Mutex<Vec<Box<[u8]>>>` + cached `size: usize` for O(1) lock-free `size()`.\n  - `pub struct Pool<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `Arc<PoolInner<S>>` handle; manual `Clone` impl avoids spurious `S: Clone` bound.\n    - `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32)` — allocates buffers, calls `S::from_buffers`.\n    - `Pool::msg_size() -> u32` — bytes per buffer.\n    - `Pool::size() -> usize` — total buffer capacity (delegates to the store; will sum across sub-stores once multi-size pools land).\n    - `Pool::get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` — validates `size`, pops via `S::get`, returns the wrapped buffer.\n    - `Pool::free_len()` — `#[cfg(test)]` only.\n  - `pub struct PooledMsg<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `{ buf: BufRef, len: usize, pool: Arc<PoolInner<S>> }`.\n    - `Deref<Target=[u8]>` / `DerefMut` — slice the underlying `Box<[u8]>` to `[..len]`.\n    - `Drop` — `S::ret(self.buf.take().unwrap())` returns the buffer to the store.\n\n### Tests added (all `#[cfg(test)]` in `pool.rs`, 15 total)\n\n- `new_reports_msg_size_and_size` — fresh pool reports `msg_size`, `size`, and free-list `len`.\n- `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` — `size()` stays at `msg_count` while in-flight buffers reduce `len()`.\n- `get_msg_returns_requested_size` — `len()` of the returned slice matches the requested size.\n- `get_msg_at_msg_size_ok` — `size == msg_size` boundary works.\n- `get_msg_zero_size_ok` — `size == 0` yields an empty slice.\n- `get_msg_over_msg_size_returns_size_too_large` — error carries `requested` + `max` correctly.\n- `get_msg_on_exhaustion_returns_no_msgs` — after all slots are in flight, `get_msg` yields `NoMsgs`.\n- `zero_count_pool_always_no_msgs` — `Pool::new(_, 0)` always errors; sanity check for the zero edge.\n- `drop_restores_slot_and_get_succeeds` — dropping an in-flight msg re-opens a slot.\n- `buffer_is_reused_across_get_drop_get` — pointer equality survives a get / drop / get cycle (LIFO, no realloc).\n- `clone_pool_shares_free_list` — drop on one `Pool` clone is visible from another.\n- `writes_round_trip` — `DerefMut` writes are visible through `Deref`.\n- `pool_works_across_threads` — `Pool` cloned to a worker thread; buffer dropped on worker returns to the shared free list.\n- `pool_error_display` — both variants render readable one-liners.\n- `mutex_lifo_trait_contract` — `BufRefStore` contract direct on `MutexLifo` without a `Pool` wrapper: `from_buffers` / `get` / `ret` / `len` / `size` round-trip end-to-end.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- `BufRefStore` trait + generic `Pool<S>` rather than a hardcoded backend.\n  - Primary motivation: the user wants to swap concurrency strategies and benchmark them — Mutex baseline today, lock-free `ArrayQueue`, ring buffers, hand-rolled atomics later.\n  - Generic-with-default (`S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo`) keeps the common call site unchanged (`Pool::new(64, 128)`) while letting benchmarks opt into other backends with `Pool::<X>::new(64, 128)`.\n  - Trait surface stays minimal: `from_buffers` / `get` / `ret` / `len` / `size`. No \"ordering\" or \"is_lockfree\" methods; ordering is implementation-defined and `Pool` treats buffers as fungible.\n  - Dyn-dispatch via `Box<dyn BufRefStore>` was considered and rejected for benchmark fidelity — a `dyn` indirection in the hot path would contaminate the very thing we're trying to compare.\n- `BufRef = Option<Box<[u8]>>` alias.\n  - Documents the \"safe null/non-null pointer\" idiom in one place rather than re-explaining at each use site.\n  - Used in `PooledMsg.buf` (nullable transiently during `Drop::drop`) and as `BufRefStore::get`'s return (signaling empty store).\n- Fixed-capacity pre-allocation rather than lazy growth.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's \"starts empty, grows on demand\" design.\n  - Makes `NoMsgs` a real, testable condition — back-pressure is exposed instead of hidden behind silent allocation.\n  - Steady-state behavior for ping-pong is unchanged (two buffers cycling).\n- `Result<PooledMsg, PoolError>` rather than panic on misuse.\n  - Supersedes the `0.5.0-0` plan's panic on `size > MAX_SIZE`.\n  - `NoMsgs` is a runtime condition; a benchmark hitting exhaustion should not crash, it should report saturation.\n  - `SizeTooLarge` follows the same shape for one error path.\n- `Box<[u8]>` + separate `len` rather than `Vec<u8>`.\n  - `Vec`'s `cap` field is dead weight when capacity is fixed at `msg_size`; `Box<[u8]>` is `(ptr, len)` only.\n  - `Vec` also implies \"growable\", which is misleading here.\n  - `len` lives on `PooledMsg` instead of in the store, so the store treats buffers as identical fixed-size allocations.\n- `Option<Box<[u8]>>` inside `PooledMsg` so `Drop` can move the inner `Box` out.\n  - Niche-optimized to a single pointer at runtime (no discriminant tag).\n  - `None` only observed transiently inside `Drop::drop`; `Deref` / `DerefMut` can't see it.\n  - `unsafe` alternatives (`ManuallyDrop` + a destructor) would shave the `unwrap`s but add unsafety; not worth it.\n- `BufRefStore::size()` returns the constant store capacity.\n  - Distinct from `len()` (current count), which fluctuates as buffers go in flight.\n  - Cached at `from_buffers` time so `MutexLifo::size()` is O(1) lock-free.\n  - Test `pool_size_is_constant_and_matches_store` validates the invariant.\n  - Future-proofs the multi-size sub-pool extension: `Pool::size()` already sums \"across sub-stores\" (one for now).\n- `msg_size` / `msg_count` typed as `u32`.\n  - Matches the user's requested shape: explicit byte-range and count limits at the type level.\n  - Internally converted to `usize` for `Vec` / slice APIs; widening on every platform actor-x1 targets.\n  - `get_msg(size: usize)` stays `usize` for clean interop with slice/`Vec` lengths; if `size > u32::MAX` the `SizeTooLarge` guard catches it.\n- `is_empty` as a default-impl trait method.\n  - Required by clippy's `len_without_is_empty` lint when a type / trait exposes `len()`.\n  - Default body `len() == 0` is correct for any conforming impl; concrete impls can override if they have a faster path.\n- No zeroing on drop.\n  - Slots `0..new_size` of a reused buffer carry residual bytes from the previous occupant.\n  - For ping-pong workloads the next consumer either overwrites or ignores those bytes — benign.\n  - Real applications crossing trust domains would need an explicit `zeroize`-on-drop step; documented in the module-level comment.\n- No `Default for Pool`.\n  - There's no sensible default for `msg_size` / `msg_count`; every caller has to choose.\n\n",

}

{

  "old_string": "## ActorManager + RuntimeZC: split catalog from transport (0.5.0-2)\n\nAdds the multi-threaded zerocopy actor runtime — split into\ntwo types with disjoint responsibilities — plus the\n`ActorZC` / `ContextZC` traits actors implement and use.\nThe split deviates from the `0.5.0-0` plan in three ways,\nall decided during review of an initial single-`RuntimeZC`\ncut:\n\n- Two types instead of one.\n  - `ActorManager<S>` — actor catalog. Holds instances and the probe-name prefix; assigns ids; nothing else.\n  - `RuntimeZC<S>` — message-moving infrastructure. Owns the pool, threads, channels.\n- Actors are registered as instances, not as factory closures.\n  - The `0.5.0-0` plan called for `add_actor<F: FnOnce() -> A>(factory)`. `MultiThreadRuntime` needs that pattern because its `Actor` trait isn't `Send`; `ActorZC` already requires `Send`, so direct instance registration is strictly more ergonomic and the factory bought nothing here.\n- Initial messages live in the app, not the runtime.\n  - The `0.5.0-0` plan had `RuntimeZC::seed`. Seeds are an app-level concern (this benchmark wants ping-pong; another might want a burst pattern); the Runtime just delivers what the app passes to `run`. Manager doesn't store them either — it has no notion of \"kickoff messages\".\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-1` → `0.5.0-2`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/lib.rs`: `pub mod runtime_zc;` added after `pub mod runtime;`.\n- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`: new module.\n  - `pub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send` — `fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8])`.\n  - `pub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — `get_msg(size) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>`, `send(dst_id, msg: PooledMsg<S>)`. Send to closed channel silently drops the message (its `PooledMsg`'s buffer returns to the pool).\n  - `pub struct ActorManager<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — actor catalog.\n    - `new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self`.\n    - `add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(actor: A) -> u32` — instance registration, no factory.\n    - `take_actors() -> Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>>` — drained by Runtime at run time.\n    - `probe_name_prefix() -> &str`.\n  - `pub struct RuntimeZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>` — transport.\n    - `new(pool: Pool<S>) -> Self`.\n    - `pool() -> &Pool<S>`.\n    - `run(&mut self, mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>, initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>, warmup, measurement, pin_cores) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)>` — tprobe-instrumented.\n    - `run_no_probe(...)` — probe-clean; returns `Vec<u64>`.\n    - `run_inner<R>` — shared orchestration body taking an `ActorLoopFn<S, R>` function pointer; `run` / `run_no_probe` differ only in which fn-ptr they pass. Function pointers are `Copy + Send + Sync`, so the orchestration stays generic without closure / `Fn`-bound complications.\n  - Internal:\n    - `enum SignalZC<S> { User(PooledMsg<S>), ClearProbe, Shutdown }`.\n    - `actor_loop_probe::<S>` / `actor_loop_no_probe::<S>` — per-thread loops.\n    - `MultiCtxZC<'a, S>` — borrows peer senders + pool; impls `ContextZC<S>`.\n\n### Tests added (6 new in `runtime_zc::tests`, 28 total in lib)\n\n- `ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down` — two-actor ping-pong drives traffic for 40 ms each phase; both actors record nonzero counts.\n- `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` — after `run` returns, every buffer that was ever in flight has come back; `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.\n- `run_no_probe_returns_counts` — probe-clean path runs the same workload, returns counts only, drains the pool the same way.\n- `take_actors_drains_and_resets` — after `take_actors`, the manager is empty and follow-up `add` restarts ids from 0.\n- `runtime_pool_accessor_returns_same_handle` — `RuntimeZC::pool()` exposes the caller's handle (verifies `msg_size` / `size`).\n- `manager_exposes_probe_name_prefix` — verifies the prefix accessor; full `\"<prefix>.actor<id>\"` composition is verified end-to-end via `goalzc`'s output later (TProbe's name is private).\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- Two-type split: `ActorManager` (catalog) + `RuntimeZC` (transport).\n  - The runtime's job is \"move messages efficiently\"; nothing else.\n  - The manager's job is \"hold actor instances and assign ids\"; nothing else.\n  - The two only touch each other through `take_actors` + `probe_name_prefix` when `run` is called. A future swap of either side (M:N runtime, name-based discovery) leaves the other untouched.\n- Initial messages as a parameter to `run`, not state on either type.\n  - `run` neither stores them nor knows what they're for — it just delivers them after spawn / before warmup, then forgets.\n  - The benchmark / app is what knows \"ping-pong needs one seed message\"; that knowledge stays in the binary rather than leaking into runtime types.\n- Direct instance registration rather than factory closures.\n  - `ActorZC: Send` is already on the trait, so the factory pattern's \"don't require Send on the trait\" benefit is moot.\n  - `mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 })` reads cleaner than `mgr.add_actor(|| PingPong { peer: 1 })`.\n  - Future escape hatch (`add_with_factory(closure)`) is easy to add if a `!Send`-during-construction actor type ever appears.\n- Generic `ActorZC<S>` / `ContextZC<S>` over the store backend.\n  - Pool is generic over `S` since `0.5.0-1`; static dispatch all the way down preserves benchmark fidelity.\n  - Type-erasure in `ContextZC::send` would add a vtable hop per send, contaminating the measurement.\n  - Default `S = MutexLifo` keeps ergonomic call sites unannotated.\n- Handler takes `msg: &[u8]`; runtime owns the inbound `PooledMsg<S>`.\n  - The runtime's `actor_loop` calls `actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg)` then `drop(msg)` — buffer returns to the pool one channel hop later, predictably and once per dispatch.\n- Two run methods rather than one with a `bool probe_enabled` parameter.\n  - A runtime branch on every dispatch would itself be a cost goalzc might want to measure or eliminate.\n  - Function-pointer dispatch (`ActorLoopFn`) monomorphizes cleanly per `R`.\n- Caller-supplied `Pool<S>`.\n  - Lets the app build initial messages from the same pool the runtime uses.\n  - Lets tests inspect `free_len` after shutdown to verify drain.\n  - Lets `goalzc-crit` keep the pool across `iter_custom` calls (long-lived) while the runtime is rebuilt each call.\n- `Send` bound on `ActorZC` (not `ContextZC`).\n  - Actors get moved into spawned threads; must be `Send`.\n  - `ContextZC` lives only inside one thread (constructed per-recv inside `actor_loop`); no `Send` needed.\n- `let _ = sender.send(...)` in `MultiCtxZC::send` — closed channel drops the message silently.\n  - The `Err(SendError(SignalZC::User(msg)))` case drops the `SignalZC`, which drops the inner `PooledMsg`, which returns the buffer to the pool — no leak.\n- Drain assertion as part of the test contract.\n  - `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` codifies \"every PooledMsg that was ever in flight returns to the pool by the time `run` joins\".\n  - Non-obvious because it depends on `Drop for PooledMsg`, mpsc receivers dropping queued messages on close, and main-thread senders being dropped before join.\n  - Codifying it now means a future change that breaks one of those steps gets caught immediately.\n\n### Future direction (noted, not built today)\n\n- `ActorManager` itself becomes an `ActorZC` for control-plane work — a sibling actor on the same Runtime that handles \"register a new actor at runtime\", \"lookup by name\", \"drain probes\", etc.\n- Multiple manager-actors for separate concerns (GUI, CLI, log drain, probe collect) all running on one Runtime.\n- Discovery beyond `u32` ids — name-based, type-indexed.\n- Closure-based init for richer post-spawn / pre-warmup work (`init: FnOnce(&Mailboxes<S>)`) if a workload ever needs more than a static `initial_messages` list.\n- `add_with_factory` escape hatch if a `!Send`-during-construction actor type ever appears.\n\n",
  "new_string": ""
}

> AGENT

**Naming**: underscore. Look at `runtime_zc.rs` / `band_table.rs` — Rust modules map `mod foo_bar` → `foo_bar.rs` natively, while `actor-manager.rs` would force a `#[path = "..."]` attribute just to avoid annoying Cargo. Hyphens in this repo are reserved for crate names (`actor-x1`, `tprobe`) and bench / bin *target names* declared in Cargo.toml (`goal1-crit.rs` is a bench target name, not a `mod`). So: `actor_manager.rs`.

**Splitting**: I lean yes, with a caveat about how to structure it. Reasoning:

- The headline of 0.5.0-2 was "split catalog from transport". The two types have disjoint responsibilities, no shared internal state, and connect only at `run`-time through `take_actors` + `probe_name_prefix`. File-level separation just finishes the split that types-level already made.
- You've already telegraphed that `ActorManager` will grow — "method-based API but also an Actor itself, likely multiple actors". One file gives that growth somewhere to land without the runtime's transport details polluting diffs.
- We're already touching this code in 0.5.0-2; doing the file split now is cheap and lands as part of the same commit. Doing it later is a separate refactor commit for marginal gain.

The caveat is **where the traits live**. `ActorZC` and `ContextZC` are consumed by both `ActorManager` (which holds `Box<dyn ActorZC<S>>`) and `RuntimeZC` (whose internal `MultiCtxZC` impls `ContextZC`). Three plausible layouts:

- **A** — traits stay in `runtime_zc.rs`; `actor_manager.rs` imports them. Cleanest mechanically but inverts the conceptual hierarchy: the manager owns the actor world, the runtime is "just a transport", but the traits would live in the transport file.
- **B** — move `ActorZC` + `ContextZC` to `actor_manager.rs` along with `ActorManager`; `runtime_zc.rs` imports them. Matches the conceptual hierarchy (actor model in one file; transport in another). My pick.
- **C** — traits in their own `actor_zc.rs`, manager in `actor_manager.rs`, runtime in `runtime_zc.rs`. Three files. Probably overkill for the volume we have today.

Tradeoff to flag: option B leaves `runtime_zc.rs` named after a trait (`ActorZC`) it no longer defines. Mild oddness; could rename later (`runtime_zc.rs` → `runtime_mt.rs` or similar) if you ever build a second transport.

Recommendation: **option B, file name `actor_manager.rs`, do it as part of 0.5.0-2** so the file-level split rides the same commit as the type-level split. If you'd rather defer (keep this commit code-stable, do the file move in 0.5.0-2.5 or fold into 0.5.0-3 prep), that's also defensible — just costlier in commit count.

> DEVELOPER

I'll go with (B) for now we can do more later runtime is a user and ActorManager is it's controller, so runtime exports what AM needs to talk with it but AM is "God" :)

```
1	//! Multi-threaded zerocopy runtime + actor catalog.
2	//!
3	//! Two types with disjoint responsibilities:
4	//!
5	//! - [`ActorManager`] — actor catalog. Holds actor instances
6	//!   and the probe-name prefix; assigns ids; nothing else.
7	//!   No knowledge of threads, channels, the pool, or what's
8	//!   being measured.
9	//! - [`RuntimeZC`] — message-moving infrastructure. Owns
10	//!   the [`Pool<S>`]; spawns actor threads; wires per-actor
11	//!   `mpsc` channels; runs the warmup / measure / shutdown
12	//!   lifecycle. Knows nothing about "seeds", named lookups,
13	//!   or what a benchmark is.
14	//!
15	//! The two interact through a small surface — Runtime drains
16	//! the Manager (via [`ActorManager::take_actors`] +
17	//! [`ActorManager::probe_name_prefix`]) when `run` is called.
18	//! Initial messages are passed to [`RuntimeZC::run`] directly
19	//! by the app; the Runtime delivers them after spawn / before
20	//! warmup, then forgets them.
21	//!
22	//! Two run entry points on [`RuntimeZC`]:
23	//!
24	//! - [`RuntimeZC::run`] — `tprobe`-instrumented; returns
25	//!   `(count, TProbe)` per actor. Used by the `goalzc` binary.
26	//! - [`RuntimeZC::run_no_probe`] — probe-free; returns `count`
27	//!   per actor. Used by the `goalzc-crit` criterion bench so
28	//!   measurement is not contaminated by probe overhead.
29	//!
30	//! Lifecycle inside `run` / `run_no_probe`:
31	//!
32	//! - Drain actors from the Manager.
33	//! - Build per-actor channels.
34	//! - Spawn one thread per actor, optionally pinned.
35	//! - Deliver `initial_messages` over the senders.
36	//! - Sleep `warmup`.
37	//! - Send `ClearProbe` to every actor.
38	//! - Sleep `measurement`.
39	//! - Send `Shutdown` to every actor.
40	//! - Drop main-thread senders so the channels close.
41	//! - Join, return per-actor results.
42	//!
43	//! Drain invariant: by the time the join completes, every
44	//! [`PooledMsg`] that was ever in flight has been dropped, so
45	//! `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.
46	
47	use std::sync::mpsc::{self, Receiver, Sender};
48	use std::thread::{self, JoinHandle};
49	use std::time::Duration;
50	
51	use crate::pool::{BufRefStore, MutexLifo, Pool, PoolError, PooledMsg};
52	use tprobe::TProbe;
53	
54	/// Actor behavior in the zerocopy runtime.
55	///
56	/// - Single method: handle one inbound message at a time;
57	///   never block.
58	/// - `msg: &[u8]` is the runtime-owned [`PooledMsg`]'s slice
59	///   view. The buffer returns to the pool after the handler
60	///   returns.
61	/// - Replies are fabricated via [`ContextZC::get_msg`] and
62	///   forwarded with [`ContextZC::send`].
63	/// - The trait requires `Send` so actors can ship into
64	///   spawned threads.
65	pub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send {
66	    /// Handle one inbound message. Use `ctx` to fabricate /
67	    /// dispatch any outbound messages.
68	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]);
69	}
70	
71	/// Send-side handle the runtime supplies to each
72	/// [`ActorZC::handle_message`] call.
73	///
74	/// - Carries access to the shared [`Pool<S>`] via
75	///   [`ContextZC::get_msg`].
76	/// - Dispatches outbound messages to other actors via
77	///   [`ContextZC::send`].
78	/// - Drops the `PooledMsg` if the destination channel is
79	///   closed (peer already shut down) so the buffer never
80	///   leaks.
81	pub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {
82	    /// Acquire a buffer of length `size` from the pool.
83	    /// Forwards [`PoolError`] on failure (`SizeTooLarge` /
84	    /// `NoMsgs`); the actor handler decides how to react
85	    /// (typically `expect` for ping-pong workloads).
86	    fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>;
87	
88	    /// Send `msg` to the actor identified by `dst_id`. A
89	    /// closed channel silently drops the message; the dropped
90	    /// `PooledMsg`'s buffer returns to the pool.
91	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>);
92	}
93	
94	/// Internal channel payload.
95	///
96	/// - `User(msg)` — caller-visible traffic.
97	/// - `ClearProbe` — runtime injects at warmup → measurement
98	///   boundary so the report only sees steady-state data.
99	/// - `Shutdown` — runtime injects after the measurement
100	///   window; actor exits its `recv` loop on receipt.
101	enum SignalZC<S: BufRefStore> {
102	    User(PooledMsg<S>),
103	    ClearProbe,
104	    Shutdown,
105	}
106	
107	/// Actor catalog — registration and lifecycle, nothing else.
108	///
109	/// - Holds actor instances and the probe-name prefix.
110	/// - Hands out `u32` ids on `add`; that's the discovery
111	///   primitive other actors use as `dst_id` in `Context::send`.
112	/// - Drained by a [`RuntimeZC`] at run time via
113	///   [`ActorManager::take_actors`]; single-shot for now.
114	/// - Knows nothing about threads, channels, the pool, or
115	///   what's being measured.
116	pub struct ActorManager<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {
117	    actors: Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>>,
118	    probe_name_prefix: String,
119	}
120	
121	impl<S: BufRefStore + 'static> ActorManager<S> {
122	    /// Build an empty manager.
123	    ///
124	    /// `probe_name_prefix` is combined with each actor's id by
125	    /// the runtime to form per-thread probe names (e.g.
126	    /// `"goalzc.dispatch.actor0"`).
127	    pub fn new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self {
128	        Self {
129	            actors: Vec::new(),
130	            probe_name_prefix: probe_name_prefix.to_string(),
131	        }
132	    }
133	
134	    /// Register an actor instance.
135	    ///
136	    /// - Returns the assigned id (0-indexed; usable as
137	    ///   `dst_id` in [`ContextZC::send`]).
138	    /// - The actor must be `Send + 'static`. The trait already
139	    ///   requires `Send`; `'static` is the usual no-borrow
140	    ///   bound for moving across threads.
141	    pub fn add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(&mut self, actor: A) -> u32 {
142	        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;
143	        self.actors.push(Box::new(actor));
144	        id
145	    }
146	
147	    /// Drain the registered actors, leaving the manager empty.
148	    /// Called by [`RuntimeZC::run`] at the start of a run.
149	    pub fn take_actors(&mut self) -> Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>> {
150	        std::mem::take(&mut self.actors)
151	    }
152	
153	    /// The probe-name prefix the runtime combines with each
154	    /// actor's id to name its `TProbe`.
155	    pub fn probe_name_prefix(&self) -> &str {
156	        &self.probe_name_prefix
157	    }
158	}
159	
160	/// Multi-threaded zerocopy runtime — message-moving
161	/// infrastructure.
162	///
163	/// - Owns the [`Pool<S>`] (cloned cheaply to each actor
164	///   thread).
165	/// - Spawns threads, wires channels, runs the warmup /
166	///   measure / shutdown lifecycle.
167	/// - Knows nothing about "seeds", named lookups, or what's
168	///   being measured. Initial messages come in as a parameter
169	///   to `run` and are delivered after spawn / before warmup.
170	/// - Generic over the store backend `S` so concurrency
171	///   strategies can be swapped and benchmarked. Default
172	///   [`MutexLifo`].
173	pub struct RuntimeZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {
174	    pool: Pool<S>,
175	}
176	
177	impl<S: BufRefStore + 'static> RuntimeZC<S> {
178	    /// Build a runtime that draws buffers from `pool`.
179	    ///
180	    /// - The runtime stores a cheap `Arc` clone; the caller
181	    ///   keeps the original handle so it can build initial
182	    ///   messages or inspect (`free_len`, `size`).
183	    pub fn new(pool: Pool<S>) -> Self {
184	        Self { pool }
185	    }
186	
187	    /// Borrow the runtime's pool handle.
188	    pub fn pool(&self) -> &Pool<S> {
189	        &self.pool
190	    }
191	
192	    /// Drain the manager's actors, run with `tprobe`
193	    /// instrumentation.
194	    ///
195	    /// - Each actor thread owns a [`TProbe`] recording per-
196	    ///   `handle_message` latency at `inner=1`.
197	    /// - `initial_messages` are delivered after thread spawn,
198	    ///   before warmup.
199	    /// - Returns `(count, probe)` per actor in id order.
200	    pub fn run(
201	        &mut self,
202	        mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>,
203	        initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>,
204	        warmup: Duration,
205	        measurement: Duration,
206	        pin_cores: &[usize],
207	    ) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)> {
208	        self.run_inner(
209	            mgr,
210	            initial_messages,
211	            warmup,
212	            measurement,
213	            pin_cores,
214	            actor_loop_probe::<S>,
215	        )
216	    }
217	
218	    /// Same orchestration as [`RuntimeZC::run`], minus the
219	    /// per-message `TProbe` calls in the hot loop.
220	    ///
221	    /// - Used by the `goalzc-crit` criterion bench so the
222	    ///   measurement is probe-clean.
223	    /// - The lesson learned from `goal2-crit`'s probe
224	    ///   contamination: keeping a probe-free path on the
225	    ///   runtime from the start avoids the trap of a bench
226	    ///   that secretly measures `work + tprobe`.
227	    pub fn run_no_probe(
228	        &mut self,
229	        mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>,
230	        initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>,
231	        warmup: Duration,
232	        measurement: Duration,
233	        pin_cores: &[usize],
234	    ) -> Vec<u64> {
235	        self.run_inner(
236	            mgr,
237	            initial_messages,
238	            warmup,
239	            measurement,
240	            pin_cores,
241	            actor_loop_no_probe::<S>,
242	        )
243	    }
244	
245	    /// Shared orchestration body.
246	    ///
247	    /// - Drains the manager, builds channels, spawns one
248	    ///   thread per actor, delivers initial messages, runs
249	    ///   the warmup / measure / shutdown lifecycle, joins,
250	    ///   and returns per-actor results.
251	    /// - Per-thread work is delegated to `actor_loop`, a
252	    ///   function pointer (`ActorLoopFn<S, R>`) so this method
253	    ///   monomorphizes per `R` without the closure / `Fn`-bound
254	    ///   overhead.
255	    fn run_inner<R: Send + 'static>(
256	        &mut self,
257	        mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>,
258	        initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>,
259	        warmup: Duration,
260	        measurement: Duration,
261	        pin_cores: &[usize],
262	        actor_loop: ActorLoopFn<S, R>,
263	    ) -> Vec<R> {
264	        let actors = mgr.take_actors();
265	        let n = actors.len();
266	        let pool = self.pool.clone();
267	        let probe_name_prefix = mgr.probe_name_prefix().to_string();
268	
269	        let mut senders: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
270	        let mut receivers: Vec<Option<Receiver<SignalZC<S>>>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
271	        for _ in 0..n {
272	            let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
273	            senders.push(tx);
274	            receivers.push(Some(rx));
275	        }
276	
277	        let mut handles: Vec<JoinHandle<R>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
278	        for (id, actor) in actors.into_iter().enumerate() {
279	            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
280	            // OK: receivers[id] was set to `Some(...)` in the loop above.
281	            let rx = receivers[id].take().unwrap();
282	            let peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>> = senders.to_vec();
283	            let probe_name = format!("{}.actor{}", probe_name_prefix, id);
284	            let pin_target = if pin_cores.is_empty() {
285	                None
286	            } else {
287	                Some(pin_cores[id % pin_cores.len()])
288	            };
289	            let actor_pool = pool.clone();
290	            let h = thread::Builder::new()
291	                .name(format!("actor-zc-{id}"))
292	                .spawn(move || {
293	                    tprobe::pin::pin_current(pin_target);
294	                    actor_loop(actor, rx, peers, actor_pool, probe_name)
295	                })
296	                .expect("failed to spawn actor thread");
297	            handles.push(h);
298	        }
299	
300	        for (dst, msg) in initial_messages {
301	            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
302	            // OK: receivers are alive (threads just spawned); a send
303	            //   failure indicates an immediate thread panic at startup
304	            //   which the join below surfaces with a clearer message.
305	            senders[dst as usize].send(SignalZC::User(msg)).unwrap();
306	        }
307	
308	        thread::sleep(warmup);
309	        for tx in &senders {
310	            let _ = tx.send(SignalZC::ClearProbe);
311	        }
312	
313	        thread::sleep(measurement);
314	        for tx in &senders {
315	            let _ = tx.send(SignalZC::Shutdown);
316	        }
317	        drop(senders);
318	
319	        let mut results = Vec::with_capacity(n);
320	        for h in handles {
321	            results.push(h.join().expect("actor thread panicked"));
322	        }
323	        results
324	    }
325	}
326	
327	/// Function-pointer type for the per-thread actor loop, used
328	/// by [`RuntimeZC::run_inner`]. `fn(...) -> R` is `Copy +
329	/// Send + Sync`, which keeps the orchestration scaffold
330	/// generic without closure / `Fn`-bound complications.
331	type ActorLoopFn<S, R> = fn(
332	    Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,
333	    Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,
334	    Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,
335	    Pool<S>,
336	    String,
337	) -> R;
338	
339	/// `ContextZC` impl used inside each actor thread.
340	///
341	/// - Holds borrowed references to the actor's peer senders
342	///   and the shared pool.
343	/// - `get_msg` delegates to [`Pool::get_msg`].
344	/// - `send` ships a `PooledMsg` over the appropriate channel;
345	///   on closed channel the message drops back to the pool.
346	struct MultiCtxZC<'a, S: BufRefStore> {
347	    senders: &'a [Sender<SignalZC<S>>],
348	    pool: &'a Pool<S>,
349	}
350	
351	impl<S: BufRefStore> ContextZC<S> for MultiCtxZC<'_, S> {
352	    fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError> {
353	        self.pool.get_msg(size)
354	    }
355	
356	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>) {
357	        // Ignored Err contains the un-sent SignalZC::User(msg);
358	        // dropping it returns the buffer to the pool.
359	        let _ = self.senders[dst_id as usize].send(SignalZC::User(msg));
360	    }
361	}
362	
363	/// Per-thread loop with `tprobe` instrumentation.
364	///
365	/// - One scope per `handle_message` (`inner=1`).
366	/// - `Signal::User` records.
367	/// - `Signal::ClearProbe` resets probe + count.
368	/// - `Signal::Shutdown` (or closed channel) exits.
369	fn actor_loop_probe<S: BufRefStore + 'static>(
370	    mut actor: Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,
371	    rx: Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,
372	    peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,
373	    pool: Pool<S>,
374	    probe_name: String,
375	) -> (u64, TProbe) {
376	    let mut probe = TProbe::new(&probe_name);
377	    let mut count: u64 = 0;
378	    loop {
379	        match rx.recv() {
380	            Ok(SignalZC::User(msg)) => {
381	                let id = probe.start(0);
382	                let mut ctx = MultiCtxZC {
383	                    senders: &peers,
384	                    pool: &pool,
385	                };
386	                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg);
387	                probe.end(id);
388	                drop(msg);
389	                count += 1;
390	            }
391	            Ok(SignalZC::ClearProbe) => {
392	                probe.clear();
393	                count = 0;
394	            }
395	            Ok(SignalZC::Shutdown) | Err(_) => break,
396	        }
397	    }
398	    (count, probe)
399	}
400	
401	/// Per-thread loop with no probe involvement.
402	///
403	/// - Identical to [`actor_loop_probe`] minus `probe.start` /
404	///   `probe.end` / `probe.clear`.
405	/// - `_probe_name` is unused but kept in the signature so the
406	///   function pointer matches [`ActorLoopFn`] without
407	///   special-casing.
408	fn actor_loop_no_probe<S: BufRefStore + 'static>(
409	    mut actor: Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,
410	    rx: Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,
411	    peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,
412	    pool: Pool<S>,
413	    _probe_name: String,
414	) -> u64 {
415	    let mut count: u64 = 0;
416	    loop {
417	        match rx.recv() {
418	            Ok(SignalZC::User(msg)) => {
419	                let mut ctx = MultiCtxZC {
420	                    senders: &peers,
421	                    pool: &pool,
422	                };
423	                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg);
424	                drop(msg);
425	                count += 1;
426	            }
427	            Ok(SignalZC::ClearProbe) => {
428	                count = 0;
429	            }
430	            Ok(SignalZC::Shutdown) | Err(_) => break,
431	        }
432	    }
433	    count
434	}
435	
436	#[cfg(test)]
437	mod tests {
438	    use super::*;
439	    use crate::pool::Pool;
440	
441	    /// Generic ping-pong actor reusable across `S` choices.
442	    /// On each message: get a same-sized reply buffer, send
443	    /// to the configured peer, drop the inbound `PooledMsg`
444	    /// (returns its buffer to the pool).
445	    struct PingPong {
446	        peer: u32,
447	    }
448	
449	    impl<S: BufRefStore> ActorZC<S> for PingPong {
450	        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]) {
451	            let reply = ctx.get_msg(msg.len()).expect("get_msg should succeed");
452	            ctx.send(self.peer, reply);
453	        }
454	    }
455	
456	    /// Two-actor ping-pong drives traffic and shuts down
457	    /// cleanly; both actors record nonzero counts.
458	    #[test]
459	    fn ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down() {
460	        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);
461	        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
462	        let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("test.zc");
463	        let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });
464	        let _b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });
465	        let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(8).expect("seed"))];
466	        let results = rt.run(
467	            &mut mgr,
468	            initial,
469	            Duration::from_millis(40),
470	            Duration::from_millis(40),
471	            &[],
472	        );
473	        assert_eq!(results.len(), 2);
474	        let total: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();
475	        assert!(total > 0, "expected nonzero throughput, got count={total}");
476	    }
477	
478	    /// After `run` returns, every buffer the pool ever
479	    /// handed out has come back. Tests the drain story
480	    /// across the channel-close path.

483	        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);

486	        let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });
487	        let _b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });
488	        let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(8).expect("seed"))];
489	        let _ = rt.run(
490	            &mut mgr,
491	            initial,
492	            Duration::from_millis(20),
493	            Duration::from_millis(20),
494	            &[],
495	        );
496	        assert_eq!(
497	            pool.free_len(),
498	            pool.size(),
499	            "all buffers should return to pool after shutdown"
500	        );
501	    }
502	
503	    /// `run_no_probe` orchestrates identically and returns
504	    /// per-actor counts (no probes). Validates that the
505	    /// probe-clean path runs the same workload.

507	    fn run_no_probe_returns_counts() {

514	        let results = rt.run_no_probe(

  "content": "//! Actor model surface — traits actors implement, plus the\n//! catalog that registers and addresses them.\n//!\n//! This module defines the **what** of the zerocopy actor model:\n//!\n//! - [`ActorZC`] — the actor trait. One method, one message at\n//!   a time.\n//! - [`ContextZC`] — the send-side handle the runtime supplies.\n//! - [`ActorManager`] — actor catalog. Holds instances and the\n//!   probe-name prefix; assigns ids; nothing else.\n//!\n//! It deliberately knows nothing about the **how** — threads,\n//! channels, the pool's lifecycle, or what's being measured —\n//! that lives in [`crate::runtime_zc`]. The runtime is one\n//! consumer of this surface; future control-plane / discovery\n//! actors will be others.\n\nuse crate::pool::{BufRefStore, MutexLifo, PoolError, PooledMsg};\n\n/// Actor behavior in the zerocopy runtime.\n///\n/// - Single method: handle one inbound message at a time;\n///   never block.\n/// - `msg: &[u8]` is the runtime-owned [`PooledMsg`]'s slice\n///   view. The buffer returns to the pool after the handler\n///   returns.\n/// - Replies are fabricated via [`ContextZC::get_msg`] and\n///   forwarded with [`ContextZC::send`].\n/// - The trait requires `Send` so actors can ship into\n///   spawned threads.\npub trait ActorZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo>: Send {\n    /// Handle one inbound message. Use `ctx` to fabricate /\n    /// dispatch any outbound messages.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]);\n}\n\n/// Send-side handle the runtime supplies to each\n/// [`ActorZC::handle_message`] call.\n///\n/// - Carries access to the shared pool via\n///   [`ContextZC::get_msg`].\n/// - Dispatches outbound messages to other actors via\n///   [`ContextZC::send`].\n/// - Drops the `PooledMsg` if the destination channel is\n///   closed (peer already shut down) so the buffer never\n///   leaks.\npub trait ContextZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {\n    /// Acquire a buffer of length `size` from the pool.\n    /// Forwards [`PoolError`] on failure (`SizeTooLarge` /\n    /// `NoMsgs`); the actor handler decides how to react\n    /// (typically `expect` for ping-pong workloads).\n    fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>;\n\n    /// Send `msg` to the actor identified by `dst_id`. A\n    /// closed channel silently drops the message; the dropped\n    /// `PooledMsg`'s buffer returns to the pool.\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>);\n}\n\n/// Actor catalog — registration and lifecycle, nothing else.\n///\n/// - Holds actor instances and the probe-name prefix.\n/// - Hands out `u32` ids on `add`; that's the discovery\n///   primitive other actors use as `dst_id` in\n///   [`ContextZC::send`].\n/// - Drained by a runtime at run time via\n///   [`ActorManager::take_actors`]; single-shot for now.\n/// - Knows nothing about threads, channels, the pool, or\n///   what's being measured.\npub struct ActorManager<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {\n    actors: Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>>,\n    probe_name_prefix: String,\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore + 'static> ActorManager<S> {\n    /// Build an empty manager.\n    ///\n    /// `probe_name_prefix` is combined with each actor's id by\n    /// the runtime to form per-thread probe names (e.g.\n    /// `\"goalzc.dispatch.actor0\"`).\n    pub fn new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            actors: Vec::new(),\n            probe_name_prefix: probe_name_prefix.to_string(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register an actor instance.\n    ///\n    /// - Returns the assigned id (0-indexed; usable as\n    ///   `dst_id` in [`ContextZC::send`]).\n    /// - The actor must be `Send + 'static`. The trait already\n    ///   requires `Send`; `'static` is the usual no-borrow\n    ///   bound for moving across threads.\n    pub fn add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(&mut self, actor: A) -> u32 {\n        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;\n        self.actors.push(Box::new(actor));\n        id\n    }\n\n    /// Drain the registered actors, leaving the manager empty.\n    /// Called by the runtime at the start of a run.\n    pub fn take_actors(&mut self) -> Vec<Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>> {\n        std::mem::take(&mut self.actors)\n    }\n\n    /// The probe-name prefix the runtime combines with each\n    /// actor's id to name its `TProbe`.\n    pub fn probe_name_prefix(&self) -> &str {\n        &self.probe_name_prefix\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Trivial actor stub — never invoked by these tests; only\n    /// needs to satisfy `ActorZC<S> + Send + 'static` for\n    /// `ActorManager::add`.\n    struct Noop;\n\n    impl<S: BufRefStore> ActorZC<S> for Noop {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, _ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, _msg: &[u8]) {}\n    }\n\n    /// `take_actors` drains the manager and resets it to\n    /// empty so a follow-up registration starts from id 0.\n    #[test]\n    fn take_actors_drains_and_resets() {\n        let mut mgr: ActorManager = ActorManager::new(\"test.drain-mgr\");\n        assert_eq!(mgr.add(Noop), 0);\n        assert_eq!(mgr.add(Noop), 1);\n        let drained = mgr.take_actors();\n        assert_eq!(drained.len(), 2);\n        // Manager is now empty; new registrations restart from 0.\n        assert_eq!(mgr.add(Noop), 0);\n    }\n\n    /// `ActorManager::probe_name_prefix` exposes the\n    /// caller-supplied prefix verbatim; the runtime composes\n    /// `\"<prefix>.actor<id>\"` for each thread's probe name\n    /// (verified end-to-end via `goalzc` output rather than\n    /// here, since `TProbe::name` is not on the public\n    /// surface).\n    #[test]\n    fn manager_exposes_probe_name_prefix() {\n        let mgr: ActorManager = ActorManager::new(\"custom.prefix\");\n        assert_eq!(mgr.probe_name_prefix(), \"custom.prefix\");\n    }\n}\n"
}
